46: 
47: class Threshold(Module):
48:     r"""Thresholds each element of the input Tensor.
49: 
50:     Threshold is defined as:
51: 
52:     .. math::
53:         y =
54:         \begin{cases}
55:         x, &\text{ if } x > \text{threshold} \\
56:         \text{value}, &\text{ otherwise }
57:         \end{cases}
58: 
59:     Args:
60:         threshold: The value to threshold at
61:         value: The value to replace with
62:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
63: 
64:     Shape:
65:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
66:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
67: 
68:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Threshold.png
69: 
70:     Examples::
71: 
72:         >>> m = nn.Threshold(0, 0.5)
73:         >>> input = torch.arange(-3, 3)
74:         >>> output = m(input)
75:     """
76: 
77:     __constants__ = ["threshold", "value", "inplace"]
78: 
79:     threshold: float
80:     value: float
81:     inplace: bool
82: 
83:     def __init__(self, threshold: float, value: float, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
84:         super().__init__()
85:         self.threshold = threshold
86:         self.value = value
87:         self.inplace = inplace
88:         # TODO: check in THNN (if inplace == True, then assert value <= threshold)
89: 
90:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
91:         """
92:         Runs the forward pass.
93:         """
94:         return F.threshold(input, self.threshold, self.value, self.inplace)
95: 
96:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
97:         """
98:         Return the extra representation of the module.
99:         """
100:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
101:         return f"threshold={self.threshold}, value={self.value}{inplace_str}"
102: 
103: 
104: class ReLU(Module):
105:     r"""Applies the rectified linear unit function element-wise.
106: 
107:     :math:`\text{ReLU}(x) = (x)^+ = \max(0, x)`
108: 
109:     Args:
110:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
111: 
112:     Shape:
113:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
114:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
115: 
116:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/ReLU.png
117: 
118:     Examples::
119: 
120:         >>> m = nn.ReLU()
121:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
122:         >>> output = m(input)
123: 
124: 
125:       An implementation of CReLU - https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.05201
126: 
127:         >>> m = nn.ReLU()
128:         >>> input = torch.randn(2).unsqueeze(0)
129:         >>> output = torch.cat((m(input), m(-input)))
130:     """
131: 
132:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
133:     inplace: bool
134: 
135:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
136:         super().__init__()
137:         self.inplace = inplace
138: 
139:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
140:         """
141:         Runs the forward pass.
142:         """
143:         return F.relu(input, inplace=self.inplace)
144: 
145:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
146:         """
147:         Return the extra representation of the module.
148:         """
149:         inplace_str = "inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
150:         return inplace_str

152: 
153: class RReLU(Module):
154:     r"""Applies the randomized leaky rectified linear unit function, element-wise.
155: 
156:     Method described in the paper:
157:     `Empirical Evaluation of Rectified Activations in Convolutional Network <https://arxiv.org/abs/1505.00853>`_.
158: 
159:     The function is defined as:
160: 
161:     .. math::
162:         \text{RReLU}(x) =
163:         \begin{cases}
164:             x & \text{if } x \geq 0 \\
165:             ax & \text{ otherwise }
166:         \end{cases}
167: 
168:     where :math:`a` is randomly sampled from uniform distribution
169:     :math:`\mathcal{U}(\text{lower}, \text{upper})` during training while during
170:     evaluation :math:`a` is fixed with :math:`a = \frac{\text{lower} + \text{upper}}{2}`.
171: 
172:     Args:
173:         lower: lower bound of the uniform distribution. Default: :math:`\frac{1}{8}`
174:         upper: upper bound of the uniform distribution. Default: :math:`\frac{1}{3}`
175:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
176: 
177:     Shape:
178:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
179:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
180: 
181:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/RReLU.png
182: 
183:     Examples::
184: 
185:         >>> m = nn.RReLU(0.1, 0.3)
186:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
187:         >>> output = m(input)
188: 
189:     """
190: 
191:     __constants__ = ["lower", "upper", "inplace"]
192: 
193:     lower: float
194:     upper: float
195:     inplace: bool
196: 
197:     def __init__(
198:         self, lower: float = 1.0 / 8, upper: float = 1.0 / 3, inplace: bool = False
199:     ) -> None:
200:         super().__init__()
201:         self.lower = lower
202:         self.upper = upper
203:         self.inplace = inplace
204: 
205:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
206:         """
207:         Runs the forward pass.
208:         """
209:         return F.rrelu(input, self.lower, self.upper, self.training, self.inplace)
210: 
211:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
212:         """
213:         Return the extra representation of the module.
214:         """
215:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
216:         return f"lower={self.lower}, upper={self.upper}{inplace_str}"
217: 
218: 
219: class Hardtanh(Module):
220:     r"""Applies the HardTanh function element-wise.
221: 
222:     HardTanh is defined as:
223: 
224:     .. math::
225:         \text{HardTanh}(x) = \begin{cases}
226:             \text{max\_val} & \text{ if } x > \text{ max\_val } \\
227:             \text{min\_val} & \text{ if } x < \text{ min\_val } \\
228:             x & \text{ otherwise } \\
229:         \end{cases}
230: 
231:     Args:
232:         min_val: minimum value of the linear region range. Default: -1
233:         max_val: maximum value of the linear region range. Default: 1
234:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
235: 
236:     Keyword arguments :attr:`min_value` and :attr:`max_value`
237:     have been deprecated in favor of :attr:`min_val` and :attr:`max_val`.
238: 
239:     Shape:
240:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
241:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
242: 
243:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Hardtanh.png
244: 
245:     Examples::
246: 
247:         >>> m = nn.Hardtanh(-2, 2)
248:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
249:         >>> output = m(input)
250:     """
251: 
252:     __constants__ = ["min_val", "max_val", "inplace"]
253: 
254:     min_val: float
255:     max_val: float
256:     inplace: bool
257: 
258:     def __init__(
259:         self,
260:         min_val: float = -1.0,
261:         max_val: float = 1.0,
262:         inplace: bool = False,
263:         min_value: float | None = None,
264:         max_value: float | None = None,
265:     ) -> None:
266:         super().__init__()
267:         if min_value is not None:
268:             warnings.warn(
269:                 "keyword argument `min_value` is deprecated and rename to `min_val`",
270:                 FutureWarning,
271:                 stacklevel=2,
272:             )
273:             min_val = min_value
274:         if max_value is not None:
275:             warnings.warn(
276:                 "keyword argument `max_value` is deprecated and rename to `max_val`",
277:                 FutureWarning,
278:                 stacklevel=2,
279:             )
280:             max_val = max_value
281: 
282:         self.min_val = min_val
283:         self.max_val = max_val
284:         self.inplace = inplace
285:         assert self.max_val > self.min_val
286: 
287:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
288:         """
289:         Runs the forward pass.
290:         """
291:         return F.hardtanh(input, self.min_val, self.max_val, self.inplace)
292: 
293:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
294:         """
295:         Return the extra representation of the module.
296:         """
297:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
298:         return f"min_val={self.min_val}, max_val={self.max_val}{inplace_str}"
299:

301: class ReLU6(Hardtanh):
302:     r"""Applies the ReLU6 function element-wise.
303: 
304:     .. math::
305:         \text{ReLU6}(x) = \min(\max(0,x), 6)
306: 
307:     Args:
308:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
309: 
310:     Shape:
311:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
312:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
313: 
314:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/ReLU6.png
315: 
316:     Examples::
317: 
318:         >>> m = nn.ReLU6()
319:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
320:         >>> output = m(input)
321:     """
322: 
323:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
324:         super().__init__(0.0, 6.0, inplace)
325: 
326:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
327:         """
328:         Return the extra representation of the module.
329:         """
330:         inplace_str = "inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
331:         return inplace_str

334: class Sigmoid(Module):
335:     r"""Applies the Sigmoid function element-wise.
336: 
337:     .. math::
338:         \text{Sigmoid}(x) = \sigma(x) = \frac{1}{1 + \exp(-x)}
339: 
340: 
341:     Shape:
342:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
343:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
344: 
345:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Sigmoid.png
346: 
347:     Examples::
348: 
349:         >>> m = nn.Sigmoid()
350:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
351:         >>> output = m(input)
352:     """
353: 
354:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
355:         """
356:         Runs the forward pass.
357:         """
358:         return torch.sigmoid(input)

361: class Hardsigmoid(Module):
362:     r"""Applies the Hardsigmoid function element-wise.
363: 
364:     Hardsigmoid is defined as:
365: 
366:     .. math::
367:         \text{Hardsigmoid}(x) = \begin{cases}
368:             0 & \text{if~} x \le -3, \\
369:             1 & \text{if~} x \ge +3, \\
370:             x / 6 + 1 / 2 & \text{otherwise}
371:         \end{cases}
372: 
373:     Args:
374:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
375: 
376:     Shape:
377:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
378:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
379: 
380:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Hardsigmoid.png
381: 
382:     Examples::
383: 
384:         >>> m = nn.Hardsigmoid()
385:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
386:         >>> output = m(input)
387:     """
388: 
389:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
390: 
391:     inplace: bool
392: 
393:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
394:         super().__init__()
395:         self.inplace = inplace
396: 
397:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
398:         """
399:         Runs the forward pass.
400:         """
401:         return F.hardsigmoid(input, self.inplace)

404: class Tanh(Module):
405:     r"""Applies the Hyperbolic Tangent (Tanh) function element-wise.
406: 
407:     Tanh is defined as:
408: 
409:     .. math::
410:         \text{Tanh}(x) = \tanh(x) = \frac{\exp(x) - \exp(-x)} {\exp(x) + \exp(-x)}
411: 
412:     Shape:
413:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
414:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
415: 
416:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Tanh.png
417: 
418:     Examples::
419: 
420:         >>> m = nn.Tanh()
421:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
422:         >>> output = m(input)
423:     """
424: 
425:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
426:         """
427:         Runs the forward pass.
428:         """
429:         return torch.tanh(input)

432: class SiLU(Module):
433:     r"""Applies the Sigmoid Linear Unit (SiLU) function, element-wise.
434: 
435:     The SiLU function is also known as the swish function.
436: 
437:     .. math::
438:         \text{silu}(x) = x * \sigma(x), \text{where } \sigma(x) \text{ is the logistic sigmoid.}
439: 
440:     .. note::
441:         See `Gaussian Error Linear Units (GELUs) <https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.08415>`_
442:         where the SiLU (Sigmoid Linear Unit) was originally coined, and see
443:         `Sigmoid-Weighted Linear Units for Neural Network Function Approximation
444:         in Reinforcement Learning <https://arxiv.org/abs/1702.03118>`_ and `Swish:
445:         a Self-Gated Activation Function <https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.05941v1>`_
446:         where the SiLU was experimented with later.
447: 
448:     Shape:
449:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
450:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
451: 
452:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/SiLU.png
453: 
454:     Examples::
455: 
456:         >>> m = nn.SiLU()
457:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
458:         >>> output = m(input)
459:     """
460: 
461:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
462:     inplace: bool
463: 
464:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
465:         super().__init__()
466:         self.inplace = inplace
467: 
468:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
469:         """
470:         Runs the forward pass.
471:         """
472:         return F.silu(input, inplace=self.inplace)
473: 
474:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
475:         """
476:         Return the extra representation of the module.
477:         """
478:         inplace_str = "inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
479:         return inplace_str

482: class Mish(Module):
483:     r"""Applies the Mish function, element-wise.
484: 
485:     Mish: A Self Regularized Non-Monotonic Neural Activation Function.
486: 
487:     .. math::
488:         \text{Mish}(x) = x * \text{Tanh}(\text{Softplus}(x))
489: 
490:     .. note::
491:         See `Mish: A Self Regularized Non-Monotonic Neural Activation Function <https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.08681>`_
492: 
493:     Shape:
494:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
495:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
496: 
497:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Mish.png
498: 
499:     Examples::
500: 
501:         >>> m = nn.Mish()
502:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
503:         >>> output = m(input)
504:     """
505: 
506:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
507:     inplace: bool
508: 
509:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
510:         super().__init__()
511:         self.inplace = inplace
512: 
513:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
514:         """
515:         Runs the forward pass.
516:         """
517:         return F.mish(input, inplace=self.inplace)
518: 
519:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
520:         """
521:         Return the extra representation of the module.
522:         """
523:         inplace_str = "inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
524:         return inplace_str

527: class Hardswish(Module):
528:     r"""Applies the Hardswish function, element-wise.
529: 
530:     Method described in the paper: `Searching for MobileNetV3 <https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.02244>`_.
531: 
532:     Hardswish is defined as:
533: 
534:     .. math::
535:         \text{Hardswish}(x) = \begin{cases}
536:             0 & \text{if~} x \le -3, \\
537:             x & \text{if~} x \ge +3, \\
538:             x \cdot (x + 3) /6 & \text{otherwise}
539:         \end{cases}
540: 
541:     Args:
542:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
543: 
544:     Shape:
545:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
546:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
547: 
548:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Hardswish.png
549: 
550:     Examples::
551: 
552:         >>> m = nn.Hardswish()
553:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
554:         >>> output = m(input)
555:     """
556: 
557:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
558: 
559:     inplace: bool
560: 
561:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
562:         super().__init__()
563:         self.inplace = inplace
564: 
565:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
566:         """
567:         Runs the forward pass.
568:         """
569:         return F.hardswish(input, self.inplace)

572: class ELU(Module):
573:     r"""Applies the Exponential Linear Unit (ELU) function, element-wise.
574: 
575:     Method described in the paper: `Fast and Accurate Deep Network Learning by Exponential Linear
576:     Units (ELUs) <https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.07289>`__.
577: 
578:     ELU is defined as:
579: 
580:     .. math::
581:         \text{ELU}(x) = \begin{cases}
582:         x, & \text{ if } x > 0\\
583:         \alpha * (\exp(x) - 1), & \text{ if } x \leq 0
584:         \end{cases}
585: 
586:     Args:
587:         alpha: the :math:`\alpha` value for the ELU formulation. Default: 1.0
588:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
589: 
590:     Shape:
591:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
592:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
593: 
594:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/ELU.png
595: 
596:     Examples::
597: 
598:         >>> m = nn.ELU()
599:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
600:         >>> output = m(input)
601:     """
602: 
603:     __constants__ = ["alpha", "inplace"]
604:     alpha: float
605:     inplace: bool
606: 
607:     def __init__(self, alpha: float = 1.0, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
608:         super().__init__()
609:         self.alpha = alpha
610:         self.inplace = inplace
611: 
612:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
613:         """
614:         Runs the forward pass.
615:         """
616:         return F.elu(input, self.alpha, self.inplace)
617: 
618:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
619:         """
620:         Return the extra representation of the module.
621:         """
622:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
623:         return f"alpha={self.alpha}{inplace_str}"
624: 
625: 
626: class CELU(Module):
627:     r"""Applies the CELU function element-wise.
628: 
629:     .. math::
630:         \text{CELU}(x) = \max(0,x) + \min(0, \alpha * (\exp(x/\alpha) - 1))
631: 
632:     More details can be found in the paper `Continuously Differentiable Exponential Linear Units`_ .
633: 
634:     Args:
635:         alpha: the :math:`\alpha` value for the CELU formulation. Default: 1.0
636:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
637: 
638:     Shape:
639:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
640:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
641: 
642:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/CELU.png
643: 
644:     Examples::
645: 
646:         >>> m = nn.CELU()
647:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
648:         >>> output = m(input)
649: 
650:     .. _`Continuously Differentiable Exponential Linear Units`:
651:         https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.07483
652:     """
653: 
654:     __constants__ = ["alpha", "inplace"]
655:     alpha: float
656:     inplace: bool
657: 
658:     def __init__(self, alpha: float = 1.0, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
659:         super().__init__()
660:         self.alpha = alpha
661:         self.inplace = inplace
662: 
663:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
664:         """
665:         Runs the forward pass.
666:         """
667:         return F.celu(input, self.alpha, self.inplace)
668: 
669:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
670:         """
671:         Return the extra representation of the module.
672:         """
673:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
674:         return f"alpha={self.alpha}{inplace_str}"
675: 
676: 
677: class SELU(Module):
678:     r"""Applies the SELU function element-wise.
679: 
680:     .. math::
681:         \text{SELU}(x) = \text{scale} * (\max(0,x) + \min(0, \alpha * (\exp(x) - 1)))
682: 
683:     with :math:`\alpha = 1.6732632423543772848170429916717` and
684:     :math:`\text{scale} = 1.0507009873554804934193349852946`.
685: 
686:     .. warning::
687:         When using ``kaiming_normal`` or ``kaiming_normal_`` for initialisation,
688:         ``nonlinearity='linear'`` should be used instead of ``nonlinearity='selu'``
689:         in order to get `Self-Normalizing Neural Networks`_.
690:         See :func:`torch.nn.init.calculate_gain` for more information.
691: 
692:     More details can be found in the paper `Self-Normalizing Neural Networks`_ .
693: 
694:     Args:
695:         inplace (bool, optional): can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
696: 
697:     Shape:
698:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
699:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
700: 
701:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/SELU.png
702: 
703:     Examples::
704: 
705:         >>> m = nn.SELU()
706:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
707:         >>> output = m(input)
708: 
709:     .. _Self-Normalizing Neural Networks: https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.02515
710:     """
711: 
712:     __constants__ = ["inplace"]
713:     inplace: bool
714: 
715:     def __init__(self, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
716:         super().__init__()
717:         self.inplace = inplace
718: 
719:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
720:         """
721:         Runs the forward pass.
722:         """
723:         return F.selu(input, self.inplace)
724: 
725:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
726:         """
727:         Return the extra representation of the module.
728:         """
729:         inplace_str = "inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
730:         return inplace_str

733: class GLU(Module):
734:     r"""Applies the gated linear unit function.
735: 
736:     :math:`{GLU}(a, b)= a \otimes \sigma(b)` where :math:`a` is the first half
737:     of the input matrices and :math:`b` is the second half.
738: 
739:     Args:
740:         dim (int): the dimension on which to split the input. Default: -1
741: 
742:     Shape:
743:         - Input: :math:`(\ast_1, N, \ast_2)` where `*` means, any number of additional
744:           dimensions
745:         - Output: :math:`(\ast_1, M, \ast_2)` where :math:`M=N/2`
746: 
747:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/GLU.png
748: 
749:     Examples::
750: 
751:         >>> m = nn.GLU()
752:         >>> input = torch.randn(4, 2)
753:         >>> output = m(input)
754:     """
755: 
756:     __constants__ = ["dim"]
757:     dim: int
758: 
759:     def __init__(self, dim: int = -1) -> None:
760:         super().__init__()
761:         self.dim = dim
762: 
763:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
764:         """
765:         Runs the forward pass.
766:         """
767:         return F.glu(input, self.dim)
768: 
769:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
770:         """
771:         Return the extra representation of the module.
772:         """
773:         return f"dim={self.dim}"

776: class GELU(Module):
777:     r"""Applies the Gaussian Error Linear Units function.
778: 
779:     .. math:: \text{GELU}(x) = x * \Phi(x)
780: 
781:     where :math:`\Phi(x)` is the Cumulative Distribution Function for Gaussian Distribution.
782: 
783:     When the approximate argument is 'tanh', Gelu is estimated with:
784: 
785:     .. math:: \text{GELU}(x) = 0.5 * x * (1 + \text{Tanh}(\sqrt{2 / \pi} * (x + 0.044715 * x^3)))
786: 
787:     Args:
788:         approximate (str, optional): the gelu approximation algorithm to use:
789:             ``'none'`` | ``'tanh'``. Default: ``'none'``
790: 
791:     Shape:
792:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
793:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
794: 
795:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/GELU.png
796: 
797:     Examples::
798: 
799:         >>> m = nn.GELU()
800:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
801:         >>> output = m(input)
802:     """
803: 
804:     __constants__ = ["approximate"]
805:     approximate: str
806: 
807:     def __init__(self, approximate: str = "none") -> None:
808:         super().__init__()
809:         self.approximate = approximate
810: 
811:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
812:         """
813:         Runs the forward pass.
814:         """
815:         return F.gelu(input, approximate=self.approximate)
816: 
817:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
818:         """
819:         Return the extra representation of the module.
820:         """
821:         return f"approximate={repr(self.approximate)}"

824: class Hardshrink(Module):
825:     r"""Applies the Hard Shrinkage (Hardshrink) function element-wise.
826: 
827:     Hardshrink is defined as:
828: 
829:     .. math::
830:         \text{HardShrink}(x) =
831:         \begin{cases}
832:         x, & \text{ if } x > \lambda \\
833:         x, & \text{ if } x < -\lambda \\
834:         0, & \text{ otherwise }
835:         \end{cases}
836: 
837:     Args:
838:         lambd: the :math:`\lambda` value for the Hardshrink formulation. Default: 0.5
839: 
840:     Shape:
841:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
842:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
843: 
844:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Hardshrink.png
845: 
846:     Examples::
847: 
848:         >>> m = nn.Hardshrink()
849:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
850:         >>> output = m(input)
851:     """
852: 
853:     __constants__ = ["lambd"]
854:     lambd: float
855: 
856:     def __init__(self, lambd: float = 0.5) -> None:
857:         super().__init__()
858:         self.lambd = lambd
859: 
860:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
861:         """
862:         Run forward pass.
863:         """
864:         return F.hardshrink(input, self.lambd)
865: 
866:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
867:         """
868:         Return the extra representation of the module.
869:         """
870:         return f"{self.lambd}"

873: class LeakyReLU(Module):
874:     r"""Applies the LeakyReLU function element-wise.
875: 
876:     .. math::
877:         \text{LeakyReLU}(x) = \max(0, x) + \text{negative\_slope} * \min(0, x)
878: 
879: 
880:     or
881: 
882:     .. math::
883:         \text{LeakyReLU}(x) =
884:         \begin{cases}
885:         x, & \text{ if } x \geq 0 \\
886:         \text{negative\_slope} \times x, & \text{ otherwise }
887:         \end{cases}
888: 
889:     Args:
890:         negative_slope: Controls the angle of the negative slope (which is used for
891:           negative input values). Default: 1e-2
892:         inplace: can optionally do the operation in-place. Default: ``False``
893: 
894:     Shape:
895:         - Input: :math:`(*)` where `*` means, any number of additional
896:           dimensions
897:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input
898: 
899:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/LeakyReLU.png
900: 
901:     Examples::
902: 
903:         >>> m = nn.LeakyReLU(0.1)
904:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
905:         >>> output = m(input)
906:     """
907: 
908:     __constants__ = ["inplace", "negative_slope"]
909:     inplace: bool
910:     negative_slope: float
911: 
912:     def __init__(self, negative_slope: float = 1e-2, inplace: bool = False) -> None:
913:         super().__init__()
914:         self.negative_slope = negative_slope
915:         self.inplace = inplace
916: 
917:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
918:         """
919:         Run forward pass.
920:         """
921:         return F.leaky_relu(input, self.negative_slope, self.inplace)
922: 
923:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
924:         """
925:         Return the extra representation of the module.
926:         """
927:         inplace_str = ", inplace=True" if self.inplace else ""
928:         return f"negative_slope={self.negative_slope}{inplace_str}"

936: 
937:     Shape:

949:

957: class Softplus(Module):
958:     r"""Applies the Softplus function element-wise.
959: 
960:     .. math::
961:         \text{Softplus}(x) = \frac{1}{\beta} * \log(1 + \exp(\beta * x))
962: 
963:     SoftPlus is a smooth approximation to the ReLU function and can be used
964:     to constrain the output of a machine to always be positive.
965: 
966:     For numerical stability the implementation reverts to the linear function
967:     when :math:`input \times \beta > threshold`.
968: 
969:     Args:
970:         beta: the :math:`\beta` value for the Softplus formulation. Default: 1
971:         threshold: values above this revert to a linear function. Default: 20
972: 
973:     Shape:
974:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
975:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
976: 
977:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Softplus.png
978: 
979:     Examples::
980: 
981:         >>> m = nn.Softplus()
982:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
983:         >>> output = m(input)
984:     """
985: 
986:     __constants__ = ["beta", "threshold"]
987:     beta: float
988:     threshold: float
989: 
990:     def __init__(self, beta: float = 1.0, threshold: float = 20.0) -> None:
991:         super().__init__()
992:         self.beta = beta
993:         self.threshold = threshold
994: 
995:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
996:         """
997:         Run forward pass.
998:         """
999:         return F.softplus(input, self.beta, self.threshold)
1000: 
1001:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1002:         """
1003:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1004:         """
1005:         return f"beta={self.beta}, threshold={self.threshold}"
1006:

1008: class Softshrink(Module):
1009:     r"""Applies the soft shrinkage function element-wise.
1010: 
1011:     .. math::
1012:         \text{SoftShrinkage}(x) =
1013:         \begin{cases}
1014:         x - \lambda, & \text{ if } x > \lambda \\
1015:         x + \lambda, & \text{ if } x < -\lambda \\
1016:         0, & \text{ otherwise }
1017:         \end{cases}
1018: 
1019:     Args:
1020:         lambd: the :math:`\lambda` (must be no less than zero) value for the Softshrink formulation. Default: 0.5
1021: 
1022:     Shape:
1023:         - Input: :math:`(*)`, where :math:`*` means any number of dimensions.
1024:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
1025: 
1026:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/Softshrink.png
1027: 
1028:     Examples::
1029: 
1030:         >>> m = nn.Softshrink()
1031:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
1032:         >>> output = m(input)
1033:     """
1034: 
1035:     __constants__ = ["lambd"]
1036:     lambd: float
1037: 
1038:     def __init__(self, lambd: float = 0.5) -> None:
1039:         super().__init__()
1040:         self.lambd = lambd
1041: 
1042:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1043:         """
1044:         Run forward pass.
1045:         """
1046:         return F.softshrink(input, self.lambd)
1047: 
1048:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1049:         """
1050:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1051:         """
1052:         return str(self.lambd)

1055: def _check_arg_device(x: torch.Tensor | None) -> bool:
1056:     if x is not None:
1057:         return x.device.type in [
1058:             "cpu",
1059:             "cuda",
1060:             torch.utils.backend_registration._privateuse1_backend_name,
1061:         ]
1062:     return True
1063: 
1064: 
1065: def _arg_requires_grad(x: torch.Tensor | None) -> bool:
1066:     if x is not None:
1067:         return x.requires_grad
1068:     return False

1071: def _is_make_fx_tracing():
1072:     if not torch.jit.is_scripting():
1073:         torch_dispatch_mode_stack = (
1074:             torch.utils._python_dispatch._get_current_dispatch_mode_stack()
1075:         )
1076:         # this can be triggered when dynamo inlining the module too.
1077:         return (
1078:             any(
1079:                 type(x) is torch.fx.experimental.proxy_tensor.ProxyTorchDispatchMode
1080:                 for x in torch_dispatch_mode_stack
1081:             )
1082:             or torch.compiler.is_exporting()
1083:         )
1084:     else:
1085:         return False

1087: 
1088: class MultiheadAttention(Module):
1089:     r"""Allows the model to jointly attend to information from different representation subspaces.
1090: 
1091:     This MultiheadAttention layer implements the original architecture described
1092:     in the `Attention Is All You Need <https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762>`_ paper. The
1093:     intent of this layer is as a reference implementation for foundational understanding
1094:     and thus it contains only limited features relative to newer architectures.
1095:     Given the fast pace of innovation in transformer-like architectures, we recommend
1096:     exploring this `tutorial <https://pytorch.org/tutorials/intermediate/transformer_building_blocks.html>`_
1097:     to build efficient layers from building blocks in core or using higher
1098:     level libraries from the `PyTorch Ecosystem <https://landscape.pytorch.org/>`_.
1099: 
1100:     Multi-Head Attention is defined as:
1101: 
1102:     .. math::
1103:         \text{MultiHead}(Q, K, V) = \text{Concat}(\text{head}_1,\dots,\text{head}_h)W^O
1104: 
1105:     where :math:`\text{head}_i = \text{Attention}(QW_i^Q, KW_i^K, VW_i^V)`.
1106: 
1107:     ``nn.MultiheadAttention`` will use the optimized implementations of
1108:     ``scaled_dot_product_attention()`` when possible.
1109: 
1110:     In addition to support for the new ``scaled_dot_product_attention()``
1111:     function, for speeding up Inference, MHA will use
1112:     fastpath inference with support for Nested Tensors, iff:
1113: 
1114:     - self attention is being computed (i.e., ``query``, ``key``, and ``value`` are the same tensor).
1115:     - inputs are batched (3D) with ``batch_first==True``
1116:     - Either autograd is disabled (using ``torch.inference_mode`` or ``torch.no_grad``) or no tensor argument ``requires_grad``
1117:     - training is disabled (using ``.eval()``)
1118:     - ``add_bias_kv`` is ``False``
1119:     - ``add_zero_attn`` is ``False``
1120:     - ``kdim`` and ``vdim`` are equal to ``embed_dim``
1121:     - if a `NestedTensor <https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/nested.html>`_ is passed, neither ``key_padding_mask``
1122:       nor ``attn_mask`` is passed
1123:     - autocast is disabled
1124: 
1125:     If the optimized inference fastpath implementation is in use, a
1126:     `NestedTensor <https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/nested.html>`_ can be passed for
1127:     ``query``/``key``/``value`` to represent padding more efficiently than using a
1128:     padding mask. In this case, a `NestedTensor <https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/nested.html>`_
1129:     will be returned, and an additional speedup proportional to the fraction of the input
1130:     that is padding can be expected.
1131: 
1132:     Args:
1133:         embed_dim: Total dimension of the model.
1134:         num_heads: Number of parallel attention heads. Note that ``embed_dim`` will be split
1135:             across ``num_heads`` (i.e. each head will have dimension ``embed_dim // num_heads``).
1136:         dropout: Dropout probability on ``attn_output_weights``. Default: ``0.0`` (no dropout).
1137:         bias: If specified, adds bias to input / output projection layers. Default: ``True``.
1138:         add_bias_kv: If specified, adds bias to the key and value sequences at dim=0. Default: ``False``.
1139:         add_zero_attn: If specified, adds a new batch of zeros to the key and value sequences at dim=1.
1140:             Default: ``False``.
1141:         kdim: Total number of features for keys. Default: ``None`` (uses ``kdim=embed_dim``).
1142:         vdim: Total number of features for values. Default: ``None`` (uses ``vdim=embed_dim``).
1143:         batch_first: If ``True``, then the input and output tensors are provided
1144:             as (batch, seq, feature). Default: ``False`` (seq, batch, feature).
1145: 
1146:     Examples::
1147: 
1148:         >>> # xdoctest: +SKIP
1149:         >>> multihead_attn = nn.MultiheadAttention(embed_dim, num_heads)
1150:         >>> attn_output, attn_output_weights = multihead_attn(query, key, value)
1151: 
1152:     .. _`FlashAttention: Fast and Memory-Efficient Exact Attention with IO-Awareness`:
1153:          https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14135
1154: 
1155:     """
1156: 
1157:     __constants__ = ["batch_first"]
1158:     bias_k: torch.Tensor | None
1159:     bias_v: torch.Tensor | None
1160: 
1161:     def __init__(
1162:         self,
1163:         embed_dim,
1164:         num_heads,
1165:         dropout=0.0,
1166:         bias=True,
1167:         add_bias_kv=False,
1168:         add_zero_attn=False,
1169:         kdim=None,
1170:         vdim=None,
1171:         batch_first=False,
1172:         device=None,
1173:         dtype=None,
1174:     ) -> None:
1175:         if embed_dim <= 0 or num_heads <= 0:
1176:             raise ValueError(
1177:                 f"embed_dim and num_heads must be greater than 0,"
1178:                 f" got embed_dim={embed_dim} and num_heads={num_heads} instead"
1179:             )
1180:         factory_kwargs = {"device": device, "dtype": dtype}
1181:         super().__init__()
1182:         self.embed_dim = embed_dim
1183:         self.kdim = kdim if kdim is not None else embed_dim
1184:         self.vdim = vdim if vdim is not None else embed_dim
1185:         self._qkv_same_embed_dim = self.kdim == embed_dim and self.vdim == embed_dim
1186: 
1187:         self.num_heads = num_heads
1188:         self.dropout = dropout
1189:         self.batch_first = batch_first
1190:         self.head_dim = embed_dim // num_heads
1191:         assert self.head_dim * num_heads == self.embed_dim, (
1192:             "embed_dim must be divisible by num_heads"
1193:         )
1194: 
1195:         if not self._qkv_same_embed_dim:
1196:             self.q_proj_weight = Parameter(
1197:                 torch.empty((embed_dim, embed_dim), **factory_kwargs)
1198:             )
1199:             self.k_proj_weight = Parameter(
1200:                 torch.empty((embed_dim, self.kdim), **factory_kwargs)
1201:             )
1202:             self.v_proj_weight = Parameter(
1203:                 torch.empty((embed_dim, self.vdim), **factory_kwargs)
1204:             )
1205:             self.register_parameter("in_proj_weight", None)
1206:         else:
1207:             self.in_proj_weight = Parameter(
1208:                 torch.empty((3 * embed_dim, embed_dim), **factory_kwargs)
1209:             )
1210:             self.register_parameter("q_proj_weight", None)
1211:             self.register_parameter("k_proj_weight", None)
1212:             self.register_parameter("v_proj_weight", None)
1213: 
1214:         if bias:
1215:             self.in_proj_bias = Parameter(torch.empty(3 * embed_dim, **factory_kwargs))
1216:         else:
1217:             self.register_parameter("in_proj_bias", None)
1218:         self.out_proj = NonDynamicallyQuantizableLinear(
1219:             embed_dim, embed_dim, bias=bias, **factory_kwargs
1220:         )
1221: 
1222:         if add_bias_kv:
1223:             self.bias_k = Parameter(torch.empty((1, 1, embed_dim), **factory_kwargs))
1224:             self.bias_v = Parameter(torch.empty((1, 1, embed_dim), **factory_kwargs))
1225:         else:
1226:             self.bias_k = self.bias_v = None
1227: 
1228:         self.add_zero_attn = add_zero_attn
1229: 
1230:         self._reset_parameters()
1231: 
1232:     def _reset_parameters(self) -> None:
1233:         if self._qkv_same_embed_dim:
1234:             xavier_uniform_(self.in_proj_weight)
1235:         else:
1236:             xavier_uniform_(self.q_proj_weight)
1237:             xavier_uniform_(self.k_proj_weight)
1238:             xavier_uniform_(self.v_proj_weight)
1239: 
1240:         if self.in_proj_bias is not None:
1241:             constant_(self.in_proj_bias, 0.0)
1242:             constant_(self.out_proj.bias, 0.0)
1243:         if self.bias_k is not None:
1244:             xavier_normal_(self.bias_k)
1245:         if self.bias_v is not None:
1246:             xavier_normal_(self.bias_v)
1247: 
1248:     def __setstate__(self, state):
1249:         # Support loading old MultiheadAttention checkpoints generated by v1.1.0
1250:         if "_qkv_same_embed_dim" not in state:
1251:             state["_qkv_same_embed_dim"] = True
1252: 
1253:         super().__setstate__(state)
1254: 
1255:     def forward(
1256:         self,
1257:         query: Tensor,
1258:         key: Tensor,
1259:         value: Tensor,
1260:         key_padding_mask: Tensor | None = None,
1261:         need_weights: bool = True,
1262:         attn_mask: Tensor | None = None,
1263:         average_attn_weights: bool = True,
1264:         is_causal: bool = False,
1265:     ) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor | None]:
1266:         r"""Compute attention outputs using query, key, and value embeddings.
1267: 
1268:             Supports optional parameters for padding, masks and attention weights.
1269: 
1270:         Args:
1271:             query: Query embeddings of shape :math:`(L, E_q)` for unbatched input, :math:`(L, N, E_q)` when ``batch_first=False``
1272:                 or :math:`(N, L, E_q)` when ``batch_first=True``, where :math:`L` is the target sequence length,
1273:                 :math:`N` is the batch size, and :math:`E_q` is the query embedding dimension ``embed_dim``.
1274:                 Queries are compared against key-value pairs to produce the output.
1275:                 See "Attention Is All You Need" for more details.
1276:             key: Key embeddings of shape :math:`(S, E_k)` for unbatched input, :math:`(S, N, E_k)` when ``batch_first=False``
1277:                 or :math:`(N, S, E_k)` when ``batch_first=True``, where :math:`S` is the source sequence length,
1278:                 :math:`N` is the batch size, and :math:`E_k` is the key embedding dimension ``kdim``.
1279:                 See "Attention Is All You Need" for more details.
1280:             value: Value embeddings of shape :math:`(S, E_v)` for unbatched input, :math:`(S, N, E_v)` when
1281:                 ``batch_first=False`` or :math:`(N, S, E_v)` when ``batch_first=True``, where :math:`S` is the source
1282:                 sequence length, :math:`N` is the batch size, and :math:`E_v` is the value embedding dimension ``vdim``.
1283:                 See "Attention Is All You Need" for more details.
1284:             key_padding_mask: If specified, a mask of shape :math:`(N, S)` indicating which elements within ``key``
1285:                 to ignore for the purpose of attention (i.e. treat as "padding"). For unbatched `query`, shape should be :math:`(S)`.
1286:                 Binary and float masks are supported.
1287:                 For a binary mask, a ``True`` value indicates that the corresponding ``key`` value will be ignored for
1288:                 the purpose of attention. For a float mask, it will be directly added to the corresponding ``key`` value.
1289:             need_weights: If specified, returns ``attn_output_weights`` in addition to ``attn_outputs``.
1290:                 Set ``need_weights=False`` to use the optimized ``scaled_dot_product_attention``
1291:                 and achieve the best performance for MHA.
1292:                 Default: ``True``.
1293:             attn_mask: If specified, a 2D or 3D mask preventing attention to certain positions. Must be of shape
1294:                 :math:`(L, S)` or :math:`(N\cdot\text{num\_heads}, L, S)`, where :math:`N` is the batch size,
1295:                 :math:`L` is the target sequence length, and :math:`S` is the source sequence length. A 2D mask will be
1296:                 broadcasted across the batch while a 3D mask allows for a different mask for each entry in the batch.
1297:                 Binary and float masks are supported. For a binary mask, a ``True`` value indicates that the
1298:                 corresponding position is not allowed to attend. For a float mask, the mask values will be added to
1299:                 the attention weight.
1300:                 If both attn_mask and key_padding_mask are supplied, their types should match.
1301:             average_attn_weights: If true, indicates that the returned ``attn_weights`` should be averaged across
1302:                 heads. Otherwise, ``attn_weights`` are provided separately per head. Note that this flag only has an
1303:                 effect when ``need_weights=True``. Default: ``True`` (i.e. average weights across heads)
1304:             is_causal: If specified, applies a causal mask as attention mask.
1305:                 Default: ``False``.
1306:                 Warning:
1307:                 ``is_causal`` provides a hint that ``attn_mask`` is the
1308:                 causal mask. Providing incorrect hints can result in
1309:                 incorrect execution, including forward and backward
1310:                 compatibility.
1311: 
1312:         Outputs:
1313:             - **attn_output** - Attention outputs of shape :math:`(L, E)` when input is unbatched,
1314:               :math:`(L, N, E)` when ``batch_first=False`` or :math:`(N, L, E)` when ``batch_first=True``,
1315:               where :math:`L` is the target sequence length, :math:`N` is the batch size, and :math:`E` is the
1316:               embedding dimension ``embed_dim``.
1317:             - **attn_output_weights** - Only returned when ``need_weights=True``. If ``average_attn_weights=True``,
1318:               returns attention weights averaged across heads of shape :math:`(L, S)` when input is unbatched or
1319:               :math:`(N, L, S)`, where :math:`N` is the batch size, :math:`L` is the target sequence length, and
1320:               :math:`S` is the source sequence length. If ``average_attn_weights=False``, returns attention weights per
1321:               head of shape :math:`(\text{num\_heads}, L, S)` when input is unbatched or :math:`(N, \text{num\_heads}, L, S)`.
1322: 
1323:             .. note::
1324:                 `batch_first` argument is ignored for unbatched inputs.
1325:         """  # noqa: B950
1326:         why_not_fast_path = ""
1327:         if (
1328:             (attn_mask is not None and torch.is_floating_point(attn_mask))
1329:             or (key_padding_mask is not None)
1330:             and torch.is_floating_point(key_padding_mask)
1331:         ):
1332:             why_not_fast_path = "floating-point masks are not supported for fast path."
1333: 
1334:         is_batched = query.dim() == 3
1335: 
1336:         key_padding_mask = F._canonical_mask(
1337:             mask=key_padding_mask,
1338:             mask_name="key_padding_mask",
1339:             other_type=F._none_or_dtype(attn_mask),
1340:             other_name="attn_mask",
1341:             target_type=query.dtype,
1342:         )
1343: 
1344:         attn_mask = F._canonical_mask(
1345:             mask=attn_mask,
1346:             mask_name="attn_mask",
1347:             other_type=None,
1348:             other_name="",
1349:             target_type=query.dtype,
1350:             check_other=False,
1351:         )
1352: 
1353:         is_fastpath_enabled = torch.backends.mha.get_fastpath_enabled()
1354: 
1355:         if not is_fastpath_enabled:
1356:             why_not_fast_path = "torch.backends.mha.get_fastpath_enabled() was not True"
1357:         elif not is_batched:
1358:             why_not_fast_path = (
1359:                 f"input not batched; expected query.dim() of 3 but got {query.dim()}"
1360:             )
1361:         elif query is not key or key is not value:
1362:             # When lifting this restriction, don't forget to either
1363:             # enforce that the dtypes all match or test cases where
1364:             # they don't!
1365:             why_not_fast_path = "non-self attention was used (query, key, and value are not the same Tensor)"
1366:         elif self.in_proj_bias is not None and query.dtype != self.in_proj_bias.dtype:
1367:             why_not_fast_path = f"dtypes of query ({query.dtype}) and self.in_proj_bias ({self.in_proj_bias.dtype}) don't match"
1368:         elif self.in_proj_weight is None:
1369:             why_not_fast_path = "in_proj_weight was None"
1370:         elif query.dtype != self.in_proj_weight.dtype:
1371:             # this case will fail anyway, but at least they'll get a useful error message.
1372:             why_not_fast_path = f"dtypes of query ({query.dtype}) and self.in_proj_weight ({self.in_proj_weight.dtype}) don't match"
1373:         elif self.training:
1374:             why_not_fast_path = "training is enabled"
1375:         elif (self.num_heads % 2) != 0:
1376:             why_not_fast_path = "self.num_heads is not even"
1377:         elif not self.batch_first:
1378:             why_not_fast_path = "batch_first was not True"
1379:         elif self.bias_k is not None:
1380:             why_not_fast_path = "self.bias_k was not None"
1381:         elif self.bias_v is not None:
1382:             why_not_fast_path = "self.bias_v was not None"
1383:         elif self.add_zero_attn:
1384:             why_not_fast_path = "add_zero_attn was enabled"
1385:         elif not self._qkv_same_embed_dim:
1386:             why_not_fast_path = "_qkv_same_embed_dim was not True"
1387:         elif query.is_nested and (
1388:             key_padding_mask is not None or attn_mask is not None
1389:         ):
1390:             why_not_fast_path = (
1391:                 "supplying both src_key_padding_mask and src_mask at the same time \
1392:                                  is not supported with NestedTensor input"
1393:             )
1394:         elif torch.is_autocast_enabled():
1395:             why_not_fast_path = "autocast is enabled"
1396: 
1397:         if not why_not_fast_path:
1398:             tensor_args = (
1399:                 query,
1400:                 key,
1401:                 value,
1402:                 self.in_proj_weight,
1403:                 self.in_proj_bias,
1404:                 self.out_proj.weight,
1405:                 self.out_proj.bias,
1406:             )
1407:             # We have to use list comprehensions below because TorchScript does not support
1408:             # generator expressions.
1409:             if torch.overrides.has_torch_function(tensor_args):
1410:                 why_not_fast_path = "some Tensor argument has_torch_function"
1411:             elif _is_make_fx_tracing():
1412:                 why_not_fast_path = "we are running make_fx tracing"
1413:             elif not all(_check_arg_device(x) for x in tensor_args):
1414:                 why_not_fast_path = (
1415:                     "some Tensor argument's device is neither one of "
1416:                     f"cpu, cuda or {torch.utils.backend_registration._privateuse1_backend_name}"
1417:                 )
1418:             elif torch.is_grad_enabled() and any(
1419:                 _arg_requires_grad(x) for x in tensor_args
1420:             ):
1421:                 why_not_fast_path = (
1422:                     "grad is enabled and at least one of query or the "
1423:                     "input/output projection weights or biases requires_grad"
1424:                 )
1425:             if not why_not_fast_path:
1426:                 merged_mask, mask_type = self.merge_masks(
1427:                     attn_mask, key_padding_mask, query
1428:                 )
1429: 
1430:                 if self.in_proj_bias is not None and self.in_proj_weight is not None:
1431:                     return torch._native_multi_head_attention(
1432:                         query,
1433:                         key,
1434:                         value,
1435:                         self.embed_dim,
1436:                         self.num_heads,
1437:                         self.in_proj_weight,
1438:                         self.in_proj_bias,
1439:                         self.out_proj.weight,
1440:                         self.out_proj.bias,
1441:                         merged_mask,
1442:                         need_weights,
1443:                         average_attn_weights,
1444:                         mask_type,
1445:                     )
1446: 
1447:         any_nested = query.is_nested or key.is_nested or value.is_nested
1448:         assert not any_nested, (
1449:             "MultiheadAttention does not support NestedTensor outside of its fast path. "
1450:             + f"The fast path was not hit because {why_not_fast_path}"
1451:         )
1452: 
1453:         if self.batch_first and is_batched:
1454:             # make sure that the transpose op does not affect the "is" property
1455:             if key is value:
1456:                 if query is key:
1457:                     query = key = value = query.transpose(1, 0)
1458:                 else:
1459:                     query, key = (x.transpose(1, 0) for x in (query, key))
1460:                     value = key
1461:             else:
1462:                 query, key, value = (x.transpose(1, 0) for x in (query, key, value))
1463: 
1464:         if not self._qkv_same_embed_dim:
1465:             attn_output, attn_output_weights = F.multi_head_attention_forward(
1466:                 query,
1467:                 key,
1468:                 value,
1469:                 self.embed_dim,
1470:                 self.num_heads,
1471:                 self.in_proj_weight,
1472:                 self.in_proj_bias,
1473:                 self.bias_k,
1474:                 self.bias_v,
1475:                 self.add_zero_attn,
1476:                 self.dropout,
1477:                 self.out_proj.weight,
1478:                 self.out_proj.bias,
1479:                 training=self.training,
1480:                 key_padding_mask=key_padding_mask,
1481:                 need_weights=need_weights,
1482:                 attn_mask=attn_mask,
1483:                 use_separate_proj_weight=True,
1484:                 q_proj_weight=self.q_proj_weight,
1485:                 k_proj_weight=self.k_proj_weight,
1486:                 v_proj_weight=self.v_proj_weight,
1487:                 average_attn_weights=average_attn_weights,
1488:                 is_causal=is_causal,
1489:             )
1490:         else:
1491:             attn_output, attn_output_weights = F.multi_head_attention_forward(
1492:                 query,
1493:                 key,
1494:                 value,
1495:                 self.embed_dim,
1496:                 self.num_heads,
1497:                 self.in_proj_weight,
1498:                 self.in_proj_bias,
1499:                 self.bias_k,
1500:                 self.bias_v,
1501:                 self.add_zero_attn,
1502:                 self.dropout,
1503:                 self.out_proj.weight,
1504:                 self.out_proj.bias,
1505:                 training=self.training,
1506:                 key_padding_mask=key_padding_mask,
1507:                 need_weights=need_weights,
1508:                 attn_mask=attn_mask,
1509:                 average_attn_weights=average_attn_weights,
1510:                 is_causal=is_causal,
1511:             )
1512:         if self.batch_first and is_batched:
1513:             return attn_output.transpose(1, 0), attn_output_weights
1514:         else:
1515:             return attn_output, attn_output_weights
1516: 
1517:     def merge_masks(
1518:         self,
1519:         attn_mask: Tensor | None,
1520:         key_padding_mask: Tensor | None,
1521:         query: Tensor,
1522:     ) -> tuple[Tensor | None, int | None]:
1523:         r"""Determine mask type and combine masks if necessary.
1524: 
1525:         If only one mask is provided, that mask
1526:         and the corresponding mask type will be returned. If both masks are provided, they will be both
1527:         expanded to shape ``(batch_size, num_heads, seq_len, seq_len)``, combined with logical ``or``
1528:         and mask type 2 will be returned
1529:         Args:
1530:             attn_mask: attention mask of shape ``(seq_len, seq_len)``, mask type 0
1531:             key_padding_mask: padding mask of shape ``(batch_size, seq_len)``, mask type 1
1532:             query: query embeddings of shape ``(batch_size, seq_len, embed_dim)``
1533:         Returns:
1534:             merged_mask: merged mask
1535:             mask_type: merged mask type (0, 1, or 2)
1536:         """
1537:         mask_type: int | None = None
1538:         merged_mask: Tensor | None = None
1539: 
1540:         if key_padding_mask is not None:
1541:             mask_type = 1
1542:             merged_mask = key_padding_mask
1543: 
1544:         if attn_mask is not None:
1545:             # In this branch query can't be a nested tensor, so it has a shape
1546:             batch_size, seq_len, _ = query.shape
1547:             mask_type = 2
1548: 
1549:             # Always expands attn_mask to 4D
1550:             if attn_mask.dim() == 3:
1551:                 attn_mask_expanded = attn_mask.view(batch_size, -1, seq_len, seq_len)
1552:             else:  # attn_mask.dim() == 2:
1553:                 attn_mask_expanded = attn_mask.view(1, 1, seq_len, seq_len).expand(
1554:                     batch_size, self.num_heads, -1, -1
1555:                 )
1556:             merged_mask = attn_mask_expanded
1557: 
1558:             if key_padding_mask is not None:
1559:                 key_padding_mask_expanded = key_padding_mask.view(
1560:                     batch_size, 1, 1, seq_len
1561:                 ).expand(-1, self.num_heads, -1, -1)
1562:                 merged_mask = attn_mask_expanded + key_padding_mask_expanded
1563: 
1564:         # no attn_mask and no key_padding_mask, returns None, None
1565:         return merged_mask, mask_type
1566: 
1567: 
1568: class PReLU(Module):
1569:     r"""Applies the element-wise PReLU function.
1570: 
1571:     .. math::
1572:         \text{PReLU}(x) = \max(0,x) + a * \min(0,x)
1573: 
1574:     or
1575: 
1576:     .. math::
1577:         \text{PReLU}(x) =
1578:         \begin{cases}
1579:         x, & \text{ if } x \ge 0 \\
1580:         ax, & \text{ otherwise }
1581:         \end{cases}
1582: 
1583:     Here :math:`a` is a learnable parameter. When called without arguments, `nn.PReLU()` uses a single
1584:     parameter :math:`a` across all input channels. If called with `nn.PReLU(nChannels)`,
1585:     a separate :math:`a` is used for each input channel.
1586: 
1587: 
1588:     .. note::
1589:         weight decay should not be used when learning :math:`a` for good performance.
1590: 
1591:     .. note::
1592:         Channel dim is the 2nd dim of input. When input has dims < 2, then there is
1593:         no channel dim and the number of channels = 1.
1594: 
1595:     Args:
1596:         num_parameters (int): number of :math:`a` to learn.
1597:             Although it takes an int as input, there is only two values are legitimate:
1598:             1, or the number of channels at input. Default: 1
1599:         init (float): the initial value of :math:`a`. Default: 0.25
1600: 
1601:     Shape:
1602:         - Input: :math:`( *)` where `*` means, any number of additional
1603:           dimensions.
1604:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input.
1605: 
1606:     Attributes:
1607:         weight (Tensor): the learnable weights of shape (:attr:`num_parameters`).
1608: 
1609:     .. image:: ../scripts/activation_images/PReLU.png
1610: 
1611:     Examples::
1612: 
1613:         >>> m = nn.PReLU()
1614:         >>> input = torch.randn(2)
1615:         >>> output = m(input)
1616:     """
1617: 
1618:     __constants__ = ["num_parameters"]
1619:     num_parameters: int
1620: 
1621:     def __init__(
1622:         self, num_parameters: int = 1, init: float = 0.25, device=None, dtype=None
1623:     ) -> None:
1624:         factory_kwargs = {"device": device, "dtype": dtype}
1625:         self.num_parameters = num_parameters
1626:         super().__init__()
1627:         self.init = init
1628:         self.weight = Parameter(torch.empty(num_parameters, **factory_kwargs))
1629:         self.reset_parameters()
1630: 
1631:     def reset_parameters(self) -> None:
1632:         """
1633:         Resets parameters based on their initialization used in ``__init__``.
1634:         """
1635:         torch.nn.init.constant_(self.weight, self.init)
1636: 
1637:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1638:         """
1639:         Runs the forward pass.
1640:         """
1641:         return F.prelu(input, self.weight)
1642: 
1643:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1644:         """
1645:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1646:         """
1647:         return f"num_parameters={self.num_parameters}"
1648:

1702: class Softmin(Module):
1703:     r"""Applies the Softmin function to an n-dimensional input Tensor.
1704: 
1705:     Rescales them so that the elements of the n-dimensional output Tensor
1706:     lie in the range `[0, 1]` and sum to 1.
1707: 
1708:     Softmin is defined as:
1709: 
1710:     .. math::
1711:         \text{Softmin}(x_{i}) = \frac{\exp(-x_i)}{\sum_j \exp(-x_j)}
1712: 
1713:     Shape:
1714:         - Input: :math:`(*)` where `*` means, any number of additional
1715:           dimensions
1716:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input
1717: 
1718:     Args:
1719:         dim (int): A dimension along which Softmin will be computed (so every slice
1720:             along dim will sum to 1).
1721: 
1722:     Returns:
1723:         a Tensor of the same dimension and shape as the input, with
1724:         values in the range [0, 1]
1725: 
1726:     Examples::
1727: 
1728:         >>> m = nn.Softmin(dim=1)
1729:         >>> input = torch.randn(2, 3)
1730:         >>> output = m(input)
1731:     """
1732: 
1733:     __constants__ = ["dim"]
1734:     dim: int | None
1735: 
1736:     def __init__(self, dim: int | None = None) -> None:
1737:         super().__init__()
1738:         self.dim = dim
1739: 
1740:     def __setstate__(self, state):
1741:         super().__setstate__(state)
1742:         if not hasattr(self, "dim"):
1743:             self.dim = None
1744: 
1745:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1746:         """
1747:         Runs the forward pass.
1748:         """
1749:         return F.softmin(input, self.dim, _stacklevel=5)
1750: 
1751:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1752:         """
1753:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1754:         """
1755:         return f"dim={self.dim}"
1756: 
1757: 
1758: class Softmax(Module):
1759:     r"""Applies the Softmax function to an n-dimensional input Tensor.
1760: 
1761:     Rescales them so that the elements of the n-dimensional output Tensor
1762:     lie in the range [0,1] and sum to 1.
1763: 
1764:     Softmax is defined as:
1765: 
1766:     .. math::
1767:         \text{Softmax}(x_{i}) = \frac{\exp(x_i)}{\sum_j \exp(x_j)}
1768: 
1769:     When the input Tensor is a sparse tensor then the unspecified
1770:     values are treated as ``-inf``.
1771: 
1772:     Shape:
1773:         - Input: :math:`(*)` where `*` means, any number of additional
1774:           dimensions
1775:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input
1776: 
1777:     Returns:
1778:         a Tensor of the same dimension and shape as the input with
1779:         values in the range [0, 1]
1780: 
1781:     Args:
1782:         dim (int): A dimension along which Softmax will be computed (so every slice
1783:             along dim will sum to 1).
1784: 
1785:     .. note::
1786:         This module doesn't work directly with NLLLoss,
1787:         which expects the Log to be computed between the Softmax and itself.
1788:         Use `LogSoftmax` instead (it's faster and has better numerical properties).
1789: 
1790:     Examples::
1791: 
1792:         >>> m = nn.Softmax(dim=1)
1793:         >>> input = torch.randn(2, 3)
1794:         >>> output = m(input)
1795: 
1796:     """
1797: 
1798:     __constants__ = ["dim"]
1799:     dim: int | None
1800: 
1801:     def __init__(self, dim: int | None = None) -> None:
1802:         super().__init__()
1803:         self.dim = dim
1804: 
1805:     def __setstate__(self, state):
1806:         super().__setstate__(state)
1807:         if not hasattr(self, "dim"):
1808:             self.dim = None
1809: 
1810:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1811:         """
1812:         Runs the forward pass.
1813:         """
1814:         return F.softmax(input, self.dim, _stacklevel=5)
1815: 
1816:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1817:         """
1818:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1819:         """
1820:         return f"dim={self.dim}"
1821: 
1822: 
1823: class Softmax2d(Module):
1824:     r"""Applies SoftMax over features to each spatial location.
1825: 
1826:     When given an image of ``Channels x Height x Width``, it will
1827:     apply `Softmax` to each location :math:`(Channels, h_i, w_j)`
1828: 
1829:     Shape:
1830:         - Input: :math:`(N, C, H, W)` or :math:`(C, H, W)`.
1831:         - Output: :math:`(N, C, H, W)` or :math:`(C, H, W)` (same shape as input)
1832: 
1833:     Returns:
1834:         a Tensor of the same dimension and shape as the input with
1835:         values in the range [0, 1]
1836: 
1837:     Examples::
1838: 
1839:         >>> m = nn.Softmax2d()
1840:         >>> # you softmax over the 2nd dimension
1841:         >>> input = torch.randn(2, 3, 12, 13)
1842:         >>> output = m(input)
1843:     """
1844: 
1845:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1846:         """
1847:         Runs the forward pass.
1848:         """
1849:         if input.dim() not in (3, 4):
1850:             raise ValueError(
1851:                 f"Softmax2d: expected input to be 3D or 4D, got {input.dim()}D instead"
1852:             )
1853:         return F.softmax(input, -3, _stacklevel=5)
1854: 
1855: 
1856: class LogSoftmax(Module):
1857:     r"""Applies the :math:`\log(\text{Softmax}(x))` function to an n-dimensional input Tensor.
1858: 
1859:     The LogSoftmax formulation can be simplified as:
1860: 
1861:     .. math::
1862:         \text{LogSoftmax}(x_{i}) = \log\left(\frac{\exp(x_i) }{ \sum_j \exp(x_j)} \right)
1863: 
1864:     Shape:
1865:         - Input: :math:`(*)` where `*` means, any number of additional
1866:           dimensions
1867:         - Output: :math:`(*)`, same shape as the input
1868: 
1869:     Args:
1870:         dim (int): A dimension along which LogSoftmax will be computed.
1871: 
1872:     Returns:
1873:         a Tensor of the same dimension and shape as the input with
1874:         values in the range [-inf, 0)
1875: 
1876:     Examples::
1877: 
1878:         >>> m = nn.LogSoftmax(dim=1)
1879:         >>> input = torch.randn(2, 3)
1880:         >>> output = m(input)
1881:     """
1882: 
1883:     __constants__ = ["dim"]
1884:     dim: int | None
1885: 
1886:     def __init__(self, dim: int | None = None) -> None:
1887:         super().__init__()
1888:         self.dim = dim
1889: 
1890:     def __setstate__(self, state):
1891:         super().__setstate__(state)
1892:         if not hasattr(self, "dim"):
1893:             self.dim = None
1894: 
1895:     def forward(self, input: Tensor) -> Tensor:
1896:         """
1897:         Runs the forward pass.
1898:         """
1899:         return F.log_softmax(input, self.dim, _stacklevel=5)
1900: 
1901:     def extra_repr(self) -> str:
1902:         """
1903:         Return the extra representation of the module.
1904:         """
1905:         return f"dim={self.dim}"

55: def _addindent(s_, numSpaces):
56:     s = s_.split("\n")
57:     # don't do anything for single-line stuff
58:     if len(s) == 1:
59:         return s_
60:     first = s.pop(0)
61:     s = [(numSpaces * " ") + line for line in s]
62:     s = "\n".join(s)
63:     s = first + "\n" + s
64:     return s

67: r"""This tracks hooks common to all modules that are executed immediately before
68: .registering the buffer/module/parameter"""
69: _global_buffer_registration_hooks: dict[int, Callable] = OrderedDict()
70: _global_module_registration_hooks: dict[int, Callable] = OrderedDict()
71: _global_parameter_registration_hooks: dict[int, Callable] = OrderedDict()

73: 
74: class _WrappedHook:
75:     def __init__(self, hook: Callable, module: Optional["Module"] = None) -> None:
76:         self.hook: Callable = hook
77:         functools.update_wrapper(self, hook)
78: 
79:         self.with_module: bool = False
80: 
81:         if module is not None:
82:             self.module: weakref.ReferenceType[Module] = weakref.ref(module)
83:             self.with_module = True
84: 
85:     def __call__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
86:         if self.with_module:
87:             module = self.module()
88:             if module is None:
89:                 raise RuntimeError("You are trying to call the hook of a dead Module!")
90:             return self.hook(module, *args, **kwargs)
91:         return self.hook(*args, **kwargs)
92: 
93:     def __getstate__(self) -> dict:
94:         result = {"hook": self.hook, "with_module": self.with_module}
95:         if self.with_module:
96:             # pyrefly: ignore [unsupported-operation]
97:             result["module"] = self.module()
98: 
99:         return result
100: 
101:     def __setstate__(self, state: dict):
102:         self.hook = state["hook"]
103:         self.with_module = state["with_module"]
104: 
105:         if self.with_module:
106:             if state["module"] is None:
107:                 raise RuntimeError(
108:                     "You are trying to revive the hook of a dead Module!"
109:                 )
110:             self.module = weakref.ref(state["module"])

139: def register_module_buffer_registration_hook(
140:     hook: Callable[..., None],
141: ) -> RemovableHandle:
142:     r"""Register a buffer registration hook common to all modules.
143: 
144:     .. warning ::
145: 
146:         This adds global state to the `nn.Module` module
147: 
148:     The hook will be called every time :func:`register_buffer` is invoked.
149:     It should have the following signature::
150: 
151:         hook(module, name, buffer) -> None or new buffer
152: 
153:     The hook can modify the input or return a single modified value in the hook.
154: 
155:     Returns:
156:         :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
157:             a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
158:             ``handle.remove()``
159:     """
160:     handle = RemovableHandle(_global_buffer_registration_hooks)
161:     _global_buffer_registration_hooks[handle.id] = hook
162:     return handle

217: def register_module_forward_pre_hook(hook: Callable[..., None]) -> RemovableHandle:
218:     r"""Register a forward pre-hook common to all modules.
219: 
220:     .. warning ::
221: 
222:         This adds global state to the `nn.module` module
223:         and it is only intended for debugging/profiling purposes.
224: 
225:     The hook will be called every time before :func:`forward` is invoked.
226:     It should have the following signature::
227: 
228:         hook(module, input) -> None or modified input
229: 
230:     The input contains only the positional arguments given to the module.
231:     Keyword arguments won't be passed to the hooks and only to the ``forward``.
232:     The hook can modify the input. User can either return a tuple or a
233:     single modified value in the hook. We will wrap the value into a tuple
234:     if a single value is returned(unless that value is already a tuple).
235: 
236:     This hook has precedence over the specific module hooks registered with
237:     ``register_forward_pre_hook``.
238: 
239:     Returns:
240:         :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
241:             a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
242:             ``handle.remove()``
243:     """
244:     handle = RemovableHandle(_global_forward_pre_hooks)
245:     _global_forward_pre_hooks[handle.id] = hook
246:     return handle
247: 
248: 
249: def register_module_forward_hook(
250:     hook: Callable[..., None],
251:     *,
252:     with_kwargs: bool = False,
253:     always_call: bool = False,
254: ) -> RemovableHandle:
255:     r"""Register a global forward hook for all the modules.
256: 
257:     .. warning ::
258: 
259:         This adds global state to the `nn.module` module
260:         and it is only intended for debugging/profiling purposes.
261: 
262:     The hook will be called every time after :func:`forward` has computed an output.
263:     It should have the following signature::
264: 
265:         hook(module, input, output) -> None or modified output
266: 
267:     The input contains only the positional arguments given to the module.
268:     Keyword arguments won't be passed to the hooks and only to the ``forward``.
269:     You can optionally modify the output of the module by returning a new value
270:     that will replace the output from the :func:`forward` function.
271: 
272:     Parameters:
273:         hook (Callable): The user defined hook to be registered.
274:         always_call (bool): If ``True`` the ``hook`` will be run regardless of
275:             whether an exception is raised while calling the Module.
276:             Default: ``False``
277:     Returns:
278:         :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
279:             a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
280:             ``handle.remove()``
281: 
282:     This hook will be executed before specific module hooks registered with
283:     ``register_forward_hook``.
284:     """
285:     handle = RemovableHandle(
286:         _global_forward_hooks, extra_dict=_global_forward_hooks_always_called
287:     )
288:     _global_forward_hooks[handle.id] = hook
289:     if with_kwargs:
290:         _global_forward_hooks_with_kwargs[handle.id] = True
291:     if always_call:
292:         _global_forward_hooks_always_called[handle.id] = True
293:     return handle
294:

296: def register_module_backward_hook(
297:     hook: Callable[["Module", _grad_t, _grad_t], None | _grad_t],
298: ) -> RemovableHandle:
299:     r"""Register a backward hook common to all the modules.
300: 
301:     This function is deprecated in favor of
302:     :func:`torch.nn.modules.module.register_module_full_backward_hook`
303:     and the behavior of this function will change in future versions.
304: 
305:     Returns:
306:         :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
307:             a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
308:             ``handle.remove()``
309: 
310:     """
311:     global _global_is_full_backward_hook
312:     if _global_is_full_backward_hook is True:
313:         raise RuntimeError(
314:             "Cannot use both regular backward hooks and full backward hooks as a "
315:             "global Module hook. Please use only one of them."
316:         )
317: 
318:     _global_is_full_backward_hook = False
319: 
320:     handle = RemovableHandle(_global_backward_hooks)
321:     _global_backward_hooks[handle.id] = hook
322:     return handle

388: # Trick mypy into not applying contravariance rules to inputs by defining
389: # forward as a value, rather than a function.  See also
390: # https://github.com/python/mypy/issues/8795
391: def _forward_unimplemented(self, *input: Any) -> None:
392:     r"""Define the computation performed at every call.
393: 
394:     Should be overridden by all subclasses.
395: 
396:     .. note::
397:         Although the recipe for forward pass needs to be defined within
398:         this function, one should call the :class:`Module` instance afterwards
399:         instead of this since the former takes care of running the
400:         registered hooks while the latter silently ignores them.
401:     """
402:     raise NotImplementedError(
403:         f'Module [{type(self).__name__}] is missing the required "forward" function'
404:     )
405: 
406: 
407: class Module:
408:     r"""Base class for all neural network modules.
409: 
410:     Your models should also subclass this class.
411: 
412:     Modules can also contain other Modules, allowing them to be nested in
413:     a tree structure. You can assign the submodules as regular attributes::
414: 
415:         import torch.nn as nn
416:         import torch.nn.functional as F
417: 
418: 
419:         class Model(nn.Module):
420:             def __init__(self) -> None:
421:                 super().__init__()
422:                 self.conv1 = nn.Conv2d(1, 20, 5)
423:                 self.conv2 = nn.Conv2d(20, 20, 5)
424: 
425:             def forward(self, x):
426:                 x = F.relu(self.conv1(x))
427:                 return F.relu(self.conv2(x))
428: 
429:     Submodules assigned in this way will be registered, and will also have their
430:     parameters converted when you call :meth:`to`, etc.
431: 
432:     .. note::
433:         As per the example above, an ``__init__()`` call to the parent class
434:         must be made before assignment on the child.
435: 
436:     :ivar training: Boolean represents whether this module is in training or
437:                     evaluation mode.
438:     :vartype training: bool
439:     """
440: 
441:     dump_patches: bool = False
442: 
443:     _version: int = 1
444:     r"""This allows better BC support for :meth:`load_state_dict`. In
445:     :meth:`state_dict`, the version number will be saved as in the attribute
446:     `_metadata` of the returned state dict, and thus pickled. `_metadata` is a
447:     dictionary with keys that follow the naming convention of state dict. See
448:     ``_load_from_state_dict`` on how to use this information in loading.
449: 
450:     If new parameters/buffers are added/removed from a module, this number shall
451:     be bumped, and the module's `_load_from_state_dict` method can compare the
452:     version number and do appropriate changes if the state dict is from before
453:     the change."""
454: 
455:     training: bool
456:     _parameters: dict[str, Parameter | None]
457:     _buffers: dict[str, Tensor | None]
458:     _non_persistent_buffers_set: set[str]
459:     _backward_pre_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
460:     _backward_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
461:     _is_full_backward_hook: bool | None
462:     _forward_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
463:     # Marks whether the corresponding _forward_hooks accept kwargs or not.
464:     # As JIT does not support set[int], this dict is used as a set, where all
465:     # hooks represented in this dict accept kwargs.
466:     _forward_hooks_with_kwargs: dict[int, bool]
467:     # forward hooks that should always be called even if an exception is raised
468:     _forward_hooks_always_called: dict[int, bool]
469:     _forward_pre_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
