470:     # Marks whether the corresponding _forward_hooks accept kwargs or not.
471:     # As JIT does not support set[int], this dict is used as a set, where all
472:     # hooks represented in this dict accept kwargs.
473:     _forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs: dict[int, bool]
474:     _state_dict_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
475:     _load_state_dict_pre_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
476:     _state_dict_pre_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
477:     _load_state_dict_post_hooks: dict[int, Callable]
478:     _modules: dict[str, Optional["Module"]]
479:     call_super_init: bool = False
480:     _compiled_call_impl: Callable | None = None
481: 
482:     def __init__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> None:
483:         """Initialize internal Module state, shared by both nn.Module and ScriptModule."""
484:         torch._C._log_api_usage_once("python.nn_module")
485: 
486:         # Backward compatibility: no args used to be allowed when call_super_init=False
487:         if self.call_super_init is False and bool(kwargs):
488:             raise TypeError(
489:                 f"{type(self).__name__}.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument '{next(iter(kwargs))}'"
490:                 ""
491:             )
492: 
493:         if self.call_super_init is False and bool(args):
494:             raise TypeError(
495:                 f"{type(self).__name__}.__init__() takes 1 positional argument but {len(args) + 1} were"
496:                 " given"
497:             )
498: 
499:         """
500:         Calls super().__setattr__('a', a) instead of the typical self.a = a
501:         to avoid Module.__setattr__ overhead. Module's __setattr__ has special
502:         handling for parameters, submodules, and buffers but simply calls into
503:         super().__setattr__ for all other attributes.
504:         """
505:         super().__setattr__("training", True)
506:         super().__setattr__("_parameters", {})
507:         super().__setattr__("_buffers", {})
508:         super().__setattr__("_non_persistent_buffers_set", set())
509:         super().__setattr__("_backward_pre_hooks", OrderedDict())
510:         super().__setattr__("_backward_hooks", OrderedDict())
511:         super().__setattr__("_is_full_backward_hook", None)
512:         super().__setattr__("_forward_hooks", OrderedDict())
513:         super().__setattr__("_forward_hooks_with_kwargs", OrderedDict())
514:         super().__setattr__("_forward_hooks_always_called", OrderedDict())
515:         super().__setattr__("_forward_pre_hooks", OrderedDict())
516:         super().__setattr__("_forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs", OrderedDict())
517:         super().__setattr__("_state_dict_hooks", OrderedDict())
518:         super().__setattr__("_state_dict_pre_hooks", OrderedDict())
519:         super().__setattr__("_load_state_dict_pre_hooks", OrderedDict())
520:         super().__setattr__("_load_state_dict_post_hooks", OrderedDict())
521:         super().__setattr__("_modules", {})
522: 
523:         if self.call_super_init:
524:             super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
525: 
526:     forward: Callable[..., Any] = _forward_unimplemented
527: 
528:     def register_buffer(
529:         self, name: str, tensor: Tensor | None, persistent: bool = True
530:     ) -> None:
531:         r"""Add a buffer to the module.
532: 
533:         This is typically used to register a buffer that should not be
534:         considered a model parameter. For example, BatchNorm's ``running_mean``
535:         is not a parameter, but is part of the module's state. Buffers, by
536:         default, are persistent and will be saved alongside parameters. This
537:         behavior can be changed by setting :attr:`persistent` to ``False``. The
538:         only difference between a persistent buffer and a non-persistent buffer
539:         is that the latter will not be a part of this module's
540:         :attr:`state_dict`.
541: 
542:         Buffers can be accessed as attributes using given names.
543: 
544:         Args:
545:             name (str): name of the buffer. The buffer can be accessed
546:                 from this module using the given name
547:             tensor (Tensor or None): buffer to be registered. If ``None``, then operations
548:                 that run on buffers, such as :attr:`cuda`, are ignored. If ``None``,
549:                 the buffer is **not** included in the module's :attr:`state_dict`.
550:             persistent (bool): whether the buffer is part of this module's
551:                 :attr:`state_dict`.
552: 
553:         Example::
554: 
555:             >>> # xdoctest: +SKIP("undefined vars")
556:             >>> self.register_buffer('running_mean', torch.zeros(num_features))
557: 
558:         """
559:         if persistent is False and isinstance(self, torch.jit.ScriptModule):
560:             raise RuntimeError("ScriptModule does not support non-persistent buffers")
561: 
562:         if "_buffers" not in self.__dict__:
563:             raise AttributeError("cannot assign buffer before Module.__init__() call")
564:         elif not isinstance(name, str):
565:             raise TypeError(
566:                 f"buffer name should be a string. Got {torch.typename(name)}"
567:             )
568:         elif "." in name:
569:             raise KeyError('buffer name can\'t contain "."')
570:         elif name == "":
571:             raise KeyError('buffer name can\'t be empty string ""')
572:         elif hasattr(self, name) and name not in self._buffers:
573:             raise KeyError(f"attribute '{name}' already exists")
574:         elif tensor is not None and not (
575:             isinstance(tensor, torch.Tensor) or hasattr(tensor, "__torch_function__")
576:         ):
577:             raise TypeError(
578:                 f"cannot assign '{torch.typename(tensor)}' object to buffer '{name}' "
579:                 "(torch Tensor or None required)"
580:             )
581:         else:
582:             for hook in _global_buffer_registration_hooks.values():
583:                 output = hook(self, name, tensor)
584:                 if output is not None:
585:                     tensor = output
586:             self._buffers[name] = tensor
587:             if persistent:
588:                 self._non_persistent_buffers_set.discard(name)
589:             else:
590:                 self._non_persistent_buffers_set.add(name)
591: 
592:     def register_parameter(self, name: str, param: Parameter | None) -> None:
593:         r"""Add a parameter to the module.
594: 
595:         The parameter can be accessed as an attribute using given name.
596: 
597:         Args:
598:             name (str): name of the parameter. The parameter can be accessed
599:                 from this module using the given name
600:             param (Parameter or None): parameter to be added to the module. If
601:                 ``None``, then operations that run on parameters, such as :attr:`cuda`,
602:                 are ignored. If ``None``, the parameter is **not** included in the
603:                 module's :attr:`state_dict`.
604:         """
605:         if "_parameters" not in self.__dict__:
606:             raise AttributeError(
607:                 "cannot assign parameter before Module.__init__() call"
608:             )
609: 
610:         elif not isinstance(name, str):
611:             raise TypeError(
612:                 f"parameter name should be a string. Got {torch.typename(name)}"
613:             )
614:         elif "." in name:
615:             raise KeyError('parameter name can\'t contain "."')
616:         elif name == "":
617:             raise KeyError('parameter name can\'t be empty string ""')
618:         elif hasattr(self, name) and name not in self._parameters:
619:             raise KeyError(f"attribute '{name}' already exists")
620: 
621:         if param is None:
622:             self._parameters[name] = None
623:         elif not isinstance(param, Parameter):
624:             raise TypeError(
625:                 f"cannot assign '{torch.typename(param)}' object to parameter '{name}' "
626:                 "(torch.nn.Parameter or None required)"
627:             )
628:         elif param.grad_fn:
629:             raise ValueError(
630:                 f"Cannot assign non-leaf Tensor to parameter '{name}'. Model "
631:                 f"parameters must be created explicitly. To express '{name}' "
632:                 "as a function of another Tensor, compute the value in "
633:                 "the forward() method."
634:             )
635:         else:
636:             for hook in _global_parameter_registration_hooks.values():
637:                 output = hook(self, name, param)
638:                 if output is not None:
639:                     param = output
640:             self._parameters[name] = param
641: 
642:     def add_module(self, name: str, module: Optional["Module"]) -> None:
643:         r"""Add a child module to the current module.
644: 
645:         The module can be accessed as an attribute using the given name.
646: 
647:         Args:
648:             name (str): name of the child module. The child module can be
649:                 accessed from this module using the given name
650:             module (Module): child module to be added to the module.
651:         """
652:         if not isinstance(module, Module) and module is not None:
653:             raise TypeError(f"{torch.typename(module)} is not a Module subclass")
654:         elif not isinstance(name, str):
655:             raise TypeError(
656:                 f"module name should be a string. Got {torch.typename(name)}"
657:             )
658:         elif hasattr(self, name) and name not in self._modules:
659:             raise KeyError(f"attribute '{name}' already exists")
660:         elif "." in name:
661:             raise KeyError(f'module name can\'t contain ".", got: {name}')
662:         elif name == "":
663:             raise KeyError('module name can\'t be empty string ""')
664:         for hook in _global_module_registration_hooks.values():
665:             output = hook(self, name, module)
666:             if output is not None:
667:                 module = output
668:         self._modules[name] = module
669: 
670:     def register_module(self, name: str, module: Optional["Module"]) -> None:
671:         r"""Alias for :func:`add_module`."""
672:         self.add_module(name, module)
673: 
674:     def get_submodule(self, target: str) -> "Module":
675:         """Return the submodule given by ``target`` if it exists, otherwise throw an error.
676: 
677:         For example, let's say you have an ``nn.Module`` ``A`` that
678:         looks like this:
679: 
680:         .. code-block:: text
681: 
682:             A(
683:                 (net_b): Module(
684:                     (net_c): Module(
685:                         (conv): Conv2d(16, 33, kernel_size=(3, 3), stride=(2, 2))
686:                     )
687:                     (linear): Linear(in_features=100, out_features=200, bias=True)
688:                 )
689:             )
690: 
691:         (The diagram shows an ``nn.Module`` ``A``. ``A`` which has a nested
692:         submodule ``net_b``, which itself has two submodules ``net_c``
693:         and ``linear``. ``net_c`` then has a submodule ``conv``.)
694: 
695:         To check whether or not we have the ``linear`` submodule, we
696:         would call ``get_submodule("net_b.linear")``. To check whether
697:         we have the ``conv`` submodule, we would call
698:         ``get_submodule("net_b.net_c.conv")``.
699: 
700:         The runtime of ``get_submodule`` is bounded by the degree
701:         of module nesting in ``target``. A query against
702:         ``named_modules`` achieves the same result, but it is O(N) in
703:         the number of transitive modules. So, for a simple check to see
704:         if some submodule exists, ``get_submodule`` should always be
705:         used.
706: 
707:         Args:
708:             target: The fully-qualified string name of the submodule
709:                 to look for. (See above example for how to specify a
710:                 fully-qualified string.)
711: 
712:         Returns:
713:             torch.nn.Module: The submodule referenced by ``target``
714: 
715:         Raises:
716:             AttributeError: If at any point along the path resulting from
717:                 the target string the (sub)path resolves to a non-existent
718:                 attribute name or an object that is not an instance of ``nn.Module``.
719:         """
720:         if target == "":
721:             return self
722: 
723:         atoms: list[str] = target.split(".")
724:         mod: torch.nn.Module = self
725: 
726:         for item in atoms:
727:             if not hasattr(mod, item):
728:                 raise AttributeError(
729:                     mod._get_name() + " has no attribute `" + item + "`"
730:                 )
731: 
732:             mod = getattr(mod, item)
733: 
734:             if not isinstance(mod, torch.nn.Module):
735:                 raise AttributeError("`" + item + "` is not an nn.Module")
736: 
737:         return mod
738: 
739:     def set_submodule(
740:         self, target: str, module: "Module", strict: bool = False
741:     ) -> None:
742:         """
743:         Set the submodule given by ``target`` if it exists, otherwise throw an error.
744: 
745:         .. note::
746:             If ``strict`` is set to ``False`` (default), the method will replace an existing submodule
747:             or create a new submodule if the parent module exists. If ``strict`` is set to ``True``,
748:             the method will only attempt to replace an existing submodule and throw an error if
749:             the submodule does not exist.
750: 
751:         For example, let's say you have an ``nn.Module`` ``A`` that
752:         looks like this:
753: 
754:         .. code-block:: text
755: 
756:             A(
757:                 (net_b): Module(
758:                     (net_c): Module(
759:                         (conv): Conv2d(3, 3, 3)
760:                     )
761:                     (linear): Linear(3, 3)
762:                 )
763:             )
764: 
765:         (The diagram shows an ``nn.Module`` ``A``. ``A`` has a nested
766:         submodule ``net_b``, which itself has two submodules ``net_c``
767:         and ``linear``. ``net_c`` then has a submodule ``conv``.)
768: 
769:         To override the ``Conv2d`` with a new submodule ``Linear``, you
770:         could call ``set_submodule("net_b.net_c.conv", nn.Linear(1, 1))``
771:         where ``strict`` could be ``True`` or ``False``
772: 
773:         To add a new submodule ``Conv2d`` to the existing ``net_b`` module,
774:         you would call ``set_submodule("net_b.conv", nn.Conv2d(1, 1, 1))``.
775: 
776:         In the above if you set ``strict=True`` and call
777:         ``set_submodule("net_b.conv", nn.Conv2d(1, 1, 1), strict=True)``, an AttributeError
778:         will be raised because ``net_b`` does not have a submodule named ``conv``.
779: 
780:         Args:
781:             target: The fully-qualified string name of the submodule
782:                 to look for. (See above example for how to specify a
783:                 fully-qualified string.)
784:             module: The module to set the submodule to.
785:             strict: If ``False``, the method will replace an existing submodule
786:                 or create a new submodule if the parent module exists. If ``True``,
787:                 the method will only attempt to replace an existing submodule and throw an error
788:                 if the submodule doesn't already exist.
789: 
790:         Raises:
791:             ValueError: If the ``target`` string is empty or if ``module`` is not an instance of ``nn.Module``.
792:             AttributeError: If at any point along the path resulting from
793:                 the ``target`` string the (sub)path resolves to a non-existent
794:                 attribute name or an object that is not an instance of ``nn.Module``.
795:         """
796:         if target == "":
797:             raise ValueError("Cannot set the submodule without a target name!")
798: 
799:         atoms: list[str] = target.split(".")
800:         if not isinstance(module, torch.nn.Module):
801:             raise ValueError(
802:                 "`" + "module" + f"` is not an nn.Module, found {type(module)}"
803:             )
804:         if len(atoms) == 1:
805:             parent: torch.nn.Module = self
806:         else:
807:             parent_key = ".".join(atoms[:-1])
808:             parent = self.get_submodule(parent_key)
809: 
810:         if strict and not hasattr(parent, atoms[-1]):
811:             raise AttributeError(
812:                 parent._get_name() + " has no attribute `" + atoms[-1] + "`"
813:             )
814:         if hasattr(parent, atoms[-1]):
815:             mod = getattr(parent, atoms[-1])
816:             if not isinstance(mod, torch.nn.Module):
817:                 raise AttributeError("`" + atoms[-1] + "` is not an nn.Module")
818:         setattr(parent, atoms[-1], module)
819: 
820:     def get_parameter(self, target: str) -> "Parameter":
821:         """Return the parameter given by ``target`` if it exists, otherwise throw an error.
822: 
823:         See the docstring for ``get_submodule`` for a more detailed
824:         explanation of this method's functionality as well as how to
825:         correctly specify ``target``.
826: 
827:         Args:
828:             target: The fully-qualified string name of the Parameter
829:                 to look for. (See ``get_submodule`` for how to specify a
830:                 fully-qualified string.)
831: 
832:         Returns:
833:             torch.nn.Parameter: The Parameter referenced by ``target``
834: 
835:         Raises:
836:             AttributeError: If the target string references an invalid
837:                 path or resolves to something that is not an
838:                 ``nn.Parameter``
839:         """
840:         module_path, _, param_name = target.rpartition(".")
841: 
842:         mod: torch.nn.Module = self.get_submodule(module_path)
843: 
844:         if not hasattr(mod, param_name):
845:             raise AttributeError(
846:                 mod._get_name() + " has no attribute `" + param_name + "`"
847:             )
848: 
849:         param: torch.nn.Parameter = getattr(mod, param_name)
850: 
851:         if not isinstance(param, torch.nn.Parameter):
852:             raise AttributeError("`" + param_name + "` is not an nn.Parameter")
853: 
854:         return param
855: 
856:     def get_buffer(self, target: str) -> "Tensor":
857:         """Return the buffer given by ``target`` if it exists, otherwise throw an error.
858: 
859:         See the docstring for ``get_submodule`` for a more detailed
860:         explanation of this method's functionality as well as how to
861:         correctly specify ``target``.
862: 
863:         Args:
864:             target: The fully-qualified string name of the buffer
865:                 to look for. (See ``get_submodule`` for how to specify a
866:                 fully-qualified string.)
867: 
868:         Returns:
869:             torch.Tensor: The buffer referenced by ``target``
870: 
871:         Raises:
872:             AttributeError: If the target string references an invalid
873:                 path or resolves to something that is not a
874:                 buffer
875:         """
876:         module_path, _, buffer_name = target.rpartition(".")
877: 
878:         mod: torch.nn.Module = self.get_submodule(module_path)
879: 
880:         if not hasattr(mod, buffer_name):
881:             raise AttributeError(
882:                 mod._get_name() + " has no attribute `" + buffer_name + "`"
883:             )
884: 
885:         buffer: torch.Tensor = getattr(mod, buffer_name)
886: 
887:         if buffer_name not in mod._buffers:
888:             raise AttributeError("`" + buffer_name + "` is not a buffer")
889: 
890:         return buffer
891: 
892:     def get_extra_state(self) -> Any:
893:         """Return any extra state to include in the module's state_dict.
894: 
895:         Implement this and a corresponding :func:`set_extra_state` for your module
896:         if you need to store extra state. This function is called when building the
897:         module's `state_dict()`.
898: 
899:         Note that extra state should be picklable to ensure working serialization
900:         of the state_dict. We only provide backwards compatibility guarantees
901:         for serializing Tensors; other objects may break backwards compatibility if
902:         their serialized pickled form changes.
903: 
904:         Returns:
905:             object: Any extra state to store in the module's state_dict
906:         """
907:         raise RuntimeError(
908:             "Reached a code path in Module.get_extra_state() that should never be called. "
909:             "Please file an issue at https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/new?template=bug-report.yml "
910:             "to report this bug."
911:         )
912: 
913:     def set_extra_state(self, state: Any) -> None:
914:         """Set extra state contained in the loaded `state_dict`.
915: 
916:         This function is called from :func:`load_state_dict` to handle any extra state
917:         found within the `state_dict`. Implement this function and a corresponding
918:         :func:`get_extra_state` for your module if you need to store extra state within its
919:         `state_dict`.
920: 
921:         Args:
922:             state (dict): Extra state from the `state_dict`
923:         """
924:         raise RuntimeError(
925:             "Reached a code path in Module.set_extra_state() that should never be called. "
926:             "Please file an issue at https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/new?template=bug-report.yml "
927:             "to report this bug."
928:         )
929: 
930:     def _apply(self, fn, recurse=True):
931:         if recurse:
932:             for module in self.children():
933:                 module._apply(fn)
934: 
935:         from torch._subclasses.fake_tensor import FakeTensor
936: 
937:         def compute_should_use_set_data(tensor, tensor_applied) -> bool:
938:             if torch._has_compatible_shallow_copy_type(
939:                 tensor, tensor_applied
940:             ) and not isinstance(tensor_applied, FakeTensor):
941:                 # If the new tensor has compatible tensor type as the existing tensor,
942:                 # the current behavior is to change the tensor in-place using `.data =`,
943:                 # and the future behavior is to overwrite the existing tensor. However,
944:                 # changing the current behavior is a BC-breaking change, and we want it
945:                 # to happen in future releases. So for now we introduce the
946:                 # `torch.__future__.get_overwrite_module_params_on_conversion()`
947:                 # global flag to let the user control whether they want the future
948:                 # behavior of overwriting the existing tensor or not.
949:                 return not torch.__future__.get_overwrite_module_params_on_conversion()
950:             else:
951:                 return False
952: 
953:         should_use_swap_tensors = (
954:             torch.__future__.get_swap_module_params_on_conversion()
955:         )
956: 
957:         for key, param in self._parameters.items():
958:             if param is None:
959:                 continue
960:             # Tensors stored in modules are graph leaves, and we don't want to
961:             # track autograd history of `param_applied`, so we have to use
962:             # `with torch.no_grad():`
963:             with torch.no_grad():
964:                 param_applied = fn(param)
965:             p_should_use_set_data = compute_should_use_set_data(param, param_applied)
966: 
967:             # subclasses may have multiple child tensors so we need to use swap_tensors
968:             p_should_use_swap_tensors = (
969:                 should_use_swap_tensors
970:                 or is_traceable_wrapper_subclass(param_applied)
971:                 or isinstance(param, FakeTensor)
972:             )
973: 
974:             param_grad = param.grad
975:             if p_should_use_swap_tensors:
976:                 try:
977:                     if param_grad is not None:
978:                         # Accessing param.grad makes its at::Tensor's use_count 2, which will prevent swapping.
979:                         # Decrement use count of the gradient by setting to None
980:                         param.grad = None
981:                     param_applied = torch.nn.Parameter(
982:                         # pyrefly: ignore [bad-argument-type]
983:                         param_applied,
984:                         requires_grad=param.requires_grad,
985:                     )
986:                     torch.utils.swap_tensors(param, param_applied)
987:                 except Exception as e:
988:                     if param_grad is not None:
989:                         param.grad = param_grad
990:                     raise RuntimeError(
991:                         f"_apply(): Couldn't swap {self._get_name()}.{key}"
992:                     ) from e
993:                 out_param = param
994:             elif p_should_use_set_data:
995:                 # pyrefly: ignore [bad-assignment]
996:                 param.data = param_applied
997:                 out_param = param
998:             else:
999:                 assert isinstance(param, Parameter)
1000:                 assert param.is_leaf
1001:                 # pyrefly: ignore [bad-argument-type]
1002:                 out_param = Parameter(param_applied, param.requires_grad)
1003:                 self._parameters[key] = out_param
1004: 
1005:             if param_grad is not None:
1006:                 with torch.no_grad():
1007:                     grad_applied = fn(param_grad)
1008:                 g_should_use_set_data = compute_should_use_set_data(
1009:                     param_grad, grad_applied
1010:                 )
1011:                 if p_should_use_swap_tensors:
1012:                     grad_applied.requires_grad_(param_grad.requires_grad)
1013:                     try:
1014:                         torch.utils.swap_tensors(param_grad, grad_applied)
1015:                     except Exception as e:
1016:                         raise RuntimeError(
1017:                             f"_apply(): Couldn't swap {self._get_name()}.{key}.grad"
1018:                         ) from e
1019:                     out_param.grad = param_grad
1020:                 elif g_should_use_set_data:
1021:                     assert out_param.grad is not None
1022:                     out_param.grad.data = grad_applied
1023:                 else:
1024:                     assert param_grad.is_leaf
1025:                     out_param.grad = grad_applied.requires_grad_(
1026:                         param_grad.requires_grad
1027:                     )
1028: 
1029:         for key, buf in self._buffers.items():
1030:             if buf is not None:
1031:                 self._buffers[key] = fn(buf)
1032: 
1033:         return self
1034: 
1035:     def apply(self, fn: Callable[["Module"], None]) -> Self:
1036:         r"""Apply ``fn`` recursively to every submodule (as returned by ``.children()``) as well as self.
1037: 
1038:         Typical use includes initializing the parameters of a model
1039:         (see also :ref:`nn-init-doc`).
1040: 
1041:         Args:
1042:             fn (:class:`Module` -> None): function to be applied to each submodule
1043: 
1044:         Returns:
1045:             Module: self
1046: 
1047:         Example::
1048: 
1049:             >>> @torch.no_grad()
1050:             >>> def init_weights(m):
1051:             >>>     print(m)
1052:             >>>     if type(m) is nn.Linear:
1053:             >>>         m.weight.fill_(1.0)
1054:             >>>         print(m.weight)
1055:             >>> net = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(2, 2), nn.Linear(2, 2))
1056:             >>> net.apply(init_weights)
1057:             Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1058:             Parameter containing:
1059:             tensor([[1., 1.],
1060:                     [1., 1.]], requires_grad=True)
1061:             Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1062:             Parameter containing:
1063:             tensor([[1., 1.],
1064:                     [1., 1.]], requires_grad=True)
1065:             Sequential(
1066:               (0): Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1067:               (1): Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1068:             )
1069: 
1070:         """
1071:         for module in self.children():
1072:             module.apply(fn)
1073:         fn(self)
1074:         return self
1075: 
1076:     def cuda(self, device: int | device | None = None) -> Self:
1077:         r"""Move all model parameters and buffers to the GPU.
1078: 
1079:         This also makes associated parameters and buffers different objects. So
1080:         it should be called before constructing the optimizer if the module will
1081:         live on GPU while being optimized.
1082: 
1083:         .. note::
1084:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1085: 
1086:         Args:
1087:             device (int, optional): if specified, all parameters will be
1088:                 copied to that device
1089: 
1090:         Returns:
1091:             Module: self
1092:         """
1093:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.cuda(device))
1094: 
1095:     def ipu(self, device: int | device | None = None) -> Self:
1096:         r"""Move all model parameters and buffers to the IPU.
1097: 
1098:         This also makes associated parameters and buffers different objects. So
1099:         it should be called before constructing the optimizer if the module will
1100:         live on IPU while being optimized.
1101: 
1102:         .. note::
1103:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1104: 
1105:         Arguments:
1106:             device (int, optional): if specified, all parameters will be
1107:                 copied to that device
1108: 
1109:         Returns:
1110:             Module: self
1111:         """
1112:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.ipu(device))
1113: 
1114:     def xpu(self, device: int | device | None = None) -> Self:
1115:         r"""Move all model parameters and buffers to the XPU.
1116: 
1117:         This also makes associated parameters and buffers different objects. So
1118:         it should be called before constructing optimizer if the module will
1119:         live on XPU while being optimized.
1120: 
1121:         .. note::
1122:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1123: 
1124:         Arguments:
1125:             device (int, optional): if specified, all parameters will be
1126:                 copied to that device
1127: 
1128:         Returns:
1129:             Module: self
1130:         """
1131:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.xpu(device))
1132: 
1133:     def mtia(self, device: int | device | None = None) -> Self:
1134:         r"""Move all model parameters and buffers to the MTIA.
1135: 
1136:         This also makes associated parameters and buffers different objects. So
1137:         it should be called before constructing the optimizer if the module will
1138:         live on MTIA while being optimized.
1139: 
1140:         .. note::
1141:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1142: 
1143:         Arguments:
1144:             device (int, optional): if specified, all parameters will be
1145:                 copied to that device
1146: 
1147:         Returns:
1148:             Module: self
1149:         """
1150:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.mtia(device))
1151: 
1152:     def cpu(self) -> Self:
1153:         r"""Move all model parameters and buffers to the CPU.
1154: 
1155:         .. note::
1156:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1157: 
1158:         Returns:
1159:             Module: self
1160:         """
1161:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.cpu())
1162: 
1163:     def type(self, dst_type: dtype | str) -> Self:
1164:         r"""Casts all parameters and buffers to :attr:`dst_type`.
1165: 
1166:         .. note::
1167:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1168: 
1169:         Args:
1170:             dst_type (type or string): the desired type
1171: 
1172:         Returns:
1173:             Module: self
1174:         """
1175:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.type(dst_type))
1176: 
1177:     def float(self) -> Self:
1178:         r"""Casts all floating point parameters and buffers to ``float`` datatype.
1179: 
1180:         .. note::
1181:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1182: 
1183:         Returns:
1184:             Module: self
1185:         """
1186:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.float() if t.is_floating_point() else t)
1187: 
1188:     def double(self) -> Self:
1189:         r"""Casts all floating point parameters and buffers to ``double`` datatype.
1190: 
1191:         .. note::
1192:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1193: 
1194:         Returns:
1195:             Module: self
1196:         """
1197:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.double() if t.is_floating_point() else t)
1198: 
1199:     def half(self) -> Self:
1200:         r"""Casts all floating point parameters and buffers to ``half`` datatype.
1201: 
1202:         .. note::
1203:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1204: 
1205:         Returns:
1206:             Module: self
1207:         """
1208:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.half() if t.is_floating_point() else t)
1209: 
1210:     def bfloat16(self) -> Self:
1211:         r"""Casts all floating point parameters and buffers to ``bfloat16`` datatype.
1212: 
1213:         .. note::
1214:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1215: 
1216:         Returns:
1217:             Module: self
1218:         """
1219:         return self._apply(lambda t: t.bfloat16() if t.is_floating_point() else t)
1220: 
1221:     def to_empty(self, *, device: DeviceLikeType | None, recurse: bool = True) -> Self:
1222:         r"""Move the parameters and buffers to the specified device without copying storage.
1223: 
1224:         Args:
1225:             device (:class:`torch.device`): The desired device of the parameters
1226:                 and buffers in this module.
1227:             recurse (bool): Whether parameters and buffers of submodules should
1228:                 be recursively moved to the specified device.
1229: 
1230:         Returns:
1231:             Module: self
1232:         """
1233:         return self._apply(
1234:             lambda t: torch.empty_like(t, device=device), recurse=recurse
1235:         )
1236: 
1237:     @overload
1238:     def to(
1239:         self,
1240:         device: DeviceLikeType | None = ...,
1241:         dtype: dtype | None = ...,
1242:         non_blocking: bool = ...,
1243:     ) -> Self: ...
1244: 
1245:     @overload
1246:     def to(self, dtype: dtype, non_blocking: bool = ...) -> Self: ...
1247: 
1248:     @overload
1249:     def to(self, tensor: Tensor, non_blocking: bool = ...) -> Self: ...
1250: 
1251:     def to(self, *args, **kwargs):
1252:         r"""Move and/or cast the parameters and buffers.
1253: 
1254:         This can be called as
1255: 
1256:         .. function:: to(device=None, dtype=None, non_blocking=False)
1257:            :noindex:
1258: 
1259:         .. function:: to(dtype, non_blocking=False)
1260:            :noindex:
1261: 
1262:         .. function:: to(tensor, non_blocking=False)
1263:            :noindex:
1264: 
1265:         .. function:: to(memory_format=torch.channels_last)
1266:            :noindex:
1267: 
1268:         Its signature is similar to :meth:`torch.Tensor.to`, but only accepts
1269:         floating point or complex :attr:`dtype`\ s. In addition, this method will
1270:         only cast the floating point or complex parameters and buffers to :attr:`dtype`
1271:         (if given). The integral parameters and buffers will be moved
1272:         :attr:`device`, if that is given, but with dtypes unchanged. When
1273:         :attr:`non_blocking` is set, it tries to convert/move asynchronously
1274:         with respect to the host if possible, e.g., moving CPU Tensors with
1275:         pinned memory to CUDA devices.
1276: 
1277:         See below for examples.
1278: 
1279:         .. note::
1280:             This method modifies the module in-place.
1281: 
1282:         Args:
1283:             device (:class:`torch.device`): the desired device of the parameters
1284:                 and buffers in this module
1285:             dtype (:class:`torch.dtype`): the desired floating point or complex dtype of
1286:                 the parameters and buffers in this module
1287:             tensor (torch.Tensor): Tensor whose dtype and device are the desired
1288:                 dtype and device for all parameters and buffers in this module
1289:             memory_format (:class:`torch.memory_format`): the desired memory
1290:                 format for 4D parameters and buffers in this module (keyword
1291:                 only argument)
1292: 
1293:         Returns:
1294:             Module: self
1295: 
1296:         Examples::
1297: 
1298:             >>> # xdoctest: +IGNORE_WANT("non-deterministic")
1299:             >>> linear = nn.Linear(2, 2)
1300:             >>> linear.weight
1301:             Parameter containing:
1302:             tensor([[ 0.1913, -0.3420],
1303:                     [-0.5113, -0.2325]])
1304:             >>> linear.to(torch.double)
1305:             Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1306:             >>> linear.weight
1307:             Parameter containing:
1308:             tensor([[ 0.1913, -0.3420],
1309:                     [-0.5113, -0.2325]], dtype=torch.float64)
1310:             >>> # xdoctest: +REQUIRES(env:TORCH_DOCTEST_CUDA1)
1311:             >>> gpu1 = torch.device("cuda:1")
1312:             >>> linear.to(gpu1, dtype=torch.half, non_blocking=True)
1313:             Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1314:             >>> linear.weight
1315:             Parameter containing:
1316:             tensor([[ 0.1914, -0.3420],
1317:                     [-0.5112, -0.2324]], dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda:1')
1318:             >>> cpu = torch.device("cpu")
1319:             >>> linear.to(cpu)
1320:             Linear(in_features=2, out_features=2, bias=True)
1321:             >>> linear.weight
1322:             Parameter containing:
1323:             tensor([[ 0.1914, -0.3420],
1324:                     [-0.5112, -0.2324]], dtype=torch.float16)
1325: 
1326:             >>> linear = nn.Linear(2, 2, bias=None).to(torch.cdouble)
1327:             >>> linear.weight
1328:             Parameter containing:
1329:             tensor([[ 0.3741+0.j,  0.2382+0.j],
1330:                     [ 0.5593+0.j, -0.4443+0.j]], dtype=torch.complex128)
1331:             >>> linear(torch.ones(3, 2, dtype=torch.cdouble))
1332:             tensor([[0.6122+0.j, 0.1150+0.j],
1333:                     [0.6122+0.j, 0.1150+0.j],
1334:                     [0.6122+0.j, 0.1150+0.j]], dtype=torch.complex128)
1335: 
1336:         """
1337:         device, dtype, non_blocking, convert_to_format = torch._C._nn._parse_to(
1338:             # pyrefly: ignore [not-iterable]
1339:             *args,
1340:             **kwargs,
1341:         )
1342: 
1343:         if dtype is not None:
1344:             if not (dtype.is_floating_point or dtype.is_complex):
1345:                 raise TypeError(
1346:                     "nn.Module.to only accepts floating point or complex "
1347:                     f"dtypes, but got desired dtype={dtype}"
1348:                 )
1349:             if dtype.is_complex:
1350:                 warnings.warn(
1351:                     "Complex modules are a new feature under active development whose design may change, "
1352:                     "and some modules might not work as expected when using complex tensors as parameters or buffers. "
1353:                     "Please file an issue at https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/new?template=bug-report.yml "
1354:                     "if a complex module does not work as expected.",
1355:                     stacklevel=2,
1356:                 )
1357: 
1358:         def convert(t):
1359:             try:
1360:                 if convert_to_format is not None and t.dim() in (4, 5):
1361:                     return t.to(
1362:                         device,
1363:                         dtype if t.is_floating_point() or t.is_complex() else None,
1364:                         non_blocking,
1365:                         memory_format=convert_to_format,
1366:                     )
1367:                 return t.to(
1368:                     device,
1369:                     dtype if t.is_floating_point() or t.is_complex() else None,
1370:                     non_blocking,
1371:                 )
1372:             except NotImplementedError as e:
1373:                 if str(e) == "Cannot copy out of meta tensor; no data!":
1374:                     raise NotImplementedError(
1375:                         f"{e} Please use torch.nn.Module.to_empty() instead of torch.nn.Module.to() "
1376:                         f"when moving module from meta to a different device."
1377:                     ) from None
1378:                 else:
1379:                     raise
1380: 
1381:         return self._apply(convert)
1382: 
1383:     def register_full_backward_pre_hook(
1384:         self,
1385:         hook: Callable[["Module", _grad_t], None | _grad_t],
1386:         prepend: bool = False,
1387:     ) -> RemovableHandle:
1388:         r"""Register a backward pre-hook on the module.
1389: 
1390:         The hook will be called every time the gradients for the module are computed.
1391:         The hook should have the following signature::
1392: 
1393:             hook(module, grad_output) -> tuple[Tensor, ...], Tensor or None
1394: 
1395:         The :attr:`grad_output` is a tuple. The hook should
1396:         not modify its arguments, but it can optionally return a new gradient with
1397:         respect to the output that will be used in place of :attr:`grad_output` in
1398:         subsequent computations. Entries in :attr:`grad_output` will be ``None`` for
1399:         all non-Tensor arguments.
1400: 
1401:         For technical reasons, when this hook is applied to a Module, its forward function will
1402:         receive a view of each Tensor passed to the Module. Similarly the caller will receive a view
1403:         of each Tensor returned by the Module's forward function.
1404: 
1405:         .. warning ::
1406:             Modifying inputs inplace is not allowed when using backward hooks and
1407:             will raise an error.
1408: 
1409:         Args:
1410:             hook (Callable): The user-defined hook to be registered.
1411:             prepend (bool): If true, the provided ``hook`` will be fired before
1412:                 all existing ``backward_pre`` hooks on this
1413:                 :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Otherwise, the provided
1414:                 ``hook`` will be fired after all existing ``backward_pre`` hooks
1415:                 on this :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Note that global
1416:                 ``backward_pre`` hooks registered with
1417:                 :func:`register_module_full_backward_pre_hook` will fire before
1418:                 all hooks registered by this method.
1419: 
1420:         Returns:
1421:             :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
1422:                 a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
1423:                 ``handle.remove()``
1424: 
1425:         """
1426:         handle = RemovableHandle(self._backward_pre_hooks)
1427:         self._backward_pre_hooks[handle.id] = hook
1428:         if prepend:
1429:             self._backward_pre_hooks.move_to_end(handle.id, last=False)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
1430:         return handle
1431: 
1432:     def register_backward_hook(
1433:         self, hook: Callable[["Module", _grad_t, _grad_t], None | _grad_t]
1434:     ) -> RemovableHandle:
1435:         r"""Register a backward hook on the module.
1436: 
1437:         This function is deprecated in favor of :meth:`~torch.nn.Module.register_full_backward_hook` and
1438:         the behavior of this function will change in future versions.
1439: 
1440:         Returns:
1441:             :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
1442:                 a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
1443:                 ``handle.remove()``
1444: 
1445:         """
1446:         if self._is_full_backward_hook is True:
1447:             raise RuntimeError(
1448:                 "Cannot use both regular backward hooks and full backward hooks on a "
1449:                 "single Module. Please use only one of them."
1450:             )
1451: 
1452:         self._is_full_backward_hook = False
1453: 
1454:         handle = RemovableHandle(self._backward_hooks)
1455:         self._backward_hooks[handle.id] = hook
1456:         return handle
1457: 
1458:     def register_full_backward_hook(
1459:         self,
1460:         hook: Callable[["Module", _grad_t, _grad_t], None | _grad_t],
1461:         prepend: bool = False,
1462:     ) -> RemovableHandle:
1463:         r"""Register a backward hook on the module.
1464: 
1465:         The hook will be called every time the gradients with respect to a module are computed, and its firing rules are as follows:
1466: 
1467:             1. Ordinarily, the hook fires when the gradients are computed with respect to the module inputs.
1468:             2. If none of the module inputs require gradients, the hook will fire when the gradients are computed
1469:                with respect to module outputs.
1470:             3. If none of the module outputs require gradients, then the hooks will not fire.
1471: 
1472:         The hook should have the following signature::
1473: 
1474:             hook(module, grad_input, grad_output) -> tuple(Tensor) or None
1475: 
1476:         The :attr:`grad_input` and :attr:`grad_output` are tuples that contain the gradients
1477:         with respect to the inputs and outputs respectively. The hook should
1478:         not modify its arguments, but it can optionally return a new gradient with
1479:         respect to the input that will be used in place of :attr:`grad_input` in
1480:         subsequent computations. :attr:`grad_input` will only correspond to the inputs given
1481:         as positional arguments and all kwarg arguments are ignored. Entries
1482:         in :attr:`grad_input` and :attr:`grad_output` will be ``None`` for all non-Tensor
1483:         arguments.
1484: 
1485:         For technical reasons, when this hook is applied to a Module, its forward function will
1486:         receive a view of each Tensor passed to the Module. Similarly the caller will receive a view
1487:         of each Tensor returned by the Module's forward function.
1488: 
1489:         .. warning ::
1490:             Modifying inputs or outputs inplace is not allowed when using backward hooks and
1491:             will raise an error.
1492: 
1493:         Args:
1494:             hook (Callable): The user-defined hook to be registered.
1495:             prepend (bool): If true, the provided ``hook`` will be fired before
1496:                 all existing ``backward`` hooks on this
1497:                 :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Otherwise, the provided
1498:                 ``hook`` will be fired after all existing ``backward`` hooks on
1499:                 this :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Note that global
1500:                 ``backward`` hooks registered with
1501:                 :func:`register_module_full_backward_hook` will fire before
1502:                 all hooks registered by this method.
1503: 
1504:         Returns:
1505:             :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
1506:                 a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
1507:                 ``handle.remove()``
1508: 
1509:         """
1510:         if self._is_full_backward_hook is False:
1511:             raise RuntimeError(
1512:                 "Cannot use both regular backward hooks and full backward hooks on a "
1513:                 "single Module. Please use only one of them."
1514:             )
1515: 
1516:         self._is_full_backward_hook = True
1517: 
1518:         handle = RemovableHandle(self._backward_hooks)
1519:         self._backward_hooks[handle.id] = hook
1520:         if prepend:
1521:             self._backward_hooks.move_to_end(handle.id, last=False)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
1522:         return handle
1523: 
1524:     def _get_backward_hooks(self):
1525:         r"""Return the backward hooks for use in the call function.
1526: 
1527:         It returns two lists, one with the full backward hooks and one with the non-full
1528:         backward hooks.
1529:         """
1530:         full_backward_hooks: list[Callable] = []
1531:         if _global_is_full_backward_hook is True:
1532:             full_backward_hooks += _global_backward_hooks.values()
1533:         if self._is_full_backward_hook is True:
1534:             full_backward_hooks += self._backward_hooks.values()
1535: 
1536:         non_full_backward_hooks: list[Callable] = []
1537:         if _global_is_full_backward_hook is False:
1538:             non_full_backward_hooks += _global_backward_hooks.values()
1539:         if self._is_full_backward_hook is False:
1540:             non_full_backward_hooks += self._backward_hooks.values()
1541: 
1542:         return full_backward_hooks, non_full_backward_hooks
1543: 
1544:     def _get_backward_pre_hooks(self):
1545:         backward_pre_hooks: list[Callable] = []
1546:         backward_pre_hooks += _global_backward_pre_hooks.values()
1547:         backward_pre_hooks += self._backward_pre_hooks.values()
1548: 
1549:         return backward_pre_hooks
1550: 
1551:     def _maybe_warn_non_full_backward_hook(self, inputs, result, grad_fn) -> None:
1552:         if not isinstance(result, torch.Tensor):
1553:             if not (
1554:                 isinstance(result, tuple)
1555:                 and all(isinstance(r, torch.Tensor) for r in result)
1556:             ):
1557:                 warnings.warn(
1558:                     "Using non-full backward hooks on a Module that does not return a "
1559:                     "single Tensor or a tuple of Tensors is deprecated and will be removed "
1560:                     "in future versions. This hook will be missing some of the grad_output. "
1561:                     "Please use register_full_backward_hook to get the documented behavior.",
1562:                     FutureWarning,
1563:                     stacklevel=2,
1564:                 )
1565:                 return
1566:         else:
1567:             result = (result,)
1568: 
1569:         if not isinstance(inputs, torch.Tensor):
1570:             if not (
1571:                 isinstance(inputs, tuple)
1572:                 and all(isinstance(i, torch.Tensor) for i in inputs)
1573:             ):
1574:                 warnings.warn(
1575:                     "Using non-full backward hooks on a Module that does not take as input a "
1576:                     "single Tensor or a tuple of Tensors is deprecated and will be removed "
1577:                     "in future versions. This hook will be missing some of the grad_input. "
1578:                     "Please use register_full_backward_hook to get the documented behavior.",
1579:                     FutureWarning,
1580:                     stacklevel=2,
1581:                 )
1582:                 return
1583:         else:
1584:             inputs = (inputs,)
1585: 
1586:         # At this point we are sure that inputs and result are tuple of Tensors
1587:         out_grad_fn = {r.grad_fn for r in result if r.grad_fn is not None}
1588:         if len(out_grad_fn) == 0 or (
1589:             len(out_grad_fn) == 1 and grad_fn not in out_grad_fn
1590:         ):
1591:             warnings.warn(
1592:                 "Using a non-full backward hook when outputs are nested in python data structure "
1593:                 "is deprecated and will be removed in future versions. This hook will be missing "
1594:                 "some grad_output.",
1595:                 FutureWarning,
1596:                 stacklevel=2,
1597:             )
1598:         elif len(out_grad_fn) > 1:
1599:             warnings.warn(
1600:                 "Using a non-full backward hook when outputs are generated by different autograd Nodes "
1601:                 "is deprecated and will be removed in future versions. This hook will be missing "
1602:                 "some grad_output. Please use register_full_backward_hook to get the documented behavior.",
1603:                 FutureWarning,
1604:                 stacklevel=2,
1605:             )
1606:         else:
1607:             # At this point the grad_output part of the hook will most likely be correct
1608:             inputs_grad_fn = {i.grad_fn for i in inputs if i.grad_fn is not None}
1609: 
1610:             next_functions = {n[0] for n in grad_fn.next_functions}
1611: 
1612:             if inputs_grad_fn != next_functions:
1613:                 warnings.warn(
1614:                     "Using a non-full backward hook when the forward contains multiple autograd Nodes "
1615:                     "is deprecated and will be removed in future versions. This hook will be missing "
1616:                     "some grad_input. Please use register_full_backward_hook to get the documented "
1617:                     "behavior.",
1618:                     FutureWarning,
1619:                     stacklevel=2,
1620:                 )
1621: 
1622:     def register_forward_pre_hook(
1623:         self,
1624:         hook: Callable[[T, tuple[Any, ...]], Any | None]
1625:         | Callable[
1626:             [T, tuple[Any, ...], dict[str, Any]], tuple[Any, dict[str, Any]] | None
1627:         ],
1628:         *,
1629:         prepend: bool = False,
1630:         with_kwargs: bool = False,
1631:     ) -> RemovableHandle:
1632:         r"""Register a forward pre-hook on the module.
1633: 
1634:         The hook will be called every time before :func:`forward` is invoked.
1635: 
1636: 
1637:         If ``with_kwargs`` is false or not specified, the input contains only
1638:         the positional arguments given to the module. Keyword arguments won't be
1639:         passed to the hooks and only to the ``forward``. The hook can modify the
1640:         input. User can either return a tuple or a single modified value in the
1641:         hook. We will wrap the value into a tuple if a single value is returned
1642:         (unless that value is already a tuple). The hook should have the
1643:         following signature::
1644: 
1645:             hook(module, args) -> None or modified input
1646: 
1647:         If ``with_kwargs`` is true, the forward pre-hook will be passed the
1648:         kwargs given to the forward function. And if the hook modifies the
1649:         input, both the args and kwargs should be returned. The hook should have
1650:         the following signature::
1651: 
1652:             hook(module, args, kwargs) -> None or a tuple of modified input and kwargs
1653: 
1654:         Args:
1655:             hook (Callable): The user defined hook to be registered.
1656:             prepend (bool): If true, the provided ``hook`` will be fired before
1657:                 all existing ``forward_pre`` hooks on this
1658:                 :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Otherwise, the provided
1659:                 ``hook`` will be fired after all existing ``forward_pre`` hooks
1660:                 on this :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Note that global
1661:                 ``forward_pre`` hooks registered with
1662:                 :func:`register_module_forward_pre_hook` will fire before all
1663:                 hooks registered by this method.
1664:                 Default: ``False``
1665:             with_kwargs (bool): If true, the ``hook`` will be passed the kwargs
1666:                 given to the forward function.
1667:                 Default: ``False``
1668: 
1669:         Returns:
1670:             :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
1671:                 a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
1672:                 ``handle.remove()``
1673:         """
1674:         handle = RemovableHandle(
1675:             self._forward_pre_hooks, extra_dict=self._forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs
1676:         )
1677:         self._forward_pre_hooks[handle.id] = hook
1678:         if with_kwargs:
1679:             self._forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs[handle.id] = True
1680: 
1681:         if prepend:
1682:             self._forward_pre_hooks.move_to_end(handle.id, last=False)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
1683:         return handle
1684: 
1685:     def register_forward_hook(
1686:         self,
1687:         hook: Callable[[T, tuple[Any, ...], Any], Any | None]
1688:         | Callable[[T, tuple[Any, ...], dict[str, Any], Any], Any | None],
1689:         *,
1690:         prepend: bool = False,
1691:         with_kwargs: bool = False,
1692:         always_call: bool = False,
1693:     ) -> RemovableHandle:
1694:         r"""Register a forward hook on the module.
1695: 
1696:         The hook will be called every time after :func:`forward` has computed an output.
1697: 
1698:         If ``with_kwargs`` is ``False`` or not specified, the input contains only
1699:         the positional arguments given to the module. Keyword arguments won't be
1700:         passed to the hooks and only to the ``forward``. The hook can modify the
1701:         output. It can modify the input inplace but it will not have effect on
1702:         forward since this is called after :func:`forward` is called. The hook
1703:         should have the following signature::
1704: 
1705:             hook(module, args, output) -> None or modified output
1706: 
1707:         If ``with_kwargs`` is ``True``, the forward hook will be passed the
1708:         ``kwargs`` given to the forward function and be expected to return the
1709:         output possibly modified. The hook should have the following signature::
1710: 
1711:             hook(module, args, kwargs, output) -> None or modified output
1712: 
1713:         Args:
1714:             hook (Callable): The user defined hook to be registered.
1715:             prepend (bool): If ``True``, the provided ``hook`` will be fired
1716:                 before all existing ``forward`` hooks on this
1717:                 :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Otherwise, the provided
1718:                 ``hook`` will be fired after all existing ``forward`` hooks on
1719:                 this :class:`torch.nn.Module`. Note that global
1720:                 ``forward`` hooks registered with
1721:                 :func:`register_module_forward_hook` will fire before all hooks
1722:                 registered by this method.
1723:                 Default: ``False``
1724:             with_kwargs (bool): If ``True``, the ``hook`` will be passed the
1725:                 kwargs given to the forward function.
1726:                 Default: ``False``
1727:             always_call (bool): If ``True`` the ``hook`` will be run regardless of
1728:                 whether an exception is raised while calling the Module.
1729:                 Default: ``False``
1730: 
1731:         Returns:
1732:             :class:`torch.utils.hooks.RemovableHandle`:
1733:                 a handle that can be used to remove the added hook by calling
1734:                 ``handle.remove()``
1735:         """
1736:         handle = RemovableHandle(
1737:             self._forward_hooks,
1738:             extra_dict=[
1739:                 self._forward_hooks_with_kwargs,
1740:                 self._forward_hooks_always_called,
1741:             ],
1742:         )
1743:         self._forward_hooks[handle.id] = hook
1744:         if with_kwargs:
1745:             self._forward_hooks_with_kwargs[handle.id] = True
1746:         if always_call:
1747:             self._forward_hooks_always_called[handle.id] = True
1748:         if prepend:
1749:             self._forward_hooks.move_to_end(handle.id, last=False)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
1750:         return handle
1751: 
1752:     def _slow_forward(self, *input, **kwargs):
1753:         tracing_state = torch._C._get_tracing_state()
1754:         if not tracing_state or isinstance(self.forward, torch._C.ScriptMethod):
1755:             return self.forward(*input, **kwargs)
1756:         recording_scopes = torch.jit._trace._trace_module_map is not None
1757:         if recording_scopes:
1758:             # type ignore was added because at this point one knows that
1759:             # torch.jit._trace._trace_module_map is not Optional and has type Dict[Any, Any]
1760:             name = torch.jit._trace._trace_module_map.get(self, None)  # type: ignore[operator, union-attr]
1761:             if name:
1762:                 tracing_state.push_scope(name)
1763:             else:
1764:                 recording_scopes = False
1765:         try:
1766:             result = self.forward(*input, **kwargs)
1767:         finally:
1768:             if recording_scopes:
1769:                 tracing_state.pop_scope()
1770:         return result
1771: 
1772:     def _wrapped_call_impl(self, *args, **kwargs):
1773:         if self._compiled_call_impl is not None:
1774:             return self._compiled_call_impl(*args, **kwargs)  # type: ignore[misc]
1775:         else:
1776:             return self._call_impl(*args, **kwargs)
1777: 
1778:     # torchrec tests the code consistency with the following code
1779:     # fmt: off
1780:     def _call_impl(self, *args, **kwargs):
1781:         forward_call = (self._slow_forward if torch._C._get_tracing_state() else self.forward)
1782:         # If we don't have any hooks, we want to skip the rest of the logic in
1783:         # this function, and just call forward.
1784:         if not (self._backward_hooks or self._backward_pre_hooks or self._forward_hooks or self._forward_pre_hooks
1785:                 or _global_backward_pre_hooks or _global_backward_hooks
1786:                 or _global_forward_hooks or _global_forward_pre_hooks):
1787:             return forward_call(*args, **kwargs)
1788: 
1789:         result = None
1790:         called_always_called_hooks = set()
1791: 
1792:         def inner():
1793:             nonlocal result, args, kwargs
1794: 
1795:             full_backward_hooks, non_full_backward_hooks = [], []
1796:             backward_pre_hooks = []
1797:             if self._backward_pre_hooks or _global_backward_pre_hooks:
1798:                 backward_pre_hooks = self._get_backward_pre_hooks()
1799: 
1800:             if self._backward_hooks or _global_backward_hooks:
1801:                 full_backward_hooks, non_full_backward_hooks = self._get_backward_hooks()
1802: 
1803:             if _global_forward_pre_hooks or self._forward_pre_hooks:
1804:                 for hook_id, hook in (
1805:                     *_global_forward_pre_hooks.items(),
1806:                     *self._forward_pre_hooks.items(),
1807:                 ):
1808:                     if hook_id in self._forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs:
1809:                         args_kwargs_result = hook(self, args, kwargs)  # type: ignore[misc]
1810:                         if args_kwargs_result is not None:
1811:                             if isinstance(args_kwargs_result, tuple) and len(args_kwargs_result) == 2:
1812:                                 args, kwargs = args_kwargs_result
1813:                             else:
1814:                                 raise RuntimeError(
1815:                                     "forward pre-hook must return None or a tuple "
1816:                                     f"of (new_args, new_kwargs), but got {args_kwargs_result}."
1817:                                 )
1818:                     else:
1819:                         args_result = hook(self, args)
1820:                         if args_result is not None:
1821:                             if not isinstance(args_result, tuple):
1822:                                 args_result = (args_result,)
1823:                             args = args_result
1824: 
1825:             bw_hook = None
1826:             if full_backward_hooks or backward_pre_hooks:
1827:                 bw_hook = BackwardHook(self, full_backward_hooks, backward_pre_hooks)
1828:                 args = bw_hook.setup_input_hook(args)
1829: 
1830:             result = forward_call(*args, **kwargs)
1831:             if _global_forward_hooks or self._forward_hooks:
1832:                 for hook_id, hook in (
1833:                     *_global_forward_hooks.items(),
1834:                     *self._forward_hooks.items(),
1835:                 ):
1836:                     # mark that always called hook is run
1837:                     if hook_id in self._forward_hooks_always_called or hook_id in _global_forward_hooks_always_called:
1838:                         called_always_called_hooks.add(hook_id)
1839: 
1840:                     if hook_id in self._forward_hooks_with_kwargs or hook_id in _global_forward_hooks_with_kwargs:
1841:                         hook_result = hook(self, args, kwargs, result)
1842:                     else:
1843:                         hook_result = hook(self, args, result)
1844: 
1845:                     if hook_result is not None:
1846:                         result = hook_result
1847: 
1848:             if bw_hook:
1849:                 if not isinstance(result, (torch.Tensor, tuple)):
1850:                     warnings.warn("For backward hooks to be called,"
1851:                                   " module output should be a Tensor or a tuple of Tensors"
1852:                                   f" but received {type(result)}", stacklevel=2)
1853:                 result = bw_hook.setup_output_hook(result)
1854: 
1855:             # Handle the non-full backward hooks
1856:             if non_full_backward_hooks:
1857:                 var = result
1858:                 while not isinstance(var, torch.Tensor):
1859:                     if isinstance(var, dict):
1860:                         var = next(v for v in var.values() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor))
1861:                     else:
1862:                         var = var[0]
1863:                 grad_fn = var.grad_fn
1864:                 if grad_fn is not None:
1865:                     for hook in non_full_backward_hooks:
1866:                         grad_fn.register_hook(_WrappedHook(hook, self))
1867:                     self._maybe_warn_non_full_backward_hook(args, result, grad_fn)
1868: 
1869:             return result
1870: 
1871:         # This is technically not behavior equivalent when compiling, but it's
1872:         # incredibly unlikely we will ever support throwing an exception in NN
1873:         # module, and then catching it here, and then reraising it, and then
1874:         # catching it again, and expecting the resulting frame to be compiled.
1875:         # The reraise here just gunks up our exception handling for no good
1876:         # reason.  Don't try to run the always called hooks in event of
1877:         # exception.
1878:         if torch.compiler.is_compiling():
1879:             return inner()
1880: 
1881:         try:
1882:             return inner()
1883:         except Exception:
1884:             # run always called hooks if they have not already been run
1885:             # For now only forward hooks have the always_call option but perhaps
1886:             # this functionality should be added to full backward hooks as well.
1887:             for hook_id, hook in _global_forward_hooks.items():
1888:                 if hook_id in _global_forward_hooks_always_called and hook_id not in called_always_called_hooks:  # type: ignore[possibly-undefined]
1889:                     try:
1890:                         hook_result = hook(self, args, result)  # type: ignore[possibly-undefined]
1891:                         if hook_result is not None:
1892:                             result = hook_result
1893:                     except Exception as e:
1894:                         warnings.warn("global module forward hook with ``always_call=True`` raised an exception "
1895:                                       f"that was silenced as another error was raised in forward: {str(e)}", stacklevel=2)
1896:                         continue
1897: 
1898:             for hook_id, hook in self._forward_hooks.items():
1899:                 if hook_id in self._forward_hooks_always_called and hook_id not in called_always_called_hooks:  # type: ignore[possibly-undefined]
1900:                     try:
1901:                         if hook_id in self._forward_hooks_with_kwargs:
1902:                             hook_result = hook(self, args, kwargs, result)  # type: ignore[possibly-undefined]
1903:                         else:
1904:                             hook_result = hook(self, args, result)  # type: ignore[possibly-undefined]
1905:                         if hook_result is not None:
1906:                             result = hook_result
1907:                     except Exception as e:
1908:                         warnings.warn("module forward hook with ``always_call=True`` raised an exception "
1909:                                       f"that was silenced as another error was raised in forward: {str(e)}", stacklevel=2)
1910:                         continue
1911:             # raise exception raised in try block
1912:             raise
1913:     # fmt: on
1914: 
1915:     __call__: Callable[..., Any] = _wrapped_call_impl
1916: 
1917:     def __getstate__(self):
1918:         state = self.__dict__.copy()
1919:         state.pop("_compiled_call_impl", None)
1920:         return state
1921: 
1922:     def __setstate__(self, state):
1923:         self.__dict__.update(state)
1924: 
1925:         # Support loading old checkpoints that don't have the following attrs:
1926:         if "_forward_pre_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1927:             self._forward_pre_hooks = OrderedDict()
1928:         if "_forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs" not in self.__dict__:
1929:             self._forward_pre_hooks_with_kwargs = OrderedDict()
1930:         if "_forward_hooks_with_kwargs" not in self.__dict__:
1931:             self._forward_hooks_with_kwargs = OrderedDict()
1932:         if "_forward_hooks_always_called" not in self.__dict__:
1933:             self._forward_hooks_always_called = OrderedDict()
1934:         if "_state_dict_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1935:             self._state_dict_hooks = OrderedDict()
1936:         if "_state_dict_pre_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1937:             self._state_dict_pre_hooks = OrderedDict()
1938:         if "_load_state_dict_pre_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1939:             self._load_state_dict_pre_hooks = OrderedDict()
1940:         if "_load_state_dict_post_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1941:             self._load_state_dict_post_hooks = OrderedDict()
1942:         if "_non_persistent_buffers_set" not in self.__dict__:
1943:             self._non_persistent_buffers_set = set()
1944:         if "_is_full_backward_hook" not in self.__dict__:
1945:             self._is_full_backward_hook = None
1946:         if "_backward_pre_hooks" not in self.__dict__:
1947:             self._backward_pre_hooks = OrderedDict()
1948: 
1949:     # It is crucial that the return type is not annotated as `Any`, otherwise type checking
1950:     # on `torch.nn.Module` and all its subclasses is largely disabled as a result. See:
1951:     # https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/pull/115074
1952:     def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> Union[Tensor, "Module"]:
1953:         if "_parameters" in self.__dict__:
1954:             _parameters = self.__dict__["_parameters"]
1955:             if name in _parameters:
1956:                 return _parameters[name]
1957:         if "_buffers" in self.__dict__:
1958:             _buffers = self.__dict__["_buffers"]
1959:             if name in _buffers:
1960:                 return _buffers[name]
1961:         if "_modules" in self.__dict__:
1962:             modules = self.__dict__["_modules"]
1963:             if name in modules:
1964:                 return modules[name]
1965:         raise AttributeError(
1966:             f"'{type(self).__name__}' object has no attribute '{name}'"
1967:         )
1968: 
1969:     def __setattr__(self, name: str, value: Union[Tensor, "Module"]) -> None:
1970:         def remove_from(*dicts_or_sets) -> None:
1971:             for d in dicts_or_sets:
1972:                 if name in d:
1973:                     if isinstance(d, dict):
1974:                         del d[name]
1975:                     else:
1976:                         d.discard(name)
1977: 
1978:         params = self.__dict__.get("_parameters")
1979:         if isinstance(value, Parameter):
1980:             if params is None:
1981:                 raise AttributeError(
1982:                     "cannot assign parameters before Module.__init__() call"
1983:                 )
1984:             remove_from(
1985:                 self.__dict__,
1986:                 self._buffers,
1987:                 self._modules,
1988:                 self._non_persistent_buffers_set,
1989:             )
1990:             self.register_parameter(name, value)
1991:         elif params is not None and name in params:
1992:             if value is not None:
1993:                 raise TypeError(
1994:                     f"cannot assign '{torch.typename(value)}' as parameter '{name}' "
1995:                     "(torch.nn.Parameter or None expected)"
1996:                 )
1997:             self.register_parameter(name, value)
1998:         else:
1999:             modules = self.__dict__.get("_modules")
2000:             if isinstance(value, Module):
2001:                 if modules is None:
2002:                     raise AttributeError(
2003:                         "cannot assign module before Module.__init__() call"
2004:                     )
2005:                 remove_from(
2006:                     self.__dict__,
2007:                     self._parameters,
2008:                     self._buffers,
2009:                     self._non_persistent_buffers_set,
2010:                 )
2011:                 for hook in _global_module_registration_hooks.values():
2012:                     output = hook(self, name, value)
2013:                     if output is not None:
2014:                         value = output
2015:                 modules[name] = value
2016:             elif modules is not None and name in modules:
2017:                 if value is not None:
2018:                     raise TypeError(
2019:                         f"cannot assign '{torch.typename(value)}' as child module '{name}' "
2020:                         "(torch.nn.Module or None expected)"
2021:                     )
2022:                 for hook in _global_module_registration_hooks.values():
2023:                     output = hook(self, name, value)
2024:                     if output is not None:
2025:                         value = output
2026:                 modules[name] = value
2027:             else:
2028:                 buffers = self.__dict__.get("_buffers")
2029:                 if isinstance(value, Buffer) or buffers is not None and name in buffers:
2030:                     if value is not None and not (
2031:                         isinstance(value, torch.Tensor)
2032:                         or hasattr(value, "__torch_function__")
2033:                     ):
2034:                         raise TypeError(
2035:                             f"cannot assign '{torch.typename(value)}' as buffer '{name}' "
2036:                             "(torch.nn.Buffer, torch.Tensor or None expected)"
2037:                         )
2038:                     if isinstance(value, Buffer):
2039:                         persistent = value.persistent
2040:                     else:
2041:                         persistent = name not in self._non_persistent_buffers_set
2042:                     # === HACK ===
2043:                     # This whole block below should just be:
2044:                     # self.register_buffer(name, value, persistent)
2045: 
2046:                     # But to support subclasses of nn.Module that (wrongfully) implement a
2047:                     # register_buffer() method that doesn't have the "persistent"
2048:                     # argument. Only pass it in if it is accepted otherwise assume
2049:                     # it is always true
2050:                     if (
2051:                         getattr(self.register_buffer, "__func__", None)
2052:                         is torch.nn.Module.register_buffer
2053:                     ):
2054:                         self.register_buffer(name, value, persistent)
2055:                     else:
2056:                         sign = inspect.signature(self.register_buffer)
2057:                         if "persistent" in sign.parameters:
2058:                             self.register_buffer(name, value, persistent)
2059:                         else:
2060:                             if not persistent:
2061:                                 raise RuntimeError(
2062:                                     "Registering a non-persistent buffer "
2063:                                     "on a Module subclass that implements "
2064:                                     "register_buffer() without the persistent "
2065:                                     "argument is not allowed."
2066:                                 )
2067:                             # Assume that the implementation without the argument has the
2068:                             # behavior from before the argument was added: persistent=True
2069:                             self.register_buffer(name, value)
2070:                     # === HACK END ===
2071:                 else:
2072:                     super().__setattr__(name, value)
2073: 
2074:     def __delattr__(self, name) -> None:
2075:         if name in self._parameters:
2076:             del self._parameters[name]
2077:         elif name in self._buffers:
2078:             del self._buffers[name]
2079:             self._non_persistent_buffers_set.discard(name)
2080:         elif name in self._modules:
2081:             del self._modules[name]
2082:         else:
2083:             super().__delattr__(name)
2084: 
2085:     def _register_state_dict_hook(self, hook):
2086:         r"""Register a post-hook for the :meth:`~torch.nn.Module.state_dict` method.
2087: 
2088:         It should have the following signature::
2089:             hook(module, state_dict, prefix, local_metadata) -> None or state_dict
2090: 
2091:         The registered hooks can modify the ``state_dict`` inplace or return a new one.
2092:         If a new ``state_dict`` is returned, it will only be respected if it is the root
