1354:         @register(torch.set_default_device)
1355:         def handle_set_default_device(
1356:             self, tx: "InstructionTranslator", *args, **kwargs
1357:         ):
1358:             # Today this is inserted in the graph, once TF mode
1359:             # handling is complete, we can trace the device context
1360:             # like any other TF mode and remove this special handling
1361:             # Insert the TF mode representing the device context at
1362:             # the bottom of the stack to match the eager semantics
1363:             # Running the graph will ensure that the DeviceContext mode is
1364:             # at the correct position in the stack
1365:             TorchFunctionModeStackVariable.register_mutation(tx)
1366:             if args[0].is_constant_none():
1367:                 TorchFunctionModeStackVariable.clear_default_device(tx)
1368:             else:
1369:                 TorchFunctionModeStackVariable.register_device_context_insertion(tx)
1370: 
1371:             return ConstantVariable.create(None)
1372: 
1373:         @register(torch._check)
1374:         def handle_check(self, tx: "InstructionTranslator", *args, **kwargs):
1375:             predicate_vt = None
1376:             message_vt = None
1377: 
1378:             if args:
1379:                 predicate_vt = args[0]
1380:                 rest_args = args[1:]
1381:             else:
1382:                 rest_args = ()
1383: 
1384:             if predicate_vt is None and "cond" in kwargs:
1385:                 predicate_vt = kwargs.pop("cond")
1386: 
1387:             if rest_args:
1388:                 message_vt = rest_args[0]
1389:             elif "message" in kwargs:
1390:                 message_vt = kwargs.pop("message")
1391: 
1392:             if predicate_vt is None:
1393:                 return wrap_fx_proxy(
1394:                     tx=tx,
1395:                     proxy=tx.output.create_proxy(
1396:                         "call_function",
1397:                         self.value,
1398:                         (),
1399:                         {},
1400:                     ),
1401:                 )
1402: 
1403:             message_eager = None
1404:             message_graph_proxy = None
1405:             if message_vt is not None:
1406:                 if (
1407:                     not isinstance(message_vt, NestedUserFunctionVariable)
1408:                     or message_vt.has_closure()
1409:                 ):
1410:                     unimplemented(
1411:                         gb_type="Can't extract message from torch._check()",
1412:                         context=str(message_vt),
1413:                         explanation=(
1414:                             "The second argument of torch._check() must be a function"
1415:                             "defined within the torch.compile region"
1416:                             "that does not reference a non-local variable."
1417:                         ),
1418:                         hints=[
1419:                             "Make sure the message function is defined in the torch.compile region.",
1420:                             "Remove any closure variables, e.g. "
1421:                             "remove references to closure variable `x` in `lambda: f'{x} failed check'`",
1422:                             *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1423:                         ],
1424:                     )
1425:                 message_eager = message_vt.get_function()
1426: 
1427:                 message_graph_proxy = tx.output.register_static_attr_and_return_proxy(
1428:                     "_check_message", message_eager
1429:                 )
1430: 
1431:             if predicate_vt.is_python_constant():
1432:                 self.value(predicate_vt.as_python_constant(), message_eager)
1433:                 return ConstantVariable.create(None)
1434: 
1435:             predicate_proxy = predicate_vt.as_proxy()
1436: 
1437:             proxy_args: tuple[Any, ...]
1438:             if message_graph_proxy is None:
1439:                 proxy_args = (predicate_proxy,)
1440:             else:
1441:                 proxy_args = (predicate_proxy, message_graph_proxy)
1442: 
1443:             return wrap_fx_proxy(
1444:                 tx=tx,
1445:                 proxy=tx.output.create_proxy(
1446:                     "call_function",
1447:                     self.value,
1448:                     proxy_args,
1449:                     {},
1450:                 ),
1451:             )
1452: 
1453:         return handlers
1454: 
1455:     def call_function(
1456:         self,
1457:         tx: "InstructionTranslator",
1458:         args: Sequence[VariableTracker],
1459:         kwargs: "dict[str, VariableTracker]",
1460:     ) -> "VariableTracker":
1461:         from . import ConstantVariable, SymNodeVariable
1462:         from .builder import wrap_fx_proxy
1463: 
1464:         if self.nonstrict_traceable:
1465:             return self._call_nonstrict_traceable_function(tx, args, kwargs)
1466: 
1467:         if self.torch_function_override_enabled(tx, args, kwargs):
1468:             return dispatch_torch_function(tx, self, args, kwargs)
1469: 
1470:         if self.can_constant_fold_through() and check_unspec_or_constant_args(
1471:             args, kwargs
1472:         ):
1473:             # constant fold functions need to be guarded.
1474:             if self.value in constant_fold_functions_need_guards:
1475:                 assert self.source is not None
1476:                 source = CallFunctionNoArgsSource(self.source)
1477:                 install_guard(source.make_guard(GuardBuilder.EQUALS_MATCH))
1478:             # constant fold
1479:             try:
1480:                 return ConstantVariable.create(
1481:                     self.as_python_constant()(
1482:                         *[x.as_python_constant() for x in args],
1483:                         **{k: v.as_python_constant() for k, v in kwargs.items()},
1484:                     ),
1485:                 )
1486:             except (OverflowError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
1487:                 raise_observed_exception(
1488:                     type(exc),
1489:                     tx,
1490:                     args=list(map(ConstantVariable.create, exc.args)),
1491:                 )
1492: 
1493:         if self.is_tensor_method():
1494:             name = self.value.__name__
1495:             # Guard against inplace view op on input tensor (not supported)
1496:             if args and args[0].is_tensor():
1497:                 tensor_var = args[0]
1498:                 # Check if input tensor and inplace_view op specifically
1499:                 if tensor_var.source is not None and hasattr(torch.ops.aten, name):
1500:                     fn = getattr(torch.ops.aten, name)
1501:                     if (
1502:                         hasattr(fn, "overloads")
1503:                         and hasattr(fn, fn.overloads()[0])
1504:                         and torch.Tag.inplace_view
1505:                         in getattr(fn, fn.overloads()[0]).tags
1506:                     ):
1507:                         unimplemented(
1508:                             gb_type="Inplace op on input tensor",
1509:                             context="",
1510:                             explanation=f"Attempted to trace an inplace view op on input tensor {typestr(self.value)}.",
1511:                             hints=[
1512:                                 *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1513:                                 "Ensure you do not modify input tensor in place.",
1514:                             ],
1515:                         )
1516:             return self.call_tensor_method(tx, args, kwargs)
1517: 
1518:         special_handler = self._get_handlers().get(self.value)
1519:         if special_handler:
1520:             result = special_handler(self, tx, *args, **kwargs)
1521:             if result:
1522:                 return result
1523: 
1524:         any_symints_or_symfloats = any(isinstance(x, SymNodeVariable) for x in args)
1525: 
1526:         all_ints_or_floats = all(
1527:             isinstance(x, SymNodeVariable) or x.is_python_constant() for x in args
1528:         )
1529:         if (
1530:             getattr(self.value, "__module__", "") == "torch"
1531:             and self.value.__name__ in bin_ops
1532:             and any_symints_or_symfloats
1533:             and all_ints_or_floats
1534:         ):
1535:             msg = f"""\
1536: Calling {str(self.value)} on only torch.SymInt arguments is not yet supported.
1537: To support this behavior, we need to allow const-propping tensors that store symint data.
1538: For now, dynamo will explicitly graph break when it encounters user code with this behavior.
1539: """
1540:             log.warning(msg)
1541:             unimplemented(
1542:                 gb_type="Attempted to call torch in-graph function on only torch.SymInt arguments",
1543:                 context=f"fn={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1544:                 explanation=(
1545:                     f"Attempted to call {str(self.value)} (that should be put in the FX graph) on only torch.SymInt arguments. "
1546:                     "Dynamo does not support this."
1547:                 ),
1548:                 hints=[
1549:                     *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1550:                 ],
1551:             )
1552: 
1553:         # TODO(voz): Replace w/ dynamic shape rewrite table.
1554:         # Ideally, we would be able to do this at ctor time, but alas we need a combination
1555:         # of value + args to determine this.
1556:         fn_ = self.value
1557:         if any_symints_or_symfloats:
1558:             torch_sym_op = f"_sym_{self.value.__name__}"
1559:             if getattr(self.value, "__module__", None) == "math" and hasattr(
1560:                 torch, torch_sym_op
1561:             ):
1562:                 fn_ = getattr(torch, torch_sym_op)
1563: 
1564:         # TODO for each of the following check on `out=` or `requires_grad=`
1565:         # variant torch ops, the original function could come from a user
1566:         # defined `@allow_in_graph` function as well, which doesn't have the
1567:         # same semantics as the torch ops.
1568: 
1569:         # Calling fake tensor propagation can mutate the out= tensor in
1570:         # tx.output.tracked_fakes. tracked_fakes are used to apply
1571:         # symbolic_shape guards. Mutating them destroys the information
1572:         # prior to tracing, which is essential for creating right
1573:         # guards. So save the shape now, and check later if it has
1574:         # changed. If it has, graph break.
1575:         saved_out_shapes = None
1576:         out_kwarg_vt = None
1577:         if "out" in kwargs:
1578:             out_kwarg_vt = kwargs["out"]
1579: 
1580:             # e.g., out=(t1, t2, ...)
1581:             if isinstance(out_kwarg_vt, (TupleVariable, ListVariable)):
1582:                 saved_out_shapes = []
1583:                 for vt in out_kwarg_vt.items:
1584:                     if vt.is_tensor():
1585:                         shape = vt.as_proxy().node.meta["example_value"].shape
1586:                     else:
1587:                         shape = None
1588:                     saved_out_shapes.append(shape)
1589: 
1590:             # e.g., out=output_tensor
1591:             if out_kwarg_vt.is_tensor():
1592:                 saved_out_shapes = (
1593:                     out_kwarg_vt.as_proxy().node.meta["example_value"].shape
1594:                 )
1595: 
1596:         tensor_variable = wrap_fx_proxy(
1597:             tx=tx,
1598:             proxy=tx.output.create_proxy(
1599:                 "call_function",
1600:                 fn_,
1601:                 *proxy_args_kwargs(args, kwargs),
1602:             ),
1603:         )
1604: 
1605:         # Handle e.g., `torch.ones(10, requires_grad=True)`
1606:         if (
1607:             tensor_variable.is_tensor()
1608:             and "requires_grad" in kwargs
1609:             and kwargs["requires_grad"].as_python_constant()
1610:         ):
1611:             unimplemented(
1612:                 gb_type="Attempted to use tensor creation function with requires_grad=True",
1613:                 context=f"fn={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1614:                 explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1615:                 hints=[
1616:                     "Create the tensor outside the compiled region.",
1617:                     "Do not set `requires_grad=True`.",
1618:                     *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1619:                 ],
1620:             )
1621: 
1622:         # Handle e.g., `torch.add(a, b, out=result)`
1623:         if saved_out_shapes is not None:
1624:             # out variants of torch operators like torch.sort and torch.sigmoid
1625:             # mutate the tensors in the out field.
1626:             #
1627:             # However, it's non-trivial to update all references of the old
1628:             # `TensorVariable` to the new one returned (`result_var`), so we
1629:             # take the conservative approach to graph break on size changes, and
1630:             # assume other cases can fall through soundly.
1631:             #
1632:             # Note that although these tensor variables would hold different
1633:             # proxies, the in-place mutation semantics is preserved in the FX
1634:             # graph, so we won't have correctness issues.
1635:             if isinstance(saved_out_shapes, list):
1636:                 for out_tensor_vt, saved_out_shape in zip(
1637:                     out_kwarg_vt.items,  # type: ignore[union-attr]
1638:                     saved_out_shapes,
1639:                 ):
1640:                     if saved_out_shape is None:
1641:                         # This should be extremely rare, but it's kept for now
1642:                         # until we invest in enforcing the `out=` kwarg for only
1643:                         # torch methods.
1644:                         continue
1645: 
1646:                     assert out_tensor_vt.is_tensor()
1647:                     fake_out = out_tensor_vt.proxy.node.meta["example_value"]
1648:                     if saved_out_shape != fake_out.shape:
1649:                         # It's hard to get out variants with resizing on graph inputs work
1650:                         # properly across dynamo/aot/inductor, just fall back.
1651:                         unimplemented(
1652:                             gb_type="Shape mismatch with out= list of tensor variants",
1653:                             context=f"fn={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1654:                             explanation=(
1655:                                 f"Shape mismatch when calling {self.value} with `out=`. "
1656:                                 f"Provided `out=` shape: {saved_out_shape}. Actual shape: {fake_out.shape}."
1657:                             ),
1658:                             hints=[
1659:                                 *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1660:                             ],
1661:                         )
1662:                     if not torch._prims_common.is_contiguous(fake_out):
1663:                         # It's difficult to handle strides correctly in functionalization
1664:                         # when calling an out= op with a non-contiguous out argument
1665:                         unimplemented(
1666:                             gb_type="Attempted to call op with non-contiguous `out=` list of tensors",
1667:                             context=f"self.value={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1668:                             explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1669:                             hints=[
1670:                                 *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1671:                             ],
1672:                         )
1673:             else:
1674:                 assert out_kwarg_vt is not None and out_kwarg_vt.is_tensor()
1675:                 assert "example_value" in out_kwarg_vt.as_proxy().node.meta
1676:                 fake_out = out_kwarg_vt.as_proxy().node.meta["example_value"]
1677:                 if saved_out_shapes != fake_out.shape:
1678:                     # It's hard to get out variants with resizing on graph inputs work
1679:                     # properly across dynamo/aot/inductor, just fall back.
1680:                     unimplemented(
1681:                         gb_type="Shape mismatch with out= tensor variant",
1682:                         context=f"fn={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1683:                         explanation=(
1684:                             f"Shape mismatch when calling {self.value} with `out=`. "
1685:                             f"Provided `out=` shape: {saved_out_shapes}. Actual shape: {fake_out.shape}."
1686:                         ),
1687:                         hints=[
1688:                             *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1689:                         ],
1690:                     )
1691:                 if not torch._prims_common.is_contiguous_or_false(fake_out):
1692:                     # It's difficult to handle strides correctly in functionalization
1693:                     # when calling an out= op with a non-contiguous out argument
1694:                     unimplemented(
1695:                         gb_type="Attempted to call op with non-contiguous `out=` tensor",
1696:                         context=f"self.value={self.value}, args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}",
1697:                         explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1698:                         hints=[
1699:                             *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1700:                         ],
1701:                     )
1702: 
1703:         return tensor_variable
1704: 
1705:     def _call_nonstrict_traceable_function(
1706:         self,
1707:         tx: "InstructionTranslator",
1708:         args: Sequence[VariableTracker],
1709:         kwargs: "dict[str, VariableTracker]",
1710:     ) -> "VariableTracker":
1711:         import torch._higher_order_ops.flat_apply as flat_apply
1712:         from torch._higher_order_ops.flat_apply import (
1713:             func_to_graphable,
1714:             is_graphable_type,
1715:         )
1716:         from torch._subclasses.fake_tensor import fake_tensor_tls
1717:         from torch.utils._pytree import tree_flatten
1718: 
1719:         from .base import AsPythonConstantNotImplementedError
1720:         from .builder import wrap_fx_proxy
1721: 
1722:         # 1. Convert `args, kwargs` into pytree-flattened proxy forms.
1723:         #
1724:         # Rather than reconstructing `args, kwargs` into python objects and
1725:         # then tree_flatten them, we just let Dynamo symbolically interpret
1726:         # `tree_flatten((args, kwargs))`. This saves us from having to
1727:         # worry about the reconstruction logic, side effects, and guards.
1728:         packed_input_vt = TupleVariable.build(
1729:             tx, (TupleVariable.build(tx, args), ConstDictVariable.build(tx, kwargs))
1730:         )
1731:         out_vt = variables.UserFunctionVariable(tree_flatten).call_function(  # type: ignore[arg-type]
1732:             tx, [packed_input_vt], {}
1733:         )
1734:         assert isinstance(out_vt, TupleVariable) and len(out_vt.items) == 2
1735:         flat_args_vts, input_spec_vt = out_vt.items
1736:         assert isinstance(flat_args_vts, ListVariable)
1737: 
1738:         # Handle the case when the input contains a non-graphable type.
1739:         for flat_arg_vt in flat_args_vts.items:
1740:             arg_type = flat_arg_vt.python_type()
1741:             if not is_graphable_type(arg_type):
1742:                 type_name = flat_arg_vt.python_type().__qualname__
1743:                 unimplemented(
1744:                     gb_type="Invalid input type for nonstrict_trace-ed function",
1745:                     context=f"Encountered input of type <{type_name}>.",
1746:                     explanation=(
1747:                         "For `nonstrict_trace`-ed functions, only basic types (e.g., torch.Tensor, int, float) "
1748:                         "or pytree containers of those are allowed as inputs. The provided argument contains "
1749:                         "an unsupported type."
1750:                     ),
1751:                     hints=[
1752:                         "Use one of the following to register the type with pytree:\n"
1753:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_constant`\n"
1754:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_dataclass`\n"
1755:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_pytree_node`",
1756:                     ],
1757:                 )
1758: 
1759:         # Since we checked with `is_graphable` above, `as_proxy` on the
1760:         # flat_arg VT should always work.
1761:         proxified_flat_args = [
1762:             flat_arg_vt.as_proxy() for flat_arg_vt in flat_args_vts.items
1763:         ]
1764: 
1765:         # The downstream `flat_apply` call requires the input spec; however,
1766:         # the spec not a graphable type, so we still have to reconstruct it
1767:         # into a python object, and store it as a constant attribute on the
1768:         # fx graph.
1769:         try:
1770:             input_spec = input_spec_vt.as_python_constant()
1771:         except AsPythonConstantNotImplementedError as e:
1772:             typ = e.vt.python_type()
1773:             type_name = typ.__qualname__
1774:             import torch.utils._pytree as pytree
1775: 
1776:             if pytree.is_constant_class(typ):
1777:                 unimplemented(
1778:                     gb_type="Input marked with `pytree.register_constant` constructed in the `torch.compile` region",
1779:                     context=f"Input={input_spec_vt}, offending type <{type_name}>.",
1780:                     explanation=(
1781:                         "Calling a `nonstrict_trace`-ed function with an input that contains an object "
1782:                         f"of type <{type_name}>, which was marked with `pytree.register_constant`. However, the object "
1783:                         "was constructed _inside_ the `torch.compile` region. This is not supported."
1784:                     ),
1785:                     hints=[
1786:                         "Construct the object _outside_ the `torch.compile` region, or submit an issue to GitHub.",
1787:                         *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1788:                     ],
1789:                     from_exc=e,
1790:                 )
1791:             else:
1792:                 unimplemented(
1793:                     gb_type="Invalid use of pytree_flatten with nonstrict_trace-ed function",
1794:                     context=f"Input={input_spec_vt}, offending type <{type_name}>.",
1795:                     explanation=(
1796:                         "Calling a `nonstrict_trace`-ed function where one of the inputs has been registered "
1797:                         f"with a `pytree_flatten` that places an object of type <{type_name}> into the context."
1798:                     ),
1799:                     hints=[
1800:                         "Modifying the `pytree_flatten` to avoid placing the object into the context.",
1801:                         f"Apply one of the following to <{type_name}>:\n"
1802:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_constant`\n"
1803:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_dataclass`\n"
1804:                         "* `torch.utils._pytree.register_pytree_node`",
1805:                         *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1806:                     ],
1807:                     from_exc=e,
1808:                 )
1809: 
1810:         fn = self.value
1811: 
1812:         def patched_fn(*args, **kwargs):
1813:             # This enables reads to global/captured tensors, and we'll just
1814:             # treat them as constants in the graph. Note that after
1815:             # AOTDispatcher, this logic would disappear.
1816:             old_val = fake_tensor_tls.allow_non_fake_inputs_override
1817:             fake_tensor_tls.allow_non_fake_inputs_override = True
1818:             try:
1819:                 res = fn(*args, **kwargs)
1820:             finally:  # reset even when `fn` raises
1821:                 fake_tensor_tls.allow_non_fake_inputs_override = old_val
1822:             return res
1823: 
1824:         # `flat_apply` wants a TreeSpec for the function input.
1825:         _, f_spec = func_to_graphable(patched_fn)
1826: 
1827:         # TreeSpec isn't graphable, so we register the function and input
1828:         # specs as attributes on the graph module.
1829:         f_spec_proxy = tx.output.register_static_attr_and_return_proxy(
1830:             f"{fn.__name__}_spec", f_spec
1831:         )
1832:         input_spec_proxy = tx.output.register_static_attr_and_return_proxy(
1833:             fn.__name__ + "_input_spec",
1834:             # pyrefly: ignore [unbound-name]
1835:             input_spec,
1836:         )
1837:         f_spec_proxy.node.type = type(f_spec)
1838:         # pyrefly: ignore [unbound-name]
1839:         input_spec_proxy.node.type = type(input_spec)
1840:         all_args = (f_spec_proxy, input_spec_proxy, *proxified_flat_args)
1841: 
1842:         # 2. Create a proxy call to `flat_apply`, then fake-tensor propagate
1843:         # the call and wrap output into a VariableTracker.
1844:         proxy = tx.output.create_proxy("call_function", flat_apply, all_args, {})
1845:         try:
1846:             # TODO support more output types once `flat_apply` supports
1847:             # pytree-able output types. We can have Dynamo trace through an
1848:             # unflatten call (just like we traced through a flatten above)
1849:             # to rebuild the actual output VT.
1850:             out_vt = wrap_fx_proxy(tx, proxy)
1851:         except (
1852:             # From `handle_traced_output`.
1853:             torch._dynamo.exc.Unsupported,
1854:             # From `flat_apply` assert on output type.
1855:             torch._dynamo.exc.TorchRuntimeError,
1856:         ):
1857:             unimplemented(
1858:                 gb_type="Unsupported output type for nonstrict_trace-ed function",
1859:                 context=f"Function: {fn.__name__}",
1860:                 explanation=(
1861:                     "For `nonstrict_trace`-ed functions, only basic types (e.g., torch.Tensor, int, list)"
1862:                     " are allowed as output. The result of this call contains an unsupported type."
1863:                 ),
1864:                 hints=[*graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE],
1865:             )
1866: 
1867:         return out_vt
1868: 
1869:     def _call_ntuple(self, tx: "InstructionTranslator", args, kwargs):
1870:         """inline behavior of torch.nn.modules.utils._ntuple"""
1871:         if self.value is torch.nn.modules.utils._ntuple:
1872:             count = args[0].as_python_constant()
1873:         else:
1874:             count = self.value.__closure__[0].cell_contents
1875:         assert isinstance(count, int)
1876:         assert not kwargs
1877: 
1878:         def handle_ntuple(value):
1879:             if value.has_unpack_var_sequence(tx):
1880:                 return variables.TupleVariable(
1881:                     list(value.unpack_var_sequence(tx)),
1882:                 )
1883:             elif value.is_python_constant():
1884:                 # constant prop through it
1885:                 return variables.ConstantVariable.create(
1886:                     torch.nn.modules.utils._ntuple(count)(value.as_python_constant()),
1887:                 )
1888:             else:
1889:                 unimplemented(
1890:                     gb_type="Attempted to use `torch.nn.modules.utils._ntuple` with unsupported argument type",
1891:                     context=f"value={value}",
1892:                     explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1893:                     hints=[
1894:                         "Change use of _ntuple with argument as constant or tensor.",
1895:                     ],
1896:                 )
1897: 
1898:         if self.value is torch.nn.modules.utils._ntuple:
1899:             return variables.LambdaVariable(handle_ntuple)
1900:         else:
1901:             return handle_ntuple(args[0])
1902: 
1903:     @classmethod
1904:     def call_nn_parameter(cls, tx, data=None, requires_grad=True):
1905:         """A call to torch.nn.Parameter() gets lifted to before the graph"""
1906:         if tx.export:
1907:             unimplemented(
1908:                 gb_type="Attempted to use `torch.nn.Parameter()` with export",
1909:                 context="",
1910:                 explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1911:                 hints=[
1912:                     "Do not use `torch.nn.Parameter()` with export.",
1913:                     *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1914:                 ],
1915:             )
1916: 
1917:         if isinstance(requires_grad, variables.VariableTracker):
1918:             try:
1919:                 requires_grad = requires_grad.as_python_constant()
1920:             except NotImplementedError:
1921:                 unimplemented(
1922:                     gb_type="non-constant `requires_grad` argument to `torch.nn.Parameter`",
1923:                     context=f"requires_grad={requires_grad}",
1924:                     explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1925:                     hints=[
1926:                         "Change `requires_grad` to be a bool.",
1927:                         *graph_break_hints.USER_ERROR,
1928:                     ],
1929:                 )
1930: 
1931:         if data is None or not data.is_tensor():
1932:             unimplemented(
1933:                 gb_type="`torch.nn.Parameter()` with unsupported data type",
1934:                 context=f"data={data}",
1935:                 explanation="Called `torch.nn.Parameter()` with non-Tensor argument.",
1936:                 hints=[
1937:                     "Ensure the argument to `torch.nn.Parameter()` is a `torch.Tensor`.",
1938:                     *graph_break_hints.USER_ERROR,
1939:                 ],
1940:             )
1941: 
1942:         # this results in cleaner graphs, but only works for inputs
1943:         # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
1944:         if data.source:
1945:             return cls._nn_param_via_prefix_insert(tx, data, requires_grad)
1946: 
1947:         if config.graph_break_on_nn_param_ctor:
1948:             # Need user to manually move since we cannot
1949:             unimplemented(
1950:                 gb_type="Attempted to use `torch.nn.Parameter()` constructor with Dynamo",
1951:                 context="",
1952:                 explanation="Dynamo does not support this",
1953:                 hints=[
1954:                     "Try to construct `torch.nn.Parameter()` outside the compiled region.",
1955:                     "If this is not possible, turn `graph_break_on_nn_param_ctor` off",
1956:                     *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1957:                 ],
1958:             )
1959: 
1960:         # TODO[@lucaskabela]: Remove the behavior below since it is deprecated
1961:         if isinstance(
1962:             data,
1963:             TensorWithTFOverrideVariable,
1964:             # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
1965:         ) or is_traceable_wrapper_subclass_type(data.class_type):
1966:             unimplemented(
1967:                 gb_type="Attempted to use torch.nn.Parameter constructor with tensor subclass",
1968:                 context=str(data),
1969:                 explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1970:                 hints=[
1971:                     *graph_break_hints.SUPPORTABLE,
1972:                 ],
1973:             )
1974: 
1975:         if not can_convert_to_tracable_parameter():
1976:             unimplemented(
1977:                 gb_type="`torch.nn.Parameter`: cannot convert to traceable tracable",
1978:                 context="",
1979:                 explanation="convert_tracable_parameter is set to False.",
1980:                 hints=[
1981:                     "Check usage of context manager: do_not_convert_to_tracable_parameter",
1982:                     *graph_break_hints.DIFFICULT,
1983:                 ],
1984:             )
1985: 
1986:         try:
1987:             # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
1988:             shape = tuple(data.var_getattr(tx, "shape").as_python_constant())
1989:             # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
1990:             dtype = data.var_getattr(tx, "dtype").as_python_constant()
1991:             # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
1992:             device = data.var_getattr(tx, "device").as_python_constant()
1993:         except NotImplementedError as e:
1994:             unimplemented(
1995:                 gb_type="`torch.nn.Parameter` with non-constant Tensor attributes",
1996:                 context=f"data={data}",
1997:                 explanation="Dynamo does not support this.",
1998:                 hints=[
1999:                     "Ensure the Tensor argument's shape, dtype, and device are correct.",
2000:                     *graph_break_hints.USER_ERROR,
2001:                 ],
2002:                 from_exc=e,
2003:             )
2004: 
2005:         placeholder = tx.output.synthetic_graph_input(
2006:             new_parameter_placeholder,
2007:             # pyrefly: ignore [unbound-name]
2008:             [shape, dtype, device, requires_grad],
2009:         )
2010:         # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
2011:         if data.requires_grad:
2012:             # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
2013:             data = data.call_method(tx, "detach", [], {})
2014: 
2015:         from .builder import wrap_fx_proxy
2016: 
2017:         result = wrap_fx_proxy(
2018:             tx,
2019:             tx.output.create_proxy(
2020:                 "call_function",
2021:                 tracable_create_parameter,
2022:                 # pyrefly: ignore [missing-attribute]
2023:                 (data.as_proxy(), placeholder.as_proxy()),
2024:                 {},
2025:             ),
2026:             # In reconstruct() we should use the original parameter. The one
2027:             # returned by the graph will be an alias.
2028:             source=placeholder.source,
2029:         )
2030:         assert result.is_tensor()
2031:         result.class_type = torch.nn.Parameter  # type: ignore[union-attr]
2032: 
2033:         # TODO(jansel/bdhirsh) - There is some issue with
2034:         # tracable_create_parameter. It does not seem to use the right
2035:         # grad_enabled. Since this is parameter, we can just override the
2036:         # has_grad_fn field to False to workaround the issue.
2037:         result.has_grad_fn = False  # type: ignore[union-attr]
2038: 
2039:         # TODO(jansel): if the new param falls out of scope, currently it won't get freed until
2040:         # the end of the graph.  We should fix this.
2041:         return result
2042: 
2043:     @staticmethod
2044:     def _nn_param_via_prefix_insert(tx: "InstructionTranslator", data, requires_grad):
2045:         # Alternate version if we have a .source
2046:         varname = tx.output.new_var()
2047: 
2048:         # construct the nn.Parameter before the graph save it to varname
2049:         assert tx.output.root_tx is not None
2050:         cg = PyCodegen(tx.output.root_tx)
2051:         cg.add_push_null(lambda: cg.load_import_from("torch.nn", "Parameter"))
2052:         cg(data.source)
2053:         cg(variables.ConstantVariable(requires_grad))
2054:         cg.call_function(2, False)
2055:         cg.store(varname)
2056:         tx.output.pregraph_bytecode.extend(cg.get_instructions())
2057: 
2058:         data_node = data.as_proxy().node
2059:         if data_node.op not in ("placeholder", "get_attr"):
2060:             unimplemented(
2061:                 gb_type="Unexpected type of data placeholder op for parameter construction",
2062:                 context=f"data_node.op={data_node.op}",
2063:                 explanation="Data node op should be placeholder or get_attr.",
2064:                 hints=[
2065:                     *graph_break_hints.DIFFICULT,
2066:                 ],
2067:             )
2068: 
2069:         # add the newly constructed nn.Parameter as a graph input
2070:         source = SyntheticLocalSource(varname)
2071:         example_value = torch.nn.Parameter(
2072:             tx.output.example_value_from_input_node(data.as_proxy().node),
2073:             requires_grad=requires_grad,
2074:         )
2075:         result = VariableTracker.build(tx, example_value, source)
2076:         # Realize the VT because we will delete the guards on it in the next line.
2077:         result = result.realize()
2078:         # No need to guard on this since we already guarded on `data`.
2079:         # These guards would fail since varname doesn't exist until after the function starts
2080:         TracingContext.get().guards_context.dynamo_guards.remove_guards_with_source(
2081:             source
2082:         )
2083:         return result
2084:

9: def _pow_scalar(scalar_base, tensor_exponent):
10:     """
11:     Decomposition of pow.Scalar(scalar, tensor) using mathematical identity.
12:     pow.Scalar computes scalar^tensor_exponent for each element
13: 
14:     We use the identity: a^b = exp(b * ln(a))
15:     This way we can use scalar operations without converting scalar to tensor.
16:     """
17:     # Use mathematical identity: scalar^tensor = exp(tensor * log(scalar))
18:     # Compute log(scalar) as a scalar operation
19:     import math
20: 
21:     if scalar_base <= 0:
22:         raise RuntimeError(
23:             "pow with non-positive base is not supported in this decomposition"
24:         )
25: 
26:     log_scalar_value = math.log(scalar_base)
27: 
28:     # Multiply tensor by the scalar log value
29:     mul_result = torch.ops.aten.mul(tensor_exponent, log_scalar_value)
30: 
31:     # Apply exp to get the final result
32:     return torch.ops.aten.exp(mul_result)
33: 
34: 
35: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/23cf24103963adce84b2b4c027053fec0b29ad94/aten/src/ATen/native/RNN.cpp#L743-L751
36: def _thnn_fused_lstm_cell(gi, gh, cx, bi=None, bh=None):
37:     li = gi if bi is None else gi + bi
38:     lh = gh if bh is None else gh + bh
39:     out = li + lh
40: 
41:     i, f, g, o = out.chunk(4, dim=1)
42:     cy = f.sigmoid() * cx + i.sigmoid() * g.tanh()
43:     hy = o.sigmoid() * cy.tanh()
44: 
45:     # Third return value seems to be unused
46:     return hy, cy, torch.ops.aten.empty([0], dtype=hy.dtype, device=hy.device)

49: def _atan2(y, x):
50:     """
51:     Decomposition of atan2(y, x) using only aten ops.
52: 
53:     atan2(y, x) computes the angle of the vector (x, y) in radians.
54:     This implementation handles all quadrants correctly:
55:     - Quadrant I (x > 0, y > 0): atan(y/x)
56:     - Quadrant II (x < 0, y > 0): π + atan(y/x)
57:     - Quadrant III (x < 0, y < 0): -π + atan(y/x)
58:     - Quadrant IV (x > 0, y < 0): atan(y/x)
59:     - Special cases for x=0: π/2 * sign(y)
60:     - Special case for y=0, x<0: π
61:     """
62:     # Create constants
63:     pi = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(3.141592653589793, dtype=x.dtype, device=x.device)
64:     zero = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(0.0, dtype=x.dtype, device=x.device)
65: 
66:     # Handle the case where x != 0
67:     ratio = torch.ops.aten.div(y, x)
68:     base_atan = torch.ops.aten.atan(ratio)
69: 
70:     # Determine which quadrant we're in
71:     x_positive = torch.ops.aten.gt(x, zero)
72:     x_negative = torch.ops.aten.lt(x, zero)
73:     y_positive = torch.ops.aten.gt(y, zero)
74:     y_negative = torch.ops.aten.lt(y, zero)
75:     x_zero = torch.ops.aten.eq(x, zero)
76:     y_zero = torch.ops.aten.eq(y, zero)
77: 
78:     # Quadrant adjustments
79:     # For x < 0, add π if y >= 0, subtract π if y < 0
80:     pi_adjustment = torch.ops.aten.where(
81:         x_negative,
82:         torch.ops.aten.where(
83:             y_negative, torch.ops.aten.neg(pi), pi
84:         ),  # Fixed: use y_negative instead of y_positive
85:         zero,
86:     )
87: 
88:     # Handle x = 0 case: result should be π/2 * sign(y)
89:     half_pi = torch.ops.aten.div(pi, 2.0)
90:     x_zero_result = torch.ops.aten.where(
91:         y_zero,
92:         zero,  # atan2(0, 0) = 0
93:         torch.ops.aten.mul(half_pi, torch.ops.aten.sign(y)),
94:     )
95: 
96:     # Combine results
97:     result = torch.ops.aten.where(
98:         x_zero, x_zero_result, torch.ops.aten.add(base_atan, pi_adjustment)
99:     )
100: 
101:     return result
102:

132: def _div_scalar_mode(input, other, *, rounding_mode=None):
133:     """
134:     Decomposition of div(input, scalar, rounding_mode=mode) using basic aten ops.
135: 
136:     Args:
137:         input: Tensor to divide
138:         other: Scalar divisor
139:         rounding_mode: str, one of None, "trunc", "floor"
140:     """
141:     # Basic division
142:     result = torch.ops.aten.div(input, other)
143: 
144:     if rounding_mode is None:
145:         return result
146:     elif rounding_mode == "floor":
147:         return torch.ops.aten.floor(result).to(dtype=input.dtype)
148:     elif rounding_mode == "trunc":
149:         # trunc(x) = sign(x) * floor(abs(x))
150:         # For division result, this is equivalent to:
151:         # - floor(result) if result >= 0
152:         # - ceil(result) if result < 0
153:         # Since we don't have ceil, we use: ceil(x) = -floor(-x)
154:         zero = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(
155:             0.0, dtype=result.dtype, device=result.device
156:         )
157:         neg_result = torch.ops.aten.neg(result)
158:         neg_floor = torch.ops.aten.floor(neg_result)
159:         ceil_result = torch.ops.aten.neg(neg_floor)
160: 
161:         # Select floor or ceil based on sign
162:         result_positive = torch.ops.aten.ge(result, zero)
163:         return torch.ops.aten.where(
164:             result_positive, torch.ops.aten.floor(result), ceil_result
165:         ).to(dtype=input.dtype)
166:     else:
167:         raise ValueError(f"Unsupported rounding_mode: {rounding_mode}")
168: 
169: 
170: def _div_tensor_mode(input, other, *, rounding_mode=None):
171:     """
172:     Decomposition of div(input, tensor, rounding_mode=mode) using basic aten ops.
173: 
174:     Args:
175:         input: Tensor to divide
176:         other: Tensor divisor
177:         rounding_mode: str, one of None, "trunc", "floor"
178:     """
179:     # Basic division
180:     result = torch.ops.aten.div(input, other)
181: 
182:     if rounding_mode is None:
183:         return result
184:     elif rounding_mode == "floor":
185:         return torch.ops.aten.floor(result).to(dtype=input.dtype)
186:     elif rounding_mode == "trunc":
187:         # trunc(x) = sign(x) * floor(abs(x))
188:         # For division result, this is equivalent to:
189:         # - floor(result) if result >= 0
190:         # - ceil(result) if result < 0
191:         # Since we don't have ceil, we use: ceil(x) = -floor(-x)
192:         zero = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(
193:             0.0, dtype=result.dtype, device=result.device
194:         )
195:         neg_result = torch.ops.aten.neg(result)
196:         neg_floor = torch.ops.aten.floor(neg_result)
197:         ceil_result = torch.ops.aten.neg(neg_floor)
198: 
199:         # Select floor or ceil based on sign
200:         result_positive = torch.ops.aten.ge(result, zero)
201:         return torch.ops.aten.where(
202:             result_positive, torch.ops.aten.floor(result), ceil_result
203:         ).to(dtype=input.dtype)
204:     else:
205:         raise ValueError(f"Unsupported rounding_mode: {rounding_mode}")

208: def _expm1(input):
209:     """
210:     Decomposition of expm1(x) = exp(x) - 1 using basic aten ops.
211:     """
212:     return torch.ops.aten.sub(torch.ops.aten.exp(input), 1.0)

215: def _log10(input):
216:     """
217:     Decomposition of log10(x) = log(x) / log(10) using basic aten ops.
218:     """
219:     log_10 = torch.ops.aten.log(
220:         torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(10.0, dtype=input.dtype, device=input.device)
221:     )
222:     return torch.ops.aten.div(torch.ops.aten.log(input), log_10)
223: 
224: 
225: def _log1p(input):
226:     """
227:     Decomposition of log1p(x) = log(1 + x) using basic aten ops.
228:     """
229:     one = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(1.0, dtype=input.dtype, device=input.device)
230:     return torch.ops.aten.log(torch.ops.aten.add(input, one))

232: 
233: def _remainder_scalar_tensor(scalar, input):
234:     """
235:     Decomposition of remainder(scalar, tensor) using basic aten ops.
236:     remainder(a, b) = a - b * floor(a / b)
237:     """
238:     # Convert scalar to tensor with same dtype and device as input
239:     scalar_tensor = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(
240:         scalar, dtype=input.dtype, device=input.device
241:     )
242: 
243:     # Calculate a / b
244:     div_result = torch.ops.aten.div(scalar_tensor, input)
245: 
246:     # Calculate floor(a / b)
247:     floor_result = torch.ops.aten.floor(div_result)
248: 
249:     # Calculate b * floor(a / b)
250:     mul_result = torch.ops.aten.mul(input, floor_result)
251: 
252:     # Calculate a - b * floor(a / b)
253:     return torch.ops.aten.sub(scalar_tensor, mul_result)
254: 
255: 
256: def _trunc(input):
257:     """
258:     Decomposition of trunc(x) using basic aten ops.
259:     trunc(x) = sign(x) * floor(abs(x))
260:     """
261:     # Get absolute value
262:     abs_input = torch.ops.aten.abs(input)
263: 
264:     # Floor of absolute value
265:     floor_abs = torch.ops.aten.floor(abs_input)
266: 
267:     # Get sign
268:     sign_input = torch.ops.aten.sign(input)
269: 
270:     # Multiply sign by floor(abs(x))
271:     return torch.ops.aten.mul(sign_input, floor_abs)
272: 
273: 
274: def _native_dropout(input, p, train=None):
275:     """
276:     Decomposition of native_dropout using basic aten ops.
277:     Uses normal_ instead of rand_like for random number generation.
278:     """
279:     if train is False:
280:         # In evaluation mode, return input unchanged with all-ones mask
281:         mask = torch.ops.aten.ones_like(input, dtype=torch.bool)
282:         return input, mask
283: 
284:     if p == 0.0:
285:         # No dropout, return input with all-ones mask
286:         mask = torch.ops.aten.ones_like(input, dtype=torch.bool)
287:         return input, mask
288: 
289:     if p == 1.0:
290:         # Complete dropout, return zeros with all-zeros mask
291:         mask = torch.ops.aten.zeros_like(input, dtype=torch.bool)
292:         output = torch.ops.aten.zeros_like(input)
293:         return output, mask
294: 
295:     # Generate random tensor using normal_ and transform to uniform [0,1]
296:     # Create a tensor filled with random values from normal distribution
297:     random_tensor = torch.ops.aten.empty_like(input)
298:     random_tensor.normal_(mean=0.0, std=1.0)
299: 
300:     # Transform normal distribution to uniform [0,1] using sigmoid-like transformation
301:     # Use tanh to map (-inf, inf) to (-1, 1), then scale to [0, 1]
302:     tanh_result = torch.ops.aten.tanh(random_tensor)
303:     add_result = torch.ops.aten.add(tanh_result, 1.0)
304:     uniform_tensor = torch.ops.aten.div(add_result, 2.0)
305: 
306:     # Create dropout mask (True where we keep values)
307:     mask = torch.ops.aten.gt(uniform_tensor, p)
308: 
309:     # Apply mask and scale by 1/(1-p)
310:     scale_factor = 1.0 / (1.0 - p)
311:     scaled_input = torch.ops.aten.mul(input, scale_factor)
312:     zeros_like_input = torch.ops.aten.zeros_like(input)
313:     output = torch.ops.aten.where(mask, scaled_input, zeros_like_input)
314: 
315:     return output, mask
316: 
317: 
318: def _round_decimals(input, decimals=0):
319:     """
320:     Decomposition of round(input, decimals) using basic aten ops.
321:     round(x, n) = round(x * 10^n) / 10^n
322:     """
323:     if decimals == 0:
324:         return torch.ops.aten.round(input)
325: 
326:     # Calculate 10^decimals
327:     ten = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(10.0, dtype=input.dtype, device=input.device)
328:     scale_factor = torch.ops.aten.pow(ten, decimals)
329: 
330:     # Scale up, round, then scale down
331:     scaled = torch.ops.aten.mul(input, scale_factor)
332:     rounded = torch.ops.aten.round(scaled)
333:     result = torch.ops.aten.div(rounded, scale_factor)
334: 
335:     return result
336: 
337: 
338: def _detach_copy(input):
339:     """
340:     Decomposition of detach_copy using basic aten ops.
341:     detach_copy creates a copy of the tensor that doesn't require gradients.
342:     """
343:     # Use clone and detach to create a copy without gradients
344:     return torch.ops.aten.clone(input).detach()
345: 
346: 
347: def _flip_default(input, dims):
348:     """
349:     Decomposition of flip.default using basic aten operations.
350:     Note: This is a simplified implementation. In practice, prims.rev would be used.
351:     """
352:     # For each dimension in dims, reverse the tensor along that dimension
353:     result = input
354:     for dim in dims:
355:         # Create slice indices to reverse the dimension
356:         indices = torch.ops.aten.arange(
357:             result.size(dim) - 1, -1, -1, device=result.device
358:         )
359:         result = torch.ops.aten.index_select(result, dim, indices)
360:     return result
361: 
362: 
363: def _embedding_bag(
364:     weight,
365:     indices,
366:     offsets,
367:     scale_grad_by_freq=False,
368:     mode=0,
369:     sparse=False,
370:     per_sample_weights=None,
371:     include_last_offset=False,
372:     padding_idx=None,
373: ):
374:     """
375:     Decomposition of embedding_bag using basic aten operations.
376:     This is a simplified implementation for common cases.
377:     """
378:     # Simple implementation for mode=0 (sum)
379:     if mode != 0:
380:         raise NotImplementedError(
381:             "Only sum mode (mode=0) is supported in this decomposition"
382:         )
383: 
384:     # Get embeddings for all indices
385:     # Handle padding_idx parameter properly
386:     if padding_idx is not None:
387:         embeddings = torch.ops.aten.embedding(
388:             weight, indices, padding_idx, scale_grad_by_freq, sparse
389:         )
390:     else:
391:         embeddings = torch.ops.aten.embedding(
392:             weight, indices, -1, scale_grad_by_freq, sparse
393:         )
394: 
395:     # Handle per_sample_weights if provided
396:     if per_sample_weights is not None:
397:         embeddings = embeddings * per_sample_weights.unsqueeze(-1)
398: 
399:     # Sum embeddings according to offsets
400:     bag_size = offsets.size(0)
401:     if include_last_offset:
402:         bag_size -= 1
403: 
404:     output = torch.ops.aten.zeros(
405:         [bag_size, weight.size(1)], dtype=weight.dtype, device=weight.device
406:     )
407:     offset2bag = torch.ops.aten.zeros_like(indices)
408: 
409:     for bag_idx in range(bag_size):
410:         start_idx = offsets[bag_idx]
411:         end_idx = (
412:             offsets[bag_idx + 1] if bag_idx + 1 < offsets.size(0) else indices.size(0)
413:         )
414:         if include_last_offset and bag_idx == bag_size - 1:
415:             end_idx = offsets[bag_idx + 1]
416: 
417:         bag_embeddings = embeddings[start_idx:end_idx]
418:         output[bag_idx] = torch.ops.aten.sum(bag_embeddings, dim=0)
419:         offset2bag[start_idx:end_idx] = bag_idx
420: 
421:     # Return output, offset2bag, bag_size, max_indices (simplified)
422:     max_indices = torch.ops.aten.zeros(
423:         [bag_size], dtype=torch.long, device=weight.device
424:     )
425:     bag_size_tensor = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(
426:         bag_size, dtype=torch.long, device=weight.device
427:     )
428: 
429:     return output, offset2bag, bag_size_tensor, max_indices
430:

432: def _var_mean_default(input, unbiased=True):
433:     return _var_mean_dim(input, unbiased=unbiased)
434: 
435: 
436: def _var_mean_dim(input, dim=None, unbiased=True, keepdim=False):
437:     correction = 1 if unbiased else 0
438:     return torch.ops.aten.var_mean.correction(
439:         input, dim=dim, correction=correction, keepdim=keepdim
440:     )
441: 
442: 
443: def _var_default(input, unbiased=True):
444:     return _var_dim(input, unbiased=unbiased)
445: 
446: 
447: def _var_dim(input, dim=None, unbiased=True, keepdim=False):
448:     correction = 1 if unbiased else 0
449:     return _var_correction(input, dim, correction=correction, keepdim=keepdim)
450: 
451: 
452: def _var_correction(input, dim=None, *, correction=None, keepdim=False):
453:     """
454:     Decomposition of var.correction using var_mean.correction.
455:     Computes the variance with specified correction factor.
456:     """
457:     # var_mean.correction returns (variance, mean)
458:     # We only need the variance (first return value)
459:     var_result, _ = torch.ops.aten.var_mean.correction(
460:         input, dim=dim, correction=correction, keepdim=keepdim
461:     )
462:     return var_result

464: 
465: # From torch source code (however, not exposed as a decomposition rule).
466: #
467: # Not really sure how to put this into the main library.  PrimTorch wants
468: # empty_permuted to go to the prim, and typically users don't really want
469: # to decompose to empty_strided (but inductor is OK with it, because we are
470: # cool with strides and everything goes to empty_strided)
471: # @register_decomposition([aten.empty_permuted.default])
472: def empty_permuted(
473:     size,
474:     physical_layout,
475:     **kwargs,
476: ) -> torch.Tensor:
477:     perm = [0] * len(size)
478:     for p, l in enumerate(physical_layout):
479:         perm[l] = p
480:     return torch.empty([size[l] for l in physical_layout], **kwargs).permute(perm)
481: 
482: 
483: def _to_dtype(self: torch.Tensor, dtype: torch.dtype, **kwargs):
484:     if self.dtype == dtype:
485:         return self
486:     return torch.ops.aten._to_copy.default(self, dtype=dtype, **kwargs)
487: 
488: 
489: def _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
490:     if x.dtype == torch.bool and y.dtype == torch.bool:
491:         x = torch.ops.aten.to.dtype(x, torch.int32)
492:         y = torch.ops.aten.to.dtype(y, torch.int32)
493:     return x, y
494: 
495: 
496: def _lt_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
497:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
498:     return (x - y) < 0
499: 
500: 
501: def _gt_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
502:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
503:     return (x - y) > 0
504: 
505: 
506: def _le_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
507:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
508:     return (x - y) <= 0
509: 
510: 
511: def _ge_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
512:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
513:     return (x - y) >= 0
514: 
515: 
516: def _eq_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
517:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
518:     return (x - y) == 0
519: 
520: 
521: def _ne_tensor(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor):
522:     x, y = _prepare_compare_tensor_inputs(x, y)
523:     return (x - y) != 0

526: def _reshape(self: torch.Tensor, size: list[int]):
527:     try:
528:         return self.view(*size)
529:     except:
530:         return self.contiguous().view(*size)

532: 
533: def _narrow_default(self: torch.Tensor, dim: int, start: int, length: int):
534:     return torch.ops.aten.slice.Tensor(self, dim, start, start + length, 1)
535: 
536: 
537: def _normalize_dim(dim: int, ndim: int):
538:     return dim if dim >= 0 else dim + ndim
539: 
540: 
541: def _maybe_wrap_dim(dim: int, ndim: int):
542:     effective_ndim = 1 if ndim == 0 else ndim
543:     orig_dim = dim
544:     dim = dim + effective_ndim if dim < 0 else dim
545:     if dim < 0 or dim >= effective_ndim:
546:         raise IndexError(
547:             f"Dimension out of range (expected to be in range of "
548:             f"[{-effective_ndim}, {effective_ndim - 1}], but got {orig_dim})"
549:         )
550:     return dim
551: 
552: 
553: def _slice_chunks(self: torch.Tensor, dim: int, boundaries: list[int]):
554:     dim = _normalize_dim(dim, self.ndim)
555:     starts = [0, *boundaries]
556:     ends = [*boundaries, self.size(dim)]
557:     return [
558:         torch.ops.aten.slice.Tensor(self, dim, start, end, 1)
559:         for start, end in zip(starts, ends)
560:     ]
561: 
562: 
563: def _tensor_split_sections(self: torch.Tensor, sections: int, dim: int = 0):
564:     dim = _normalize_dim(dim, self.ndim)
565:     dim_size = self.size(dim)
566:     min_split_size, num_splits_one_extra = divmod(dim_size, sections)
567:     split_sizes = [min_split_size + 1] * num_splits_one_extra + [min_split_size] * (
568:         sections - num_splits_one_extra
569:     )
570: 
571:     boundaries = []
572:     offset = 0
573:     for split_size in split_sizes[:-1]:
574:         offset += split_size
575:         boundaries.append(offset)
576: 
577:     return _slice_chunks(self, dim, boundaries)
578: 
579: 
580: def _tensor_split_indices(self: torch.Tensor, indices: list[int], dim: int = 0):
581:     return _slice_chunks(self, dim, list(indices))
582: 
583: 
584: def _tensor_split_tensor_indices_or_sections(
585:     self: torch.Tensor, tensor_indices_or_sections: torch.Tensor, dim: int = 0
586: ):
587:     if tensor_indices_or_sections.numel() == 1:
588:         return _tensor_split_sections(self, int(tensor_indices_or_sections.item()), dim)
589:     return _tensor_split_indices(self, tensor_indices_or_sections.tolist(), dim)
590:

592: def _hsplit_dim(self: torch.Tensor):
593:     return 0 if self.ndim == 1 else 1

595: 
596: def _hsplit_int(self: torch.Tensor, sections: int):
597:     return _tensor_split_sections(self, sections, _hsplit_dim(self))

601:     return _tensor_split_indices(self, indices, _hsplit_dim(self))

631: 
632: def _flatten_using_ints(self: torch.Tensor, start_dim: int = 0, end_dim: int = -1):
633:     ndim = self.ndim
634:     if ndim == 0:
635:         _maybe_wrap_dim(start_dim, ndim)
636:         _maybe_wrap_dim(end_dim, ndim)
637:         return _view_default(self, [1])
638: 
639:     start_dim = _normalize_dim(start_dim, ndim)
640:     end_dim = _normalize_dim(end_dim, ndim)
641: 
642:     if start_dim > end_dim:
643:         return _view_default(self, self.shape)
644: 
645:     flattened_size = 1
646:     for size in self.shape[start_dim : end_dim + 1]:
647:         flattened_size *= size
648: 
649:     new_shape = [
650:         *self.shape[:start_dim],
651:         flattened_size,
652:         *self.shape[end_dim + 1 :],
653:     ]
654:     return _view_default(self, new_shape)
655: 
656: 
657: def _unflatten_int(self: torch.Tensor, dim: int, sizes: list[int]):
658:     dim = _normalize_dim(dim, self.ndim)
659:     new_shape = [*self.shape[:dim], *sizes, *self.shape[dim + 1 :]]
660:     return _view_default(self, new_shape)
661: 
662: 
663: def _atleast_1d_default(self: torch.Tensor):
664:     if self.ndim == 0:
665:         return _view_default(self, [1])
666:     return _view_default(self, self.shape)

699: def _moveaxis_permutation(ndim: int, source, destination):
700:     source = _normalize_dims(source, ndim)
701:     destination = _normalize_dims(destination, ndim)
702:     order = [dim for dim in range(ndim) if dim not in source]
703:     for dst, src in sorted(zip(destination, source)):
704:         order.insert(dst, src)
705:     return order

711:     )

832: 
833: 
834: _AxisSyms = tuple[str, ...]
835: _Permutation = tuple[int, ...]
836: 
837: 
838: def _einsum(expr: str, tensors: list[torch.Tensor]) -> torch.Tensor:
839:     from collections import defaultdict
840:     from collections.abc import Sequence
841: 
842:     def is_trivial_permutation(perm: _Permutation) -> bool:
843:         return perm == tuple(range(len(perm)))
844: 
845:     def sort_permutation(l: Sequence) -> tuple[tuple, _Permutation]:
846:         x = sorted(map(lambda pair: (pair[1], pair[0]), enumerate(l)))
847:         # Unzip sorted list
848:         sorted_list, perm = zip(*x)
849:         return sorted_list, perm
850: 
851:     def extract_axis_syms(term: str, tensor: torch.Tensor) -> _AxisSyms:
852:         if "..." in term:
853:             dims_omitted = tensor.ndim - (len(term) - 3)
854:             term = term.replace("...", "." * dims_omitted)
855:             if len(term) != tensor.ndim:
856:                 raise ValueError("Syntax error in einsum expression")
857:         elif len(term) != tensor.ndim:
858:             raise ValueError(
859:                 "The number of einsum subscripts doesn't match tensor dimension"
860:             )
861:         return tuple(term)
862: 
863:     # Extract input and output expressions
864:     match expr.split("->"):
865:         case expr_input, expr_output:
866:             pass
867:         case (expr_input,):
868:             expr_output = None
869:         case _:
870:             raise ValueError("Syntax error in einsum expression")
871: 
872:     # Process input terms
873:     expr_input_split = expr_input.split(",")
874:     assert len(expr_input_split) == len(tensors)
875: 
876:     sorted_input_axes: list[_AxisSyms] = []
877:     input_perms: list[_Permutation] = []
878:     subscript_occurrences: defaultdict[str, int] = defaultdict(lambda: 0)
879:     num_ellipsis_axes = 0
880: 
881:     for expr, t in zip(expr_input_split, tensors):
882:         axis_symbols = extract_axis_syms(expr, t)
883:         dims_omitted = axis_symbols.count(".")
884:         for sub in axis_symbols:
885:             subscript_occurrences[sub] += 1
886:         num_ellipsis_axes = max(num_ellipsis_axes, dims_omitted)
887: 
888:         sorted_axes, perm = sort_permutation(axis_symbols)
889:         sorted_input_axes.append(sorted_axes)
890:         input_perms.append(perm)
891: 
892:     # Process output term
893:     if expr_output is None:
894:         # Build implicit output term from unique subscripts
895:         unique_subscripts = sorted(
896:             x for x, cnt in subscript_occurrences.items() if cnt == 1
897:         )
898:         output_axis_symbols = tuple(["."] * num_ellipsis_axes + unique_subscripts)
899:     else:
900:         if "..." in expr_output:
901:             expr_output = expr_output.replace("...", "." * num_ellipsis_axes)
902:         output_axis_symbols = tuple(expr_output)
903: 
904:     output_rev_perms = sort_permutation(output_axis_symbols)[1]
905:     output_perms = sort_permutation(output_rev_perms)[1]
906: 
907:     # Compute what axes need to be reduced
908:     output_subscripts = set(output_axis_symbols)
909:     full_subscripts = ["."] * num_ellipsis_axes + sorted(subscript_occurrences.keys())
910:     reduce_dims = tuple(
911:         idx for idx, sub in enumerate(full_subscripts) if sub not in output_subscripts
912:     )
913: 
914:     # Actual tensor operation starts here
915:     # Pointwise multiplication w/ broadcast
916:     mul = None
917:     for t, axes, perm in zip(tensors, sorted_input_axes, input_perms):
918:         # Permute to match the order of subscripts
919:         if not is_trivial_permutation(perm):
920:             t = t.permute(*perm)
921: 
922:         # Insert unit axes to align axes among tensors
923:         input_axis_idx = 0
924:         num_input_axes = len(axes)
925:         dims_omitted = axes.count(".")
926:         full_subs_base = num_ellipsis_axes - dims_omitted
927:         for idx, subscript in enumerate(full_subscripts[full_subs_base:]):
928:             if input_axis_idx < num_input_axes and subscript == axes[input_axis_idx]:
929:                 input_axis_idx += 1
930:                 continue
931:             t = t.unsqueeze(idx)
932: 
933:         if mul is None:
934:             mul = t
935:         else:
936:             mul = mul * t
937: 
938:     # Reduce by addition
939:     assert mul is not None
940:     reduced = torch.sum(mul, reduce_dims, keepdim=False) if reduce_dims else mul
941: 
942:     # Permute to match expected output shape if needed
943:     if is_trivial_permutation(output_perms):
944:         return reduced
945:     else:
946:         return reduced.permute(*output_perms).contiguous()
947: 
948: 
949: def _where_default(condition):
950:     # Decomp of aten.where.default (unary) via nonzero + unbind.
951:     if condition.ndim == 0:
952:         condition = torch.ops.aten.unsqueeze(condition, 0)
953:     indices = torch.ops.aten.nonzero(condition)
954:     return torch.ops.aten.unbind.int(indices, 1)
955: 
956: 
957: def _where_scalar(condition, self, other):
958:     # Decomp of aten.where.Scalar: promote both scalars via result_type.
959:     dtype = torch.result_type(self, other)
960:     self_t = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(self, dtype=dtype, device=condition.device)
961:     other_t = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(other, dtype=dtype, device=condition.device)
962:     return torch.ops.aten.where(condition, self_t, other_t)
963: 
964: 
965: def _where_scalar_self(condition, self, other):
966:     # Decomp of aten.where.ScalarSelf: promote scalar self + tensor other via
967:     # result_type (a one-way cast to the tensor dtype would truncate the scalar).
968:     dtype = torch.result_type(other, self)
969:     self_t = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(self, dtype=dtype, device=other.device)
970:     if other.dtype != dtype:
971:         other = torch.ops.aten._to_copy.default(other, dtype=dtype)
972:     return torch.ops.aten.where(condition, self_t, other)
973: 
974: 
975: def _where_scalar_other(condition, self, other):
976:     # Decomp of aten.where.ScalarOther: promote tensor self + scalar other via
977:     # result_type (a one-way cast to the tensor dtype would truncate the scalar).
978:     dtype = torch.result_type(self, other)
979:     other_t = torch.ops.aten.scalar_tensor(other, dtype=dtype, device=self.device)
980:     if self.dtype != dtype:
981:         self = torch.ops.aten._to_copy.default(self, dtype=dtype)
982:     return torch.ops.aten.where(condition, self, other_t)
983: 
984: 
985: def _concat_default(tensors, dim=0):
986:     # Reroute aten.concat -> aten.cat to avoid the pure-decomp self-loop.
987:     return torch.ops.aten.cat.default(tensors, dim)
988: 
989: 
990: def _softmax_int(self, dim, dtype=None):
991:     # Decomp of aten.softmax.int (cast + _softmax) to break the CIA self-loop.
992:     # half_to_float=False: the True branch asserts fp16 and clashes with the cast.
993:     if dtype is not None and dtype != self.dtype:
994:         self = torch.ops.aten._to_copy.default(self, dtype=dtype)
995:     return torch.ops.aten._softmax.default(self, dim, False)
996:

998: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Repeat.cpp#L91-L117
999: def _repeat_interleave_self_int(self, repeats, dim=None, *, output_size=None):
1000:     # Avoids re-emitting aten.repeat_interleave.self_int (CIA self-loop);
1001:     # output_size is validation-only upstream and intentionally unused here.
1002:     if dim is None:
1003:         input = torch.ops.aten.flatten.using_ints(self, 0, -1)
1004:         dim_w = 0
1005:     else:
1006:         input = self
1007:         dim_w = _maybe_wrap_dim(dim, self.dim())
1008:     input = torch.ops.aten.unsqueeze.default(input, dim_w + 1)
1009:     expand_shape = list(input.shape)
1010:     expand_shape[dim_w + 1] = repeats
1011:     input = torch.ops.aten.expand.default(input, expand_shape)
1012:     input = torch.ops.aten.clone.default(input, memory_format=torch.contiguous_format)
1013:     return torch.ops.aten.flatten.using_ints(input, dim_w, dim_w + 1)
1014:

1016: # Port of the ``is_bias_likely_fusable`` lambda inside
1017: # https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Linear.cpp#L110-L118
1018: def _linear_bias_likely_fusable(bias):
1019:     return (
1020:         bias.dim() == 1 or torch.ops.aten.squeeze.default(bias).dim() == 1
1021:     ) and bias.is_contiguous()

1024: def _linear_flatten_3d_enabled():
1025:     # Matches upstream c10::utils::check_env("TORCH_LINEAR_FLATTEN_3D") == true,
1026:     # which treats only the exact string "1" as enabled.
1027:     return os.environ.get("TORCH_LINEAR_FLATTEN_3D") == "1"

1030: def _linear_contiguous(input):
1031:     return torch.ops.aten.clone.default(
1032:         input,
1033:         memory_format=torch.contiguous_format,
1034:     )

1037: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Linear.cpp#L53-L83
1038: def _flatten_nd_linear(input, weight, bias, weight_t):
1039:     input_sizes = list(input.shape)
1040:     flattened_dim = 1
1041:     for size in input_sizes[:-1]:
1042:         flattened_dim *= size
1043: 
1044:     input_flat = torch.ops.aten.view.default(input, [flattened_dim, input_sizes[-1]])
1045:     if weight.layout == torch.strided:
1046:         result = torch.ops.aten.addmm.default(bias, input_flat, weight_t)
1047:     else:
1048:         bias_t = (
1049:             torch.ops.aten.transpose.int(bias, -2, -1)
1050:             if bias.dim() >= 2
1051:             else torch.ops.aten.unsqueeze.default(bias, -1)
1052:         )
1053:         result = torch.ops.aten.t.default(
1054:             torch.ops.aten.addmm.default(
1055:                 bias_t, weight, torch.ops.aten.t.default(input_flat)
1056:             )
1057:         )
1058: 
1059:     output_sizes = input_sizes
1060:     output_sizes[-1] = result.shape[1]
1061:     return torch.ops.aten.view.default(result, output_sizes)
1062: 
1063: 
1064: def _linear_cast_rank(dtype):
1065:     if dtype.is_complex:
1066:         return 3
1067:     if dtype.is_floating_point:
1068:         return 2
1069:     if dtype == torch.bool:
1070:         return 0
1071:     return 1
1072: 
1073: 
1074: def _linear_can_cast_to(cast_from, cast_to):
1075:     return _linear_cast_rank(cast_from) <= _linear_cast_rank(cast_to)
1076: 
1077: 
1078: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Linear.cpp#L85-L143
1079: def _linear_default(input, weight, bias=None):
1080:     # Avoids re-emitting aten.linear.default (registered via CIA, self-loops).
1081:     weight_t = torch.ops.aten.t.default(weight)
1082:     if input.dim() == 2 and bias is not None:
1083:         return torch.ops.aten.addmm.default(bias, input, weight_t)
1084:     if bias is not None and _linear_bias_likely_fusable(bias):
1085:         if input.is_contiguous():
1086:             return _flatten_nd_linear(input, weight, bias, weight_t)
1087:         if _linear_flatten_3d_enabled():
1088:             return _flatten_nd_linear(_linear_contiguous(input), weight, bias, weight_t)
1089:     output = torch.ops.aten.matmul.default(input, weight_t)
1090:     if bias is not None:
1091:         output_dtype = output.dtype
1092:         bias = torch.ops.aten.expand.default(bias, list(output.shape))
1093:         output = torch.ops.aten.add.Tensor(output, bias)
1094:         if output.dtype != output_dtype:
1095:             if not _linear_can_cast_to(output.dtype, output_dtype):
1096:                 raise RuntimeError(
1097:                     f"result type {output.dtype} can't be cast to the desired "
1098:                     f"output type {output_dtype}"
1099:                 )
1100:             output = torch.ops.aten._to_copy.default(output, dtype=output_dtype)
1101:     return output
1102: 
1103: 
1104: def _broadcast_tensors_default(tensors):
1105:     # Decomp of aten.broadcast_tensors.default via per-input expand. Expand is
1106:     # unconditional so output getitem names aren't rebound onto input placeholders.
1107:     common = list(torch.broadcast_shapes(*[t.shape for t in tensors]))
1108:     return [torch.ops.aten.expand.default(t, common) for t in tensors]
1109: 
1110: 
1111: def _meshgrid_indexing(tensors, indexing):
1112:     # Decomp of aten.meshgrid.indexing via view + expand (stays in aten, unlike
1113:     # _refs.meshgrid which emits prims::broadcast_in_dim).
1114:     n = len(tensors)
1115:     if n == 0:
1116:         return []
1117:     normalized = []
1118:     for t in tensors:
1119:         if t.dim() == 0:
1120:             t = torch.ops.aten.view.default(t, [-1])
1121:         normalized.append(t)
1122: 
1123:     swap = indexing == "xy" and n >= 2
1124:     if swap:
1125:         normalized[0], normalized[1] = normalized[1], normalized[0]
1126: 
1127:     sizes = [t.shape[0] for t in normalized]
1128:     grids = []
1129:     for i, t in enumerate(normalized):
1130:         view_shape = [1] * n
1131:         view_shape[i] = -1
1132:         g = torch.ops.aten.view.default(t, view_shape)
1133:         g = torch.ops.aten.expand.default(g, sizes)
1134:         grids.append(g)
1135: 
1136:     if swap:
1137:         grids[0], grids[1] = grids[1], grids[0]
1138:     return grids
1139: 
1140: 
1141: def _prelu_default(self, weight):
1142:     # Decomp of aten.prelu.default via _prelu_kernel + view (stays in aten, unlike
1143:     # _refs.prelu which emits prims::broadcast_in_dim).
1144:     weight_num = weight.numel()
1145:     if weight_num != 1:
1146:         view_shape = [1] * self.ndim
1147:         if self.ndim > 1:
1148:             view_shape[1] = weight_num
1149:         weight = torch.ops.aten.view.default(weight, view_shape)
1150:     return torch.ops.aten._prelu_kernel.default(self, weight)

1153: # Port of the static helper inside
1154: # https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Convolution.cpp#L826-L843
1155: def _conv_batchify(input, num_spatial_dims, name):
1156:     dim_count_no_batch = num_spatial_dims + 1
1157:     dim_count_batch = dim_count_no_batch + 1
1158:     is_batched = input.dim() == dim_count_batch
1159:     if not is_batched and input.dim() != dim_count_no_batch:
1160:         raise RuntimeError(
1161:             f"Expected {dim_count_no_batch}D (unbatched) or "
1162:             f"{dim_count_batch}D (batched) input to {name}, "
1163:             f"but got input of size: {tuple(input.shape)}"
1164:         )
1165:     if not is_batched:
1166:         input = torch.ops.aten.unsqueeze.default(input, 0)
1167:     return input, is_batched
1168: 
1169: 
1170: def _conv_nd_default(
1171:     input, weight, bias, stride, padding, dilation, groups, num_spatial_dims, name
1172: ):
1173:     # Shared body for conv{1,2,3}d.default. ``complex_convolution`` branch in
1174:     # upstream is intentionally not ported.
1175:     input_b, is_batched = _conv_batchify(input, num_spatial_dims, name)
1176:     output_padding = [0] * num_spatial_dims
1177:     output = torch.ops.aten.convolution.default(
1178:         input_b,
1179:         weight,
1180:         bias,
1181:         list(stride),
1182:         list(padding),
1183:         list(dilation),
1184:         False,
1185:         output_padding,
1186:         groups,
1187:     )
1188:     return output if is_batched else torch.ops.aten.squeeze.dim(output, 0)

1233: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/MaxPooling.cpp#L65-L96
1234: # combined with https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Pooling.cpp#L77-L102
1235: def _max_pool1d_default(
1236:     self, kernel_size, stride=(), padding=(0,), dilation=(1,), ceil_mode=False
1237: ):
1238:     # Avoids re-emitting aten.max_pool1d.default (CIA self-loop); the CIA
1239:     # max_pool1d_with_indices body is inlined to reach max_pool2d_with_indices.
1240:     if not stride:
1241:         stride = kernel_size
1242:     self_2d = torch.ops.aten.unsqueeze.default(self, -2)
1243:     output_2d, _ = torch.ops.aten.max_pool2d_with_indices.default(
1244:         self_2d,
1245:         [1, kernel_size[0]],
1246:         [1, stride[0]],
1247:         [0, padding[0]],
1248:         [1, dilation[0]],
1249:         ceil_mode,
1250:     )
1251:     return torch.ops.aten.squeeze.dim(output_2d, -2)

1254: # Port of https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/blob/v2.10.0/aten/src/ATen/native/Pooling.cpp#L104-L127

1260:     dilation=(1, 1),
1261:     ceil_mode=False,
1262: ):
1263:     # Avoids re-emitting aten.max_pool2d.default (CIA self-loop); quantized /
1264:     # MKLDNN branches upstream are intentionally not ported.
1265:     if not stride:
1266:         stride = kernel_size
1267:     output, _ = torch.ops.aten.max_pool2d_with_indices.default(
1268:         self, kernel_size, stride, padding, dilation, ceil_mode
1269:     )

1291: # ===== Normalization =====
1292: def _layer_norm_default(
1293:     input, normalized_shape, weight=None, bias=None, eps=1e-05, cudnn_enable=True
1294: ):
1295:     return torch.ops.aten.native_layer_norm.default(
1296:         input, normalized_shape, weight, bias, eps
1297:     )[0]
